- **chunk-0076.md:8** — `src/internal/api/chatbot/chat.go:20-27`, `src/internal/api/chatbot/dtos.go:64-67`, `src/internal/api/comfort/handler.go:114-117`, `src/internal/api/comfort/handler.go:157-160`: These chatbot and comfort write handlers decode request bodies with `json.Decoder.Decode` and no size limit, strict unknown-field rejection, or EOF/trailing-JSON check. Oversized or malformed/ambiguous payloads are not rejected consistently, and misspelled fields can silently become zero/default values. `src/internal/api/chartannotation/handler.go:361-375` uses a 16 KB body cap, and `src/internal/api/apibulk/helpers.go` in the catalog pattern uses strict shared decoding; unbounded/lenient parsing is inconsistent and enables transport resource use plus silent configuration changes.
- **chunk-0076.md:9** — `src/internal/api/chatbot/chat.go:270-301`: The `"online"`, `"awake"`, `"status"`, or `"vehicle state"` chatbot branch reads current vehicle state directly from the `vehicles.state` snapshot column. This exposes stale snapshot state instead of the layered live-state value used by other chatbot and climate surfaces. `src/internal/api/chatbot/handler.go` is already routed through live/StateReader for location and battery in this package, and the catalog contract reserves snapshot reads for historical/legacy migrations, not current state.
- **chunk-0076.md:10** — `src/internal/api/chartannotation/handler.go:251-281`: `Update` accepts `vehicle_id` in `chartAnnotationWriteRequest`, but never writes it to `ChartAnnotationUpdate`; the request field is silently ignored while the handler reports `200` and reloads the unchanged row. This prevents reassignment/scoping changes even though the write shape exposes the field and Create validates it. Create validates and persists `VehicleID` at `handler.go:129-174`; Update has no corresponding validation or patch member, creating contradictory POST/PATCH behavior.
- **chunk-0076.md:11** — `src/internal/api/chartannotation/handler.go:137-139`, `src/internal/api/chartannotation/handler.go:249-253`: Chart-annotation Create and Update use `json.Unmarshal` directly rather than strict shared decoding, so unknown JSON fields are silently ignored and typos in required or patch fields can be mistaken for valid requests. Create uses a separate required-field validation pass, but a misspelled `title`/`occurred_at` becomes a missing-field error while a misspelled optional field can silently do nothing on Update. The handler already has a body cap; strict unknown-field and trailing-value enforcement is the remaining transport guard needed to make the protected write contract reliable.
- **chunk-0077.md:5** — `src/internal/api/comfort/handler.go:112-117`, `src/internal/api/comfort/handler.go:155-160`, `src/internal/api/command/handler.go:159-165`: The comfort config/precondition and vehicle-command mutation handlers decode request bodies with a single `json.Decoder.Decode` and no size limit, strict unknown-field mode, or EOF/trailing-value check. A body such as valid JSON followed by a second object is accepted, and misspelled fields are ignored before config changes or an external Tesla command is considered. Neighboring protected APIs in this snapshot use bounded reads, `DisallowUnknownFields`, and EOF checks (for example `src/internal/api/datarepair/cases.go:110-129` and `close.go:96-119`), but this chunk's mutation paths still use the permissive shape. Unknown/concatenated payloads can therefore reach side-effecting config or vehicle-command code without satisfying the repository transport contract.
- **chunk-0077.md:6** — `src/internal/api/datarepair/handler.go:280-306`, `src/internal/api/datarepair/handler.go:360-388`, `src/internal/api/datarepair/handler.go:444-447`, `src/internal/api/datarepair/handler.go:563-566`, `src/internal/api/datarepair/handler.go:686-703`, `src/internal/api/datarepair/preview.go:221-226,293-298`: Data-repair charging/drive mutations, previews, and legacy deletes parse path IDs with `URLParamInt64` but do not reject zero or negative values; `UpdateCharging` and `UpdateDrive` then decode unbounded bodies with one `json.Decoder.Decode` and no EOF or unknown-field protection. Negative IDs can reach repository lookups and mutating paths, while valid-first-object-plus-trailing JSON can be silently ignored and the allowed patch applied to a real session. Canonical case, quarantine, and close paths in the same package reject `id <= 0` and use bounded strict single-object decoders (`src/internal/api/datarepair/cases.go:149-152`, `quarantine.go:94-97`, `close.go:96-119`). The inconsistent branches allow bad identifiers and malformed body ambiguity in high-impact audit/reversible-delete operations.
- **chunk-0077.md:7** — `src/internal/api/costforecast/handler.go:229-240`: `GetForecast` parses `vehicle_id` with `strconv.ParseInt` but does not require a positive ID. Zero and negative values are passed into the cost-history query and forecast model as valid scope. The cost forecast's neighboring vehicle-scoped handlers and dashboard/package paths reject non-positive IDs before querying (`src/internal/api/comfort/handler.go:201-205`, `src/internal/api/dashboardlayout/handler.go:72-77`); a negative vehicle ID is invalid input and should not be converted into an apparently valid empty/zero-data forecast response.
- **chunk-0077.md:8** — `src/internal/api/dashboardlayout/handler.go:99-108,165-173`: Dashboard-layout create/update use bounded body reads but then `json.Unmarshal` without `DisallowUnknownFields`. Unknown keys such as a misspelled `layout` or `is_default` are silently ignored, leaving required-field validation or a partial update to proceed with zero/defaulted intent instead of rejecting a typo. The same dashboard feature already validates logical fields and requires a JSON object for layout, but its transport stage remains permissive; adjacent data-repair and strict API decoders reject unknown fields at decode time and also verify one-object-only input.
- **chunk-0078.md:5** — `src/internal/api/datarepair/handler.go:302-306`, `src/internal/api/datarepair/handler.go:384-388`: The charging and drive partial-repair mutations decode `map[string]interface{}` with a single unbounded `json.Decoder.Decode`, without rejecting unknown fields at decode time, enforcing a request-size limit, or checking for EOF/trailing JSON. Valid JSON followed by another object/value is accepted because only the first value is consumed, and oversized patch bodies are read until decode finds data. The existing repair close/case decoders in the same feature use `MaxBytesReader`, unknown-field rejection, and EOF checks, so this is inconsistent transport validation. Both mutation methods feed the patch directly through allowed-field validation after decoding; a trailing payload is not visible to that validation and can therefore be ignored while the first patch is applied. Unknown keys are caught by the patch allowlist, but the leniency and body-size gaps create audit-mutating behavior from malformed or oversized requests and duplicate the request-safety class already fixed by `src/internal/api/datarepair/close.go:93-129` and `src/internal/api/datarepair/cases.go:110-129`.
- **chunk-0078.md:6** — `src/internal/api/datarepair/handler.go:280-281,362-363,446-447,565-566`, `src/internal/api/datarepair/preview.go:223-226,295-298`, `src/internal/api/datarepair/handler.go:686-687,702-703`: Multiple data-repair URL-ID handlers parse IDs with `URLParamInt64` but do not require a positive value. Negative IDs reach `GetByID`, repair patch, close, preview, and legacy quarantine/delete repository paths rather than being rejected as bad input. The neighboring suggestion and case APIs reject IDs with `id <= 0` in `src/internal/api/datarepair/suggestions_handler.go:79-82` and `src/internal/api/datarepair/cases.go:150-152` (and quarantine parsing in `src/internal/api/datarepair/quarantine.go:95-97`); accepting negative IDs is inconsistent transport validation and can trigger pointless lookups or reach cross-session mutation code instead of returning 400.
- **chunk-0079.md:5** — `src/internal/api/drivedyn/handler.go:48-51,94-97`: Both Drive Dynamics endpoints parse `vehicle_id` but reject only zero (`== 0`), so negative IDs pass validation and reach the live/timeline readers. The package's bad-request tests also omit negative IDs even though the endpoint has a numeric database-style vehicle identity contract. Neighboring canonical handlers require positive IDs, including `src/internal/api/devtools/handler.go:1033-1036,1265-1268` and `src/internal/api/drivediagnostic/handler.go:82-85`; negative IDs should be rejected consistently before any reader or database work.
- **chunk-0079.md:6** — `src/internal/api/devtools/handler.go:129,470,595,688,896`: Five DevTools mutation/proxy endpoints decode JSON with a single unrestricted `json.Decoder.Decode` call. None rejects unknown fields, applies a body-size cap, or verifies EOF, so oversized bodies, trailing second values, and misspelled fields can reach Fleet API or database operations instead of being rejected at the HTTP boundary. The codebase's established request pattern uses `http.MaxBytesReader`, `DisallowUnknownFields`, and an EOF/trailing-value check in files such as `src/internal/api/adminfeedback/handler.go:181-187`, `src/internal/api/apibulk/helpers.go:67-76`, and `src/internal/api/exports/bulk.go:119-126`; this package currently bypasses that contract across five wire endpoints.
- **chunk-0079.md:7** — `src/internal/api/diagnostic/handler.go:323-347`: The signal-log freshness check treats a future `MAX(ts)` as fresh because `time.Since(future)` is negative and therefore passes the `<= 5m` branch. It can report telemetry as healthy even though the newest stored observation is after the current clock. Clock skew, bad ingest timestamps, or a parser/backend timestamp defect can be mistaken for live telemetry; the adjacent threshold logic only distinguishes age bands and has no future-clock branch, producing an unjustified `ok` result.
- **chunk-0080.md:3** — `src/internal/api/journey/handler.go:73`, `src/internal/api/journey/handler.go:256`, `src/internal/api/journey/handler.go:327`, `src/internal/api/journey/live.go:174`, `src/internal/api/journey/replan.go:207`: These journey POST handlers use unrestricted, lenient `json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode`: they neither cap the body, reject unknown fields, nor verify that the first JSON value is the only JSON value. Typos can be ignored, and a valid prefix followed by trailing garbage can be accepted. The package already validates decoded fields later, but transport validation is incomplete. The same pattern appears at five journey write boundaries; large or malformed bodies can cause excess allocation or silently accepted request state. Canonical request handling in this catalog uses `DecodeAndValidate`/strict decoding instead.
- **chunk-0080.md:4** — `src/internal/api/journey/live.go:174-241`: `AppendCheckpoint` decodes explicit `lat` and `lng` values and live-telemetry backfill values but never validates coordinate ranges. It only checks `soc_pct` and `odometer_m` and requires that both coordinates are non-nil. Values such as latitude `99` or longitude `-999` are persisted and corrupt trail/progress calculations. `src/internal/api/journey/handler.go:85-95` has a `validCoord` range check for session coordinates, but the checkpoint path does not use it for explicit or backfilled positions. Invalid fixes make the trail and route calculations accept impossible locations instead of rejecting the request.
- **chunk-0080.md:5** — `src/internal/api/journey/session.go:27-35`, `src/internal/api/journey/session.go:48-54`: `Transition` validates entries from `allowedTransitions` only when the statuses differ; the initial `if from == to { return nil }` bypasses status validation entirely. Unknown or empty statuses that are equal, such as `Transition("bogus", "bogus")`, are accepted as no-op transitions. `ValidStatus` and the transition matrix already define the authoritative status set and terminal behavior, but `Transition` can be called directly without either check (`src/internal/api/journey/session_test.go:25-62`), allowing malformed persisted values to bypass lifecycle validation.
- **chunk-0081.md:3** — `src/internal/api/exports/handler.go:387-421`: `SubmitAccountJob` uses a one-pass `json.Decoder.Decode` with no max-body reader, strict unknown-field policy, or trailing-JSON check. It also parses start/end independently and never validates that the range is increasing, while the generic export submission validates the same date window through `apiparams.ValidateDateRange`. An account-export request can silently ignore unknown/trailing content, oversized JSON, and submit reversed/invalid bounds, producing a misleading job that may query no data or be rejected inconsistently downstream. The neighboring submit path at `src/internal/api/exports/handler.go:54-65` uses `MaxBytesReader`, `DisallowUnknownFields`, and a second decode for EOF; the bulk export decoder at `src/internal/api/exports/bulk.go:116-125` also strictly disallows unknown fields. Account export bypasses those established guards and date semantics.
- **chunk-0081.md:4** — `src/internal/api/exports/bulk.go:116-125`: `decodeExportBulkBody` uses `DisallowUnknownFields` but neither caps the request body nor verifies EOF/trailing JSON. The ID count cap does not bound payload byte size when IDs are ordinary strings, and a valid first object followed by a second JSON value is accepted because no second decoder check is performed. The generic export submit decoder immediately above at `src/internal/api/exports/handler.go:54-65` applies both a 64-KiB `MaxBytesReader` and an EOF/trailing-value check. Lenient trailing data can bypass request/body assumptions, and an unbounded body can force avoidable allocation/CPU work before the 200-ID cap matters.
- **chunk-0081.md:5** — `src/internal/api/feedback/handler.go:96-104`: Feedback submission caps bytes and rejects unknown fields, but it does not check for EOF after decoding or reject a second JSON value. Thus a body containing a valid feedback object followed by arbitrary trailing JSON is accepted as one submission. The canonical export submission decoder in `src/internal/api/exports/handler.go:54-65` explicitly performs a second decode and rejects everything except `io.EOF`. For a mutation/write endpoint, trailing JSON should not be silently discarded; it can hide malformed client framing or appended replay content.
- **chunk-0081.md:6** — `src/internal/api/energy/handler.go:54`, `src/internal/api/energy/handler.go:104-111`, `src/internal/api/energyflow/handler.go:40-43`: Route/query vehicle IDs are parsed but `Stats`, `AnalyticsStats`, and energy-flow `Get` accept zero or negative IDs and pass them into services/state readers. These endpoints represent concrete vehicle resources; invalid IDs should fail at the HTTP boundary rather than trigger repository or live-state lookups for `0`/negative keys. The shared ID parser at `src/internal/api/apiparams/params.go:52-57` only parses signed integers and the nearby charging/variance handlers perform positive-ID checks in their validation paths. Negative or zero IDs can become cross-edge cache/query keys or miss unexpectedly instead of returning a consistent 400.
- **chunk-0082.md:5** — `src/internal/api/feedback/handler.go:97-103`, `src/internal/api/feedback/handler_test.go:290-302`: `Submit` performs strict unknown-field decoding but stops after the first JSON value and never checks for EOF. A body containing a valid feedback object followed by another JSON value or garbage is accepted and persisted; only the first object is returned. Unknown fields are rejected by the existing test, but trailing-data variants are not covered. `src/internal/api/fleetops/handler.go:110-124` is the adjacent canonical body decoder for this API tree and explicitly rejects trailing JSON by decoding a second empty value and requiring `io.EOF`; `src/internal/api/adminfeedback/handler.go:186` and `src/internal/api/weberrors/handler.go:139` also use strict decode paths. Accepting concatenated payloads lets clients append conflicting objects without the handler detecting that the wire body violates the single-payload contract.
- **chunk-0083.md:5** — `src/internal/api/gasprice/handler.go:115`, `src/internal/api/gasprice/handler.go:139`: The gas-price toggle and config POST/PUT handlers decode JSON with a single permissive `json.Decoder.Decode` call and never reject unknown fields or trailing JSON. More seriously, `Toggle` has no presence check for its required `enabled` boolean: an empty object or object with only an unknown field decodes to the zero value `false`, causing the poller to stop and a false state to be persisted without any client request for that state. Valid JSON followed by a second object or garbage is also accepted. The same package's tests cover malformed and empty bodies, but not missing required fields, unknown keys, or trailing content; neighboring canonical handlers use `DisallowUnknownFields` plus strict one-object EOF validation, so transport typos cannot silently mutate runtime and restart state.
- **chunk-0084.md:5** — `src/internal/api/geocode/handler.go:68-76`: The reverse-geocoding handler parses coordinates only as floats and never validates that latitude is in `[-90,90]` or longitude is in `[-180,180]`. `strconv.ParseFloat` also accepts non-geographic strings such as `NaN` and `+Inf`, and the handler forwards these values to the provider rather than returning HTTP 400. The endpoint is therefore able to issue malformed upstream requests and its “valid coordinate” contract is different from the geofence resolver. `src/internal/database/geofence/repo_discovery.go:194-196` exports `ValidCoordinate` and already rejects out-of-range coordinates for geofence name resolution; the two HTTP coordinate boundaries must agree. Current geocode coverage checks only parse failures and never numeric bounds, so malformed provider calls can reach external adapters unnoticed.
- **chunk-0084.md:6** — `src/internal/api/geofence/handler.go:90-94`, `src/internal/api/geofence/handler.go:231-235,292-296`, `src/internal/api/geofence/rate_handler.go:337-351`, `src/internal/api/apibulk/helpers.go:67-76,87-95`: Geofence create/update and rate creation decode one JSON value with a permissive `json.Decoder`, while the bulk decoder also uses `DisallowUnknownFields` but no EOF/trailing-value check and has no body cap. In this chunk, unknown typos, second JSON objects, or unbounded bodies can therefore be accepted and mutate or validate state based on the first object. Canonical strict guard decoding in `src/internal/api/guard/handler.go:155-165` uses a capped body, rejects unknown fields, and checks EOF; neighboring bulk handlers advertise strict JSON contracts but the helper does not enforce an EOF boundary. Lenient transport parsing can silently apply partial geofence/rate/bulk updates or hide misspelled fields behind HTTP success.
- **chunk-0084.md:7** — `src/internal/api/health.go:336-342`: `ExtendedHealthCheck` copies the raw database health error into the public health JSON with `"error": err.Error()`. PostgreSQL connection strings, host names, user names, schema details, or driver-specific state may be disclosed in an unauthenticated status response. The handler deliberately exposes system status and already includes database pool/latency metadata; unlike the nearby error responses, it must not publish raw backend error text while retaining a generic `"unhealthy"` status plus server-side logging.
- **chunk-0084.md:8** — `src/internal/api/guard/handler.go:147-165`, `src/internal/api/guard/handler.go:546-550`: Two guard paths leak transport and upstream details differently: oversized SetConfig bodies are capped by `MaxBytesReader` but every decode error is mapped to HTTP 400, and panic results serialize raw `err.Error()` strings from the Tesla command client into the response. A proxy/Tesla error can therefore disclose vendor or implementation text to the caller, while oversized clients receive an inaccurate non-body-size status instead of a size rejection. Neighboring guarded APIs distinguish bounded-size and strict JSON failures; canonical clients should receive stable redacted API errors and HTTP 413/400 mappings respectively. The panic response is public vehicle-control output and should use a stable per-command failure code/message instead of untrusted upstream text.
- **chunk-0085.md:5** — `src/internal/api/importer/handler.go:126-130,214-233`, `src/internal/database/drive/repo.go:117-128`, `src/internal/database/charging/repo.go:49-56`: CSV import computes SI-canonical drive and charging values, then passes them to repository `Create` methods that persist only a subset: drive imports drop distance, duration, max speed, and end timestamp; charging imports drop total energy, end timestamp, end SOC, and peak power. Each import reports the computed row as successfully imported while the database row contains none of those imported values. The importer unit-conversion tests pass through fake creators and therefore do not exercise the real repository SQL. The concrete repositories insert `drives` with only vehicle/start data and `charging_sessions` with only vehicle/start/SOC data, so this is behaviorally different from the handler's CSV contract and can silently produce incomplete historical records.
- **chunk-0085.md:6** — `src/internal/api/journey/handler.go:73,256,327`, `src/internal/api/journey/live.go:174`, `src/internal/api/journey/replan.go:207`: Five journey POST handlers decode JSON with a single `Decode` call and do not enforce EOF, a maximum body size, or strict unknown-field rejection. A body containing a valid object followed by another object, array, or trailing junk is accepted as the first object and proceeds to session, plan, live-checkpoint, scoring, or replan logic. These five paths implement the same lenient JSON pattern within the chunk; the catalog and neighboring guarded API code require strict transport boundaries for writes. Cross-package consumers can send malformed/trailing JSON and receive successful or validation-dependent business responses instead of HTTP 400.
- **chunk-0085.md:7** — `src/internal/api/impersonate/handler.go:368-376`: The impersonation start-body decoder uses `dec.More()` after the first successful `Decode` to detect trailing input, but `Decoder.More()` is an array/object-context check and is not an EOF check outside a container. A valid `{"subject":"target"}{"subject":"other"}` body can pass this test and begin impersonation using the first object. The decoder comment claims the function rejects trailing junk, while its implementation does not consume or validate the stream's EOF. Unknown fields are also rejected by `DisallowUnknownFields`, but concatenated JSON still bypasses the intended strict single-object contract.
- **chunk-0085.md:8** — `src/internal/api/inboundwebhook/handler.go:92-99`: The inbound webhook decoder does not enforce a single JSON value: after decoding one object it returns success for a body with valid first object plus a second object or trailing content. The size cap and invalid-JSON handling protect one part of transport parsing, but EOF/trailing-token validation is absent. Unknown top-level fields are intentionally treated leniently for integrations and should remain accepted; however, concatenated/mixed JSON is not one webhook payload and can be accepted by an unauthenticated integration endpoint instead of HTTP 400, inconsistent with strict single-envelope parsing.
- **chunk-0086.md:5** — `src/internal/api/journey/handler.go:73`, `src/internal/api/journey/handler.go:256`, `src/internal/api/journey/handler.go:327`, `src/internal/api/journey/live.go:174`, `src/internal/api/journey/replan.go:207`: Five POST handlers in this chunk decode one JSON object without rejecting trailing JSON or extra top-level values. A valid object followed by another object or scalar is accepted as long as the first decode succeeds; the later bytes never affect the decoded request and never produce a 400. This repeats the same lenient transport boundary in create/save-plan/score/checkpoint/replan, while neighboring strict handlers use EOF/`DisallowUnknownFields` validation. It can persist a plan or append a checkpoint for an ambiguous/malformed body and makes API clients believe the entire payload was accepted.
- **chunk-0086.md:6** — `src/internal/api/journey/live.go:174-210`: `Append` does not validate checkpoint latitude/longitude before backfill or persistence. The body may supply `lat=99`/`lng=-999`, and backfilled live values are also inserted unchecked; the database rejects them at the repository boundary, but the handler currently converts that repository error into HTTP 500 rather than a transport-level 400. Session creation validates coordinate pairs in `src/internal/api/journey/handler.go:89-106`, and score/replan candidate validation rejects `lat` outside `[-90,90]` and `lng` outside `[-180,180]` in `src/internal/api/journey/handler.go:345`, `src/internal/api/journey/replan.go:90-93`. Checkpoint coordinates should use the same domain bounds; otherwise malformed client data becomes a server error.
- **chunk-0086.md:7** — `src/internal/api/journey/handler.go:102-107`: `validCoord` accepts a one-sided coordinate pair: a latitude with no longitude (or longitude with no latitude) passes because each nil check is independent and missing members are not rejected. Session creation can therefore persist incomplete route coordinates, but `ComputeProgress`, `AssessDeviation`, and route-distance logic treat only fully paired coordinates as usable. The API offers lat/lng as a pair and migration columns are independently nullable; `validCoord` should require either both nil or both present. Partial state is not honest route input and silently degrades live/arrival/report calculations to unknown rather than returning a 400.
- **chunk-0086.md:8** — `src/internal/api/journey/checklist.go:95-119`: `stormItem` does not handle future storm-event timestamps. If an event is generated with a clock-skewed/future `CreatedAt`, `now.Sub(ev.CreatedAt)` is negative, so the detail is rendered as `-5m ago` or `-2h ago` instead of a recent/future timestamp. Severe or warning future events also bypass no current warning semantically because the function sees only the negative age. `ago` in the same file formats any negative `time.Duration` without clamping or validating; negative elapsed time is displayed literally as a negative age. Event timestamps should be rejected as stale/future or normalized before severity detail and age rendering.
- **chunk-0087.md:3** — `src/internal/api/journey/handler.go:73-76,256-259,327-330`, `src/internal/api/journey/live.go:174-177`, `src/internal/api/journey/replan.go:207-210`: The journey mutation handlers decode JSON with a single `Decode` call and do not reject trailing JSON, unknown fields, or a body containing a valid object plus extra data. A body such as `{"...valid..."} {}` is accepted by Create, SavePlan, ScoreStops, checkpoint Append, or Replan and proceeds to domain validation or database writes. The same transport-decoder pattern appears across five journey entry points in this chunk; neighboring strict handlers use EOF/unknown-field validation, so malformed transport content can be treated as valid in every journey write path.
- **chunk-0087.md:4** — `src/internal/api/locsnap/handler.go:64-67,100-103`, `src/internal/api/media/handler.go:64-67,108-111`: Location-snapshot and media handlers accept negative vehicle IDs: they reject parse errors and exactly zero, but do not enforce `vehicleID > 0`. Therefore both List and Latest may query or forward `vehicle_id=-7` instead of returning HTTP 400. The duplicate positive-ID checks are split across both packages, while `lifetime/handler.go:505-509` and `maintenance/handler.go:142-145` reject `<= 0`; this endpoint family otherwise validates scoped IDs inconsistently.
- **chunk-0087.md:5** — `src/internal/api/maintenance/handler.go:67-125,157`, `src/internal/api/maintenance/forecast.go:32-39,79-113,140-161`: The maintenance implementation maintains two independent service catalogs with divergent names and units/interval semantics. `defaultItems` and `forecast` define overlapping services separately; `defaultItems` emits SI-metre odometer/interval fields under legacy `current_mileage`/`interval_miles` names (for example 10,000), while `ProjectForecast` interprets the same maintenance intervals in kilometres (10,000 km). The standard schedule and forecast can therefore disagree on the due distance and which service is due. The frontend also has no shared maintenance catalog and treats the legacy-looking fields as SI in `useMaintenance.ts:8` and `maintenanceModel.ts:60-64`; future interval changes can alter only one endpoint and silently produce inconsistent UI results.
- **chunk-0088.md:5** — `src/internal/api/motor/handler.go:182-184`, `src/internal/api/motor/handler.go:260-262`: Both motor history and latest-state handlers reject only `vehicleID == 0`; negative IDs pass validation and reach `Timeline` / `LiveState`. Positive-ID handlers throughout this API (for example `src/internal/api/mileage/handler.go:117-120`, `src/internal/api/charging/handler.go:133-136`) reject `<= 0`. Negative motor lookups are invalid input and can produce undefined state reads instead of HTTP 400.
- **chunk-0088.md:6** — `src/internal/api/mileage/handler.go:446-448`: `monthsAgo` subtracts calendar months from the caller's current day before snapping to month one. Go normalizes a month-end date such as March 31 minus one month into March 3 (and not February 28/29), then snapping yields March 1. A `months=1` request made at a month end therefore starts one month too late instead of the first day of the prior month. The helper's own test at `src/internal/api/mileage/handler_test.go:610-642` covers a March 31 case but subtracts 12 months, so it can normalize to March and the first-of-month snap masks the bug; nearby monthly windows should snap from an explicitly first-of-month date before subtracting.
- **chunk-0089.md:5** — `src/internal/api/notification/schedule.go:70-73,85-88,102-105,144-147`, `src/internal/api/notification/handler.go:106-109,146-149,262-265,276-279,300-303`, `src/internal/api/notification/channel.go:109-112`: Notification schedule and channel handlers parse path IDs with `strconv.ParseInt`/`URLParamInt64` but do not reject negative IDs. Negative channel, schedule, and webhook test IDs can reach repository lookups and mutation methods instead of being rejected as invalid transport values. `src/internal/api/apiparams/URLParamInt64` documents only parse/error handling and does not enforce positivity, while canonical positive-ID handlers in the neighboring API packages reject values `<= 0`; negative identifiers should not be treated as valid database keys.
- **chunk-0089.md:6** — `src/internal/api/notification/schedule.go:49-51,111-114`, `src/internal/api/notification/handler.go:173-175,285-287,646-648,669-671`, `src/internal/api/notification/channel.go:121-123,227-231`: Notification request decoders use a single `json.Decoder.Decode` without checking EOF, and schedule/channel-create decoders also have no body-size limit or strict unknown-field handling. A valid first JSON object followed by another object/value or trailing malformed content is accepted, and oversized schedule/channel payloads are not bounded. Several neighboring strict handlers and shared bulk helpers in `src/internal/api/apibulk/helpers.go:67-76` reject trailing JSON and unknown fields and/or bound bodies; accepting a prefix can silently persist schedule/channel/inbox state from a malformed request and makes payload size less predictable.
- **chunk-0089.md:7** — `src/internal/api/notification/channel.go:216-232`: `WebhookSignaturePreview` documents that both `secret` and `body` are required, but validation checks only that the secret is nonblank. An omitted `body` field decodes as the zero-length string and still returns HTTP 200 with a signature for HMAC(secret, empty). The handler comment and request type expose a two-field contract, but there is no required-field check for `body`; callers can mistake a missing preview payload for a valid empty-body signature, contrary to the endpoint's documented input contract.
- **chunk-0089.md:8** — `src/internal/api/notification/schedule.go:53-58`: Schedule creation validates only `ChannelID == 0`, not negative or oversized/invalid schedule fields; coupled with the unbounded decoder above, negative channel IDs and malformed-but-decodable schedule values can pass handler validation and reach the repository. Most entity create handlers use positive identifier/range validation before persistence; a negative foreign key or zero/negative schedule timing is less likely to be useful than to produce a misleading 500 or false draft persistence result.
- **chunk-0090.md:5** — `src/internal/api/periodstats/handler.go:181-188`: `GET /analytics/period-stats` parses `vehicle_id` with `strconv.ParseInt` and checks only that it is present and syntactically valid. Negative values such as `vehicle_id=-7` pass validation and reach both period aggregate SQL queries. The positive-ID contract is enforced nearby in packages such as `src/internal/api/quiethours/handler.go:130-137` and other API handlers; negative IDs should be rejected at the period-stats boundary before touching the database.
- **chunk-0090.md:6** — `src/internal/api/pinned/handler.go:122`, `src/internal/api/push/handler.go:138,208`: The pinned and push write handlers decode with `json.Unmarshal` into narrow DTOs but do not reject unknown fields. Misspelled fields silently become missing values (for pins, a typo leaves `item_id` empty and is then rejected; optional pin context and push fields can disappear more subtly), and the same permissive pattern is repeated across both package mutations. The neighboring strict pattern in `src/internal/api/quiethours/handler.go:100-105,119-124` uses `DisallowUnknownFields`, so typoed transport keys are rejected consistently instead of being interpreted as an empty/defaulted request.
- **chunk-0090.md:7** — `src/internal/api/quiethours/handler.go:100-105,119-124`: Quiet-hours Create and Patch enable `DisallowUnknownFields` and a body-size cap, but call only one `Decode` and never verify EOF. A body containing one valid object followed by another JSON value is accepted, and only the first object is applied to the store. Repeated strict-body implementations elsewhere validate a single JSON document before mutation (EOF plus unknown-field checks); without the EOF check, trailing values bypass the same transport contract while still triggering a create/update write.
- **chunk-0090.md:8** — `src/internal/api/openapi/handler.go:23-30`: `SetOpenAPISpec` stores a pointer to the caller's slice header instead of copying the underlying YAML bytes, even though its API comment promises caller-mutation isolation. Reusing a buffer after calling `SetOpenAPISpec`, or writing to that original byte slice, changes content already returned by concurrent handlers without any new setter call. The package comments and `src/internal/api/openapi/handler_test.go:155-170` claim caller-reuse isolation, but that test mutates/reuses neither the original buffer's contents nor asserts its body against subsequent mutation; the atomic-pointer test therefore does not cover the actual aliasing defect.
- **chunk-0091.md:5** — `src/internal/api/quiethours/handler.go:109-116,137-144`: Both quiet-hours Create and Patch decode a strict, size-capped JSON object but never require EOF after the first value. A body such as a valid object followed by a second object or trailing garbage is accepted and persisted for Create/only the first object is used for Patch. The package decoder elsewhere and neighboring handlers use a second decoder pass (`Decode(&struct{}{})`/`Decode(new(any))` expecting `io.EOF`) to enforce one-document bodies; without it, both mutations bypass the same transport contract already advertised by `DisallowUnknownFields` and the body cap.
- **chunk-0091.md:6** — `src/internal/api/rangeproj/handler.go:94-103`, `src/internal/api/rangeproj/handler.go:523-526`: `RangeProjectionHandler.Get` parses `vehicle_id` but does not reject zero or negative IDs, and `GetByVehicle` accepts any `URLParamInt64` result without a positive-ID check. Non-positive IDs reach capacity/drive SQL and StateReader calls in both entry points even though vehicle IDs are positive database keys. `src/internal/api/routeeff/handler.go:93-95,212-214` and `src/internal/api/regen/handler.go:124-128` reject non-positive IDs at the neighboring analytics boundaries; range projection should enforce the same boundary invariant before DB/state work.
- **chunk-0091.md:7** — `src/internal/api/rangeproj/handler.go:240-249,596-605`: Both range-projection entry points read `EnergyRemaining` with a direct `val.(float64)` assertion instead of the package-wide `signal.Float64` coercion. Standard encoded signal values such as `float32`, `int32`, and numeric JSON/string transport values are valid according to `src/internal/signal/coerce.go`, but are silently treated as missing here, producing an incorrect health/degradation result of 100% default values. The same file's BatteryLevel, RatedRange, IdealRange, and EstBatteryRange reads already use `signal.Float64`, so these two health reads differ in semantics despite consuming the same `SignalReader` value contract.
- **chunk-0091.md:8** — `src/internal/api/rangeproj/handler.go:380-393`, `src/internal/api/rangeproj/handler.go:418-424`, `src/internal/api/rangeproj/handler.go:272-282`: The efficiency matrix has inconsistent units within one response contract: SQL `distance_m / 1609.344` is miles, the formula returns a per-mile Wh value, but `efficiencyBucket.WhKm` and scenario calculations treat it as Wh/km, while the fallback matrix uses `defaultEfficiency` values described as Wh/km; then `calcRange` divides usable capacity by that value. A measured matrix bucket can therefore combine mismatch-scaled energy and a km route result, producing incorrect projected ranges, and SQL-produced values do not share the fallback's unit meaning. `rangeproj` response fields named `efficiency_wh_km`, `efficiency_matrix.wh_km`, and `range_km` imply SI km-specific values, but the code comments and denominator explicitly preserve mile-based legacy numerics (`distance_m / metersPerMile`); the copy-dependent behavior is a direct contract/analytics-unit defect.
- **chunk-0091.md:9** — `src/internal/api/rbac/handler.go:279-289`: `decodeRBACUpsertBody` calls `dec.More()` after a successful first decode instead of decoding a second value and requiring `io.EOF`. After an object is followed by another JSON object, `More()` reports on current array context rather than trailing-document context, so trailing junk and valid extra documents can pass and the first matrix update is written. The package and neighboring strict decoders in `src/internal/api/automation/decode.go:100-104`, `src/internal/api/alerts/alerts.go:293-297`, and `src/internal/api/exports/handler.go:59-64` use a second decode/EOF check, so RBAC's advertised strict/single-document contract is not actually enforced.
- **chunk-0092.md:5** — `src/internal/api/routeeff/handler.go:16-18,131-140,249,253-254`: Route-efficiency SQL still converts canonical SI back into legacy units: summary efficiency is computed from SOC loss divided by `distance_m / 1609.344`, so it is emitted as percent-per-mile rather than SI Wh/km; average speed is divided by `0.44704` and emitted as mph, while the detail row exposes `distance_m / 1609.344` as a generic `distance` field. The nearby frontend unit model treats route-efficiency values as SI `Wh/km` and converts only at display time (`src/web/src/features/driving/components/route-efficiency/helpers.ts:9-45`), but this backend currently invents mile-dependent values instead of the canonical contract. The same legacy mile factors are repeated by route and speed analytics (`src/internal/api/speedprofile/handler.go:268-282`, `src/internal/api/tempimpact/repo.go`, `src/internal/api/regen/repo.go`), and the routeeff detail SQL is internally inconsistent: summary `avg_speed` is mph while detail `avg_speed_mps` remains m/s. The result is either mislabeled SI/wire data or different units for the same metric depending on endpoint.
- **chunk-0092.md:6** — `src/internal/api/safety/handler.go:48-50,83-85`: Both safety endpoints accept negative `vehicle_id` values: parsing succeeds and the code rejects only `vehicleID == 0`. `vehicle_id=-42` therefore reaches `StateReader.Timeline` or `LiveStateReader.LiveState`, while the routeeff and RUL packages reject every non-positive ID before touching state/data. Canonical positive-ID validation is already used for the same transport concept in `src/internal/api/routeeff/handler.go:93-95` and `src/internal/api/rul/handler.go:343-345`; zero/negative IDs should be rejected in both safety handlers rather than treated as valid database/signal keys.
- **chunk-0092.md:7** — `src/internal/api/savedviews/handler.go:158-166,219-227`: Saved-view create and update use `json.Unmarshal` without unknown-field rejection, so misspelled keys such as `sort_ordre` are silently ignored. An unknown-field-only update becomes a successful no-op repository update/audit event instead of an invalid request, while create requests with a typo for a non-required field can silently omit defaults. The strict mutation pattern is repeated in neighboring handlers: `src/internal/api/pinned/handler.go:122,192`, `src/internal/api/push/handler.go:138,208`, and `src/internal/api/quiethours/handler.go` use strict decoding where typoed transport keys must not silently alter state. Saved views also already have a size-bounded body, so adding strict/single-document decoding is the remaining consistency gap.
- **chunk-0093.md:5** — `src/internal/api/scheduledexports/scheduled_exports_handler.go:115-132`: `decodeBody` enables `DisallowUnknownFields` and a 32 KiB body cap, but it performs only the first `Decode` and never checks for EOF. A valid JSON object followed by a second value (or trailing malformed JSON) is accepted and processed as a create/update even though the request contains more than one JSON document. The package's neighboring strict decoder pattern in `src/internal/api/serviceintelligence/admin_handler.go:77-87` explicitly performs the second decode-to-EOF; accepting a prefix can persist an export schedule from a body with trailing data.
- **chunk-0093.md:6** — `src/internal/api/scheduledexports/scheduled_exports_handler.go:63-76`, `src/internal/database/export/scheduled_repo.go:280-319`: Scheduled-export payloads accept a pointer `vehicle_id`, but neither the handler nor canonical `NormalizeScheduledExportInput` rejects negative IDs. A payload such as `{"vehicle_id":-7,...}` passes the repository's normalizers, reaches a foreign-key/write path, and can turn invalid input into a misleading 500-style repository failure instead of HTTP 400. The repository has a single canonical normalizer for create/update, but it validates name/type/format/cron/delivery/window/columns while omitting `VehicleID`; positive-ID boundary validation elsewhere is split from persistence and should be applied before repository calls.
- **chunk-0093.md:7** — `src/internal/api/security/handler.go:83-86,122-125`: Both security List and Latest parse `vehicle_id` but reject only zero and parse errors. Negative IDs such as `vehicle_id=-7` pass the handler boundary and are sent into `StateReader.Timeline` and `LiveStateReader.LiveState`. The same security domain has no positive-ID contract in these entries, while `src/internal/api/serviceintelligence/handler.go:51-54` and `src/internal/api/segments/handler.go:129-132` consistently reject `<= 0`; negative IDs should not reach signal reads.
- **chunk-0093.md:8** — `src/internal/api/search/handler.go:417-456`: Global search performs display conversion of SI canonical `distance_m` into miles in the backend (`distance_m / 1609.344`) and appends literal `" mi"` to the subtitle. This violates the catalog's SI canonical/display boundary: the API is returning a legacy-looking display unit instead of SI plus presentation-specific formatting, so it bypasses shared frontend preference formatting and will not adapt to user units; fixing it requires preserving SI data in the response and applying preference conversion at the frontend.
- **chunk-0093.md:9** — `src/internal/api/segments/segments.go:326-381`, `src/internal/api/segments/handler.go:587-601`: `RankByTime` sorts all attempts by raw duration and can rank a zero-duration attempt first as rank 1 / `IsPR`, because sorting does not exclude non-positive durations. By contrast, `bestDuration` excludes those attempts, `Summarize` prefers positive durations, and `raceResult` explicitly says a non-positive duration cannot win. Zero/negative or missing durations are common malformed/pending telemetry cases; ranking them fastest produces a false leaderboard PR and can disagree with the summary, efficiency ranking, ghost winner, and DTO delta semantics.
- **chunk-0094.md:5** — `src/internal/api/security/handler.go:83-85,121-123`: Both Security `List` and `Latest` accept negative `vehicle_id` values: validation only rejects parse errors and zero, so `vehicle_id=-7` reaches `StateReader.Timeline` and `LiveStateReader.LiveState` instead of the HTTP 400 boundary used for invalid entity IDs. The neighboring segment and saved-view handlers reject IDs with `<= 0`; a negative security vehicle key is not a valid positive vehicle identifier and should not trigger a signal-store read.
- **chunk-0094.md:6** — `src/internal/api/savedviews/handler.go:165-168,226-229`: Saved-view Create and Update use `json.Unmarshal` without rejecting unknown fields. A misspelled optional update field (for example `is_pinne`) decodes to an empty patch and still returns the repository's success response, while a misspelled create field can silently leave required/default values and create a row that does not match the caller's intent. The scheduled-export handler in this chunk uses `DisallowUnknownFields` and this same file's strict validation expectations are transport-contract sensitive; the saved-view DTO can otherwise make typoed mutation keys indistinguishable from omitted fields.
- **chunk-0094.md:7** — `src/internal/api/scheduledexports/scheduled_exports_handler.go:114-131`: `decodeBody` performs one strict JSON decode but never verifies EOF for a second JSON value. A valid scheduled-export object followed by another object (or trailing JSON value) is accepted and the first object alone is persisted or used for update. Neighboring strict decoders and shared bulk helpers in `src/internal/api/apibulk/helpers.go:67-76` reject trailing documents; scheduled exports otherwise enforce unknown-field and size limits but not the complete-document rule.
- **chunk-0094.md:8** — `src/internal/api/scheduledexports/scheduled_exports_handler.go:50-68,276-290`, `src/internal/database/export/scheduled_repo.go:276-320`: The scheduled-export request normalizer validates the export name/type/format/cron/delivery/window and route CRUD rejects non-positive schedule IDs, but it does not validate an optional `vehicle_id` pointer. Negative vehicle IDs survive JSON decoding and repository normalization and can be persisted as FK/export scope values. Positive-ID validation appears at the segment, saved-view, security-validation, and scheduled-export route boundaries; accepting a negative foreign key here is inconsistent and can produce database/export jobs scoped to a nonexistent vehicle instead of an immediate 400.
- **chunk-0095.md:5** — `src/internal/api/settings/handler.go:78`, `src/internal/api/settings/handler.go:250`: The general settings update and API-suspend toggle decode a single JSON object with plain `json.Decoder`, without `DisallowUnknownFields` or an EOF check. Unknown keys such as a misspelled `suspended` field silently produce the zero value, while two concatenated JSON objects leave the first object applied and ignore trailing data. The settings package already uses strict decoding elsewhere (`handler.go:314-316`, `import.go:60-63`); GET/update settings contracts should not silently mutate state from typos or accept a multi-document body.
- **chunk-0095.md:6** — `src/internal/api/settings/import.go:60-63`, `src/internal/api/settingsreset/handler.go:69-71`, `src/internal/api/settings/handler.go:314-316`: These strict or reset-related endpoints do not verify EOF after the first decoded object. The import and reset decoders use `DisallowUnknownFields`, but a payload such as `{"dry_run":true} {"dry_run":false}` (import) or two reset objects is accepted and only the first object governs the mutation; polling-config decoding likewise rejects unknown fields but ignores trailing JSON after the first object. Adjacent handlers consistently treat trailing JSON as a second-document parse error; without the terminal EOF check these settings writes bypass the single-document transport contract.
- **chunk-0095.md:7** — `src/internal/api/share/handler.go:172`, `src/internal/api/share/handler.go:260`: Drive and charging-session share creation use permissive decoders for their optional create bodies. Unknown fields are ignored, and concatenated JSON values are accepted because only the first `Decode` result is examined; malformed trailing input can therefore coexist with an already-created share link. Both handlers repeat the same body pattern even though these mutations expose privacy-sensitive flags, title/description text, and expiry selection; a strict single-document decoder would prevent typoed or extra fields and trailing data from silently changing the effective request.
- **chunk-0096.md:5** — `src/internal/api/speedprofile/handler.go:87-91`, `src/internal/api/speedprofile/handler_test.go:397-406`: `speed-profile` parsing validates only that `vehicle_id` is numeric; zero and negative IDs pass into all four analytics queries. The validation tests cover missing, empty, non-numeric, float, and overflow values but omit `<= 0`, despite the endpoint contract requiring a vehicle ID. The tests themselves explicitly omit non-positive values, so a request such as `vehicle_id=-7` is silently scoped as an impossible database key instead of receiving the package's `BAD_REQUEST` response.
- **chunk-0096.md:6** — `src/internal/api/speedprofile/handler.go:98-101,196-226,264-287`: Optional start/end bounds are read through `ParseDateRange`, which silently drops malformed values; `speed-profile` also never rejects a reversed range. Invalid bounds therefore either remove the range without warning or issue `BETWEEN`/ordered queries with a start after the end, affecting distribution, categories, scatter points, and hero aggregates together. The same package documents shared range bounds, but it performs no ordering/validity check before the four database calls; neighboring bounded handlers use `ParseDateRangeValues` plus `ValidateDateRange` and should return 400 rather than silently broadening or inverting the report.
- **chunk-0096.md:7** — `src/internal/api/speedprofile/handler.go:19,202-205,220-223,268-270,280-283`: The speed-profile SQL emits a field named `battery_pct_per_100km`, but its calculation divides by `distance_m / 1609.344`, then multiplies by 100. That computes battery percentage points consumed per mile, not per 100 km. It also describes the metric as per-100km and exposes that name to typed frontend/API consumers (`src/web/src/api/types.ts` has the same metric name), while the implementation's constant and test fixture preserve the wrong mile-based formula. This creates a unit-contract mismatch rather than a display-only conversion issue.
- **chunk-0096.md:8** — `src/internal/api/signalscatalog/handler.go:225-245`: `GET /signals/observations` validates `since` and `until` individually as RFC3339 but does not verify that `since <= until`. A reversed interval reaches `ObservationsCount` and `Observations`, producing a contradictory two-sided SQL filter and an unnecessary pair of reads. The repository correctly treats both bounds as independent inclusive predicates, but the handler supplies no ordering invariant; neighboring range endpoints reject reversed windows before querying.
- **chunk-0096.md:9** — `src/internal/api/sleep/handler.go:187-205`: `resolveWindow` accepts explicit start/end values and derived malformed ranges through legacy `ParseDateRange` without a validation error. If `start` or `end` is malformed, the caller falls back or silently narrows behavior without a 400; if the explicit times are reversed, it computes a negative/zero `days` and passes an inverted window to the FSM repository. Invalid-date handling is also inconsistent with bounded handlers in this package group, while `sleep` exposes no pre-query guard and the fake pool cannot protect production from receiving a reversed window.
- **chunk-0097.md:5** — `src/internal/api/stormguard/meteo.go:86-95,137-142`: The forecast client requests Open-Meteo `windgusts_10m` without setting `wind_speed_unit=ms`, while the assessment thresholds are consistently expressed as m/s (`warnGustMS=25`, `watchGustMS=17`). Open-Meteo's default forecast wind-speed unit is km/h; therefore gust values are decoded into `WindGustMS` without conversion, and roughly 25-26 km/h gusts can be falsely classified as damaging because they are compared directly with the 25 m/s threshold. The archive-history client at `src/internal/api/stormguard/meteo.go:172-183` explicitly requests `wind_speed_unit=ms`, but forecast `Fetch` does not, so the two weather paths use different units despite the same storm-assessment domain model; adding `wind_speed_unit=ms` to the forecast request (and pinning the contract in the tests) aligns the wire response with `WindGustMS` and prevents unit-dependent alerts.
- **chunk-0097.md:6** — `src/internal/api/sudo_middleware.go:244-255`: `decodeReauthBody` enables a 1 KB body cap and unknown-field rejection, but performs only one `json.Decoder.Decode` and never verifies EOF. A request such as `{"password":"secret"}{"password":"other"}` is accepted using the first object even though it contains a second JSON document. Nearby strict decoders use a second decode against EOF (for example `src/internal/api/alerts/alerts.go:277-297` and `src/internal/api/alerts/alert_rules.go:806-810`); without the same trailing-document check, the step-up credential endpoint does not enforce the single-document contract it appears to enforce.
- **chunk-0097.md:7** — `src/internal/api/stormguard/handler.go:117-133`: `UpsertConfig` decodes the request body with a single permissive `json.Decoder.Decode`, with no `DisallowUnknownFields`, body-size limit, or EOF check. Unknown fields are silently ignored, oversized payloads are not bounded, and a valid first object plus another JSON value is accepted and persisted from only the first object. The incidents and other API mutation handlers in this chunk also decode mutating JSON manually, while the catalog canonical HTTP/input policy requires strict shared transport decoding; malformed storm-guard payloads can silently update fleet charge-target state, and typos in `vehicle_id`, `target_soc`, or coordinates can become defaults.
- **chunk-0097.md:8** — `src/internal/api/status/incidents.go:104-180`: Incident Create, Patch, and AppendUpdate all use single unbounded `json.Decoder.Decode` calls with no unknown-field rejection and no EOF validation. A trailing second JSON object/value is ignored after the first mutation, and misspelled optional/required keys can silently become zero values before repository validation; the same permissive shape is repeated across three write endpoints. The repository validates business fields after decoding, but it cannot distinguish a typoed/omitted field from an omitted optional field, and a valid prefix from malformed input still causes an insert, patch, or timeline append; strict EOF/unknown-field decoding plus a payload cap is needed consistently at all three handlers.
- **chunk-0098.md:5** — `src/internal/api/tco/handler.go:31-34`, `src/internal/api/tco/ledger_handler.go:31-34`: The TCO summary handler accepts negative `vehicle_id` values (`vehicle_id=-7`), but only rejects zero. It then reaches `ComputeTCOSummary`, which rejects `vehicleID <= 0` as an internal error and incorrectly converts the bad transport value into HTTP 500; the ledger list/delete handlers already enforce positive IDs and return 400. The ledger handlers in the same package enforce `vehicleID <= 0` before calling stores (`ledger_handler.go:31-34,70-73,86-90`), and `ComputeTCOSummary` itself repeats the positive-ID contract. The summary boundary should apply it consistently and return 400 before computing or querying.
- **chunk-0098.md:6** — `src/internal/api/tco/ledger_handler.go:61-67`: TCO ledger creation decodes a single unbounded JSON object with a plain `json.Decoder.Decode`, with no `DisallowUnknownFields`, EOF/trailing-value check, or `http.MaxBytesReader` limit. Unknown keys, a second JSON value, and oversized bodies are all not rejected; typos can silently produce missing/zero fields and the handler may then return a validation 400 or, for accepted payloads, execute a write. Neighboring mutation handlers consistently cap request bodies, reject unknown fields, and verify EOF before persistence. The ledger create endpoint is therefore the only obvious weak mutation decoder in this package and can bypass the package's otherwise explicit validation contract.
- **chunk-0098.md:7** — `src/internal/api/system/handler.go:111-174`: `UpdateCheckHandler` captures a `cache` pointer and reads/writes it from concurrent HTTP requests with no synchronization. Two requests can observe or replace the cache concurrently, and the success/error response can race with cache mutation, producing a data race and nondeterministic duplicate GitHub fetches; the closure is registered as a shared handler and can serve concurrent requests. The handler's own comment promises one-hour request caching, but cache access is neither locked nor kept atomic. A shared mutex/versioned cache or per-handler synchronization is required to make that cache contract safe under concurrent dashboard refreshes.
- **chunk-0098.md:8** — `src/internal/api/system/handler.go:83-94,199-203,264-266`: Several system handlers dereference `cfg` without guarding nil even though one part of the code treats configuration as optional (`VersionHandler` explicitly checks `cfg != nil` for consent). `VersionHandler`, map-config, and config-validation paths can panic when a nil config is supplied, instead of degrading explicitly; only the consent field has a nil guard. Constructors/factories elsewhere validate nil dependencies, while these handlers are public functions and can be wired with a nil test/config snapshot. Consistent nil handling would prevent a single absent deployment config from crashing the process during diagnostics.
- **chunk-0099.md:5** — `src/internal/api/telemetry/telemetry_handler_ingest.go:116`: The HTTP telemetry ingest decoder performs one permissive `json.Decoder.Decode` call with no strict unknown-field handling, single-document EOF check, or body-size limit. A payload containing a valid first object followed by another JSON value or trailing malformed bytes is accepted and processed, while an arbitrarily large body can be read into memory. Neighboring canonical request decoding in `src/internal/api/quiethours/handler.go:100-105,119-124`, notification/channel code, and `src/internal/api/apibulk/helpers.go:67-76` rejects trailing JSON/unknown fields or bounds payloads; telemetry ingest should enforce the same transport boundary before vehicle lookup and pipeline dispatch.
- **chunk-0099.md:6** — `src/internal/api/telemetry/rule_engine.go:663-675`: Numeric alert coercion ignores `strconv.ParseFloat`/`json.Number.Float64` errors and returns zero. Thus a nonnumeric telemetry string (or malformed `json.Number`) is treated as numeric zero for threshold/range operators; comparisons such as `< 1` can then trigger an alert, despite the value not being numeric. `src/internal/api/telemetry/helpers.go:16-17` routes telemetry values through `signal.Float64`, which explicitly reports conversion success, and `src/internal/signal/coerce.go:38-75` rejects invalid strings instead of coercing them to zero. Numeric rule evaluation must preserve that validity distinction.
- **chunk-0099.md:7** — `src/internal/api/telemetry/telemetry_handler_capture.go:111-113`: Raw telemetry JSONL export silently skips every Mongo document that fails `cursor.Decode`; the response remains HTTP 200 and the stream ends normally. A corrupt or schema-incompatible document therefore produces an apparently successful but incomplete export with no count or error signal. `src/internal/api/telemetry/telemetry_handler_capture.go:92-110` establishes a streamed export contract, but unlike an explicit bad-query response it has no per-record failure reporting or rejection of partial success; export readers cannot distinguish missing captured records from a complete result.
- **chunk-0100.md:5** — `src/internal/api/tempimpact/repo.go:33-89,126-137,153-164,200-208`, `src/internal/api/tempimpact/handler.go:77-100`: The temperature-impact repository mixes SI meters with conversion factors still formatted as miles-per-100-style output. Efficiency buckets divide `distance_m` by the meters-per-mile constant and monthly trends derive efficiency from `distance_m / $2`; the JSON names promise km/100km (`avg_distance_km`, `avg_battery_pct_per_100km`, `distance_km`, `efficiency_wh_km`), but the SQL returns miles, per-mile percentage ratios, or an extra legacy `*0.75` instead. The affected values can therefore be substantially misreported even while all SQL and row scans succeed. `src/internal/api/tempimpact/shift.go:43-73` treats `AvgEfficiency` as %/100km and percentages it against a prior baseline; wrong bucket/trend units propagate directly into shift attribution and verdicts, while frontend types such as `src/web/src/api/types.ts:2168-2180` consume the same km/100km contract.
- **chunk-0100.md:6** — `src/internal/api/teslachargesess/handler.go:199-230`, `src/internal/models/tesla/tesla.go:96-118`, `src/internal/database/tesla/charging_session_repo.go:96-108,125-159`: Fleet charging-session parsing stores `energyAdded_kWh`, `peakPower_kW`, and `maxChargeRate_kW` directly in legacy unit-bearing fields/columns, whereas the neighboring charging-history integration explicitly converts Tesla kWh usage into Wh before constructing its canonical model (`src/internal/api/teslachargehist/handler.go:339-351,367-370`). The session summary then compensates only by multiplying summed kWh by 1000, while individual session values and peak/max fields remain kWh/kW and the summaries expose inconsistent units. Existing Tesla charging-history models and repos use canonical `usage_wh`/Wh, so refreshing fleet sessions stores and serves a different unit contract for equivalent charging energy. This is a unit/precision defect and can double users' monthly totals or confuse downstream display.
- **chunk-0100.md:7** — `src/internal/api/teslachargesess/handler.go:89-97`, `src/internal/api/teslaenergyhist/handler.go:371-413`: Explicit charging and energy refresh date ranges are accepted without format, ordering, or end-before-start validation. Charging sessions forward `date_from`/`date_to` verbatim, and energy history forwards `start_date`/`end_date` verbatim plus a timezone value; a reversed range or malformed date reaches the Tesla client, while the read-back window is built from a different set of rules. The catalog has `src/internal/api/apiparams/params.go:107-113` and `src/internal/api/apperror/` request validation patterns for ordered ranges, and neighboring API handlers reject reversed windows. Malformed/reversed refresh requests can become misleading successful refreshes, empty read-back results, or upstream 400/empty-page behavior instead of a handler 400.
- **chunk-0100.md:8** — `src/internal/api/telemetry/telemetry_sessions_flush_backfill.go:206-246`, `src/internal/database/position/repo.go:136-170`, `src/internal/models/telemetry/position.go:14-21`: The session backfill fallback promises an `Odometer` value in `nearestPosition`, but it never copies one because `ListByVehicle` selects no odometer column and reconstructs `Position` without odometer/range data. `nearestPosition.Odometer` therefore remains zero on every successful fallback, so drive completion cannot recompute missing distance from start/end positions and the drive logs stale/partial odometer evidence. Drive and charge completion call this fallback for start/end battery and location backfill at `src/internal/api/telemetry/telemetry_sessions_drive_tracking.go:1338-1368` and `src/internal/api/telemetry/telemetry_sessions_charge_tracking.go:913-928`; one fallback field is documented as session data but the repository/model contract makes it impossible, while other callers silently treat zero as no odometer.
- **chunk-0101.md:5** — `src/internal/api/teslaenergylivestatus/handler.go:179-182`: Live-status parsing replaces a missing or malformed upstream `timestamp` with `time.Now().UTC()` and then stores that value as the snapshot event time. The car's event time is fabricated when Tesla supplies no usable timestamp, so later history, snapshot age, and charge advice can present server observation time as vehicle telemetry time. This is the same distinction the physics package makes elsewhere (`src/internal/api/teslaphysics/types.go` clocks honesty and `src/internal/api/teslaphysics/exclusive.go:55-82`): ingest/display time must not be substituted for event time. A nullable timestamp/model field or a separately tracked observation time is needed; the current tests explicitly preserve this behavior in `src/internal/api/teslaenergylivestatus/handler_test.go:700-720`.
- **chunk-0101.md:6** — `src/internal/api/teslaenergyhist/query.go:54-72`: Backup-history parsing models `duration` as a non-pointer `int`, so a missing `duration` field becomes the indistinguishable value zero and is persisted as a real zero-second backup event. It is currently impossible for consumers to distinguish “no duration data” from “confirmed zero duration.” `src/internal/api/teslaenergyhist/query_test.go:230-245` requires missing duration to default to zero, codifying the information-loss case. The other history parsers preserve missing optional values as pointers (`src/internal/api/teslaenergyhist/query.go:14-44,84-108`), so backup duration is inconsistent with the package's null-preserving contract and can make backup aggregates misleading.
- **chunk-0101.md:7** — `src/internal/api/teslaenergyhist/handler.go:159-168,252-260,335-343`: After refreshing backup, energy, and wall-connector charging history, each handler performs its read-back using the ordinary stored-history `since/until` request values rather than the refresh request's `start_date/end_date`. A custom Tesla refresh window outside the default month can return a successful upsert while the HTTP response read-back omits the freshly written rows. The three refresh paths use `refreshDateParams` for the upstream call (`src/internal/api/teslaenergyhist/handler.go:119-147,209-239,301-322`) but independently call `energyDateRange` for post-refresh `GetByRange`; these two date-window contracts must not be substituted for one another.
- **chunk-0101.md:8** — `src/internal/api/teslaenergyhist/handler.go:371-388`, `src/internal/api/teslaenergylivestatus/handler.go:203-217`: Both historical-range helpers silently ignore malformed `since`/`until` values and silently accept a `since` later than `until`. The duplicate implementations in this chunk therefore return broad defaults or reversed database bounds instead of rejecting the request at the transport boundary. The live and energy packages duplicate the same date-only logic while there is a shared stricter date-range API in `src/internal/api/apiparams/params.go:61-104`; malformed optional values should not be treated as a valid query merely because they decode by default. Tests currently assert garbage fallback, including `src/internal/api/teslaenergylivestatus/handler_test.go:844-871`, so this behavior is active rather than only theoretical.
- **chunk-0101.md:9** — `src/internal/api/teslaenergylivestatus/handler.go:66-97,113-175`, `src/internal/api/teslaphysics/handler.go:96-99,486-508`: Live-status and physics charge/drive handlers consume path ID values without positive-ID validation. `apiparams.URLParamInt64` parses only syntax (`src/internal/api/apiparams/params.go:52-54`), so zero or negative IDs reach live-status repository and physics session/drive lookups instead of being rejected as invalid. The nearby energy-history package explicitly rejects `siteID <= 0` (`src/internal/api/teslaenergyhist/handler.go:359-367`), and `parseVehicleID` does the same in `src/internal/api/teslaphysics/handler.go:82-92`; zero/negative identifiers should follow those positive-ID handlers consistently.
- **chunk-0101.md:10** — `src/internal/api/teslaphysics/handler.go:342-454`, `src/internal/api/teslaphysics/exclusive.go:769-825`, `src/internal/api/teslaphysics/exclusive.go:227-263,481-522,524-533,535-568`: The exclusive report appends a live-state overlay to the same `PhysicsFrame` slice used by every derived view (`handler.go:395-398`), but downstream functions do not consistently distinguish live overlay from recorded telemetry. Live values can add a reported firmware epoch, be interpreted as a meter reset, be inserted into charge-port evidence, or enter the 90-second black box even though the package states that live overlays are not ingest/recorded history. The package already guards this distinction explicitly for clocks, contradictions, and logbook (`src/internal/api/teslaphysics/exclusive.go:55-82,177-225,383-427`); the unguarded functions in the second location apply recorded-history semantics to an overlay. The test in `src/internal/api/teslaphysics/exclusive_test.go:190-201` verifies live overlays do not affect clocks/car-kept-living, but does not cover meter, firmware, port, or black-box contamination.
- **chunk-0101.md:11** — `src/internal/api/teslaphysics/heartbeat.go:14-43`, `src/internal/api/teslaphysics/derive_test.go:196-222`: `BuildHeartbeat` appends a live snapshot to the historical FSD sample list, and `lastFSDTickAt` treats any upward difference as a tick at `now`. Therefore an increase seen only in the live snapshot is reported as a tick at the request's current wall clock even though the live overlay has no stored event time. This conflicts with the function documentation and package honesty: “the last observed increase” cannot become `now` merely because the live value exists. The current test checks no-tick before a meter sample but does not test an increase against the last historical reading (`src/internal/api/teslaphysics/derive_test.go:210-222`), leaving the false current-time tick untested.
- **chunk-0101.md:12** — `src/internal/api/teslaphysics/park.go:12-54`, `src/internal/api/teslaphysics/vampire.go:27-126`: Park confirmation is computed independently with separate implementations, and both can classify accessory or drain windows from short P-state runs differently because each relies on timeline sampling and wall-clock `now`, rather than a single shared confirmed-park interval computation. A sample sequence can show the 30-second confirmation threshold in one view while nearby reporting in another view uses or misses it, creating inconsistent “Park” semantics. The canonical rule is 30 seconds of continuous Gear=P (`src/internal/api/teslaphysics/types.go:parkConfirmDuration`, and `src/internal/api/teslaphysics/derive_test.go:105-131`); repeated confirmation logic in `BuildParkTruth` and `BuildVampireSplit` parameterizes different state transitions, but there is no shared helper proving both paths consume the same sample boundaries and confidence.
- **chunk-0102.md:5** — `src/internal/api/teslaphysics/handler.go:99-101,487-491`: Tesla Physics charge and drive ID parameters use `URLParamInt64` and only check parse success. Negative IDs such as session `-9` or drive `-295` pass the boundary and are sent to `GetByID`; positive-ID handlers require `> 0` elsewhere. The same parser accepts negatives in both path lookups, so malformed negative identifiers can reach charging/drive repositories instead of returning HTTP 400.
- **chunk-0102.md:6** — `src/internal/api/teslauserprofile/handler.go:107-118`, `src/internal/api/teslauserorder/handler.go:118-126,144-169`: Profile and orders refresh decode successful upstream envelopes without validating the expected `response` field. `{"response":null}` or `{}` becomes an empty profile/order batch and then calls `Upsert`/`ReplaceAll`, which can erase the last valid account snapshot; profile also persists empty required strings. Configuration refresh explicitly treats an absent/null response as an empty `{}` and has the `data` envelope contract; profile/order endpoints instead replace whole persisted datasets, so silently treating a missing response as an empty business result is a destructive data-loss edge case.
- **chunk-0102.md:7** — `src/internal/api/teslaphysics/exclusive.go:1-35,86-94,870-878`: `BuildExclusiveReport` derives its unknown-time window and certificate bounds from the first returned frame (`windowOf`), not the 14-day requested evidence window passed by the handler. If the earliest available telemetry begins hours or days after the requested start, `BuildUnknownOS` undercounts unknown time and the Vault certificate starts later than the advertised scope; live-only history intentionally remains unknown for 14 days in the same package, but stored history does not preserve that contract. The handler declares `Evidence.RequestedFrom = now.Add(-maxExclusiveLookback)` at `handler.go:356-357`, and `EvidenceScope` records the requested range, but `BuildExclusiveReport` only receives frames and recomputes a narrower `from`, creating inconsistent evidence scopes.
- **chunk-0103.md:3** — `src/internal/api/tirepressure/handler.go:62-64,99-101`: Both tire-pressure query handlers parse `vehicle_id` but reject only zero (`vehicleID == 0`), so negative IDs such as `-7` pass validation and reach `StateReader.Timeline`/`LiveStateReader.LiveState`. The same chunk's `src/internal/api/trip/handler.go:113-115` and time-machine handler reject `<= 0`, and other canonical vehicle handlers also enforce positive IDs; negative database scope keys should be rejected before a state/store query.
- **chunk-0103.md:4** — `src/internal/api/tripplanner/handler.go:63-69`, `src/internal/api/tripplanner/confidence.go:103-106`: The trip-planner Plan and Confidence POST handlers use a single permissive `json.Decoder.Decode` and have no body-size limit, strict unknown-field handling, or EOF check. A valid first JSON object followed by another value is accepted as if it were one request; oversized or typoed fields can therefore reach planning logic without the transport contract being enforced. `src/internal/api/totp/handler.go:624-628` in the same chunk already demonstrates the stronger pattern (size limit plus `DisallowUnknownFields`); it is still missing EOF, while numerous neighboring strict handlers reject unknown fields/trailing documents. Neither planner decoder currently matches either constraint.
- **chunk-0103.md:5** — `src/internal/api/tripplanner/handler.go:68-69`: Plan validation checks only `VehicleID == 0`, not `<= 0`. A negative vehicle ID passes request validation, then invokes `SignalAt` for battery/location and can be passed into planner efficiency/capacity/weather database queries. Positive-ID validation is used by `src/internal/api/trip/handler.go:113-115`, `src/internal/api/timemachine/handler.go:256-259,313-316`, and `src/internal/api/tripsdetail/handler.go:98-101`; using a negative ID should return the same 400 boundary rejection instead of scoped lookups.
- **chunk-0103.md:6** — `src/internal/api/tripplanner/confidence.go:42-54`: `ComputeConfidence` validates `current_soc` as `>0 && <=100`, but does not constrain `min_arrival_soc`; values above 100 and values that are otherwise incompatible with the calculated remaining battery are accepted. It also defaults omitted/zero capacity, efficiency, and arrival floor rather than distinguishing omitted defaults from out-of-range caller values. A caller can send `min_arrival_soc=150` or `min_arrival_soc=-10` and receive a nonphysical charge-now verdict/negative margins and a defaulted estimate, while the same function otherwise treats current SOC and distance as mandatory validity errors. The HTTP confidence endpoint should enforce all percentage and numeric ranges at the transport boundary before calculation.
- **chunk-0103.md:7** — `src/internal/api/totp/handler.go:619-632`: `decodeTOTPBody` enforces a 1 KiB cap and unknown-field rejection, but only calls `Decode` once and never checks that EOF follows the single JSON object. A second JSON value or trailing malformed JSON is ignored after the first valid TOTP object, so the verification/enrollment endpoints may process an otherwise malformed request body. A valid first object plus `{}`/junk is decoded successfully, unlike strict single-document handlers that perform EOF validation; the shared helper is used by both TOTP verification call sites, so one decoder bug affects all TOTP mutation/verify paths in this chunk.
- **chunk-0104.md:3** — `src/internal/api/user/handler.go:64-74`: The login endpoint decodes `username` and `password` but authenticates solely by comparing `username` to `"admin"`; the password is never checked. Any password, including an empty one, therefore issues an admin HMAC token. This endpoint is registered as authentication despite `AUTH_ENABLED` comments and tests explicitly demonstrate “admin any password” / “admin empty password”; it is also inconsistent with the existing password/TOTP step-up verification in `src/internal/api/sudo_middleware.go:193-224`.
- **chunk-0104.md:4** — `src/internal/api/user/handler.go:69-72`: Login uses a single permissive `json.Decoder.Decode` with no body-size limit, strict unknown-field handling, or EOF check. A valid prefix can be accepted from a multi-value/malformed body, and oversized credentials are not bounded at this endpoint. Neighboring security code uses size limits and strict one-document decoding patterns such as `src/internal/api/sudo_middleware.go:243-256`; prefix acceptance could mint an admin token from malformed transport input.
- **chunk-0104.md:5** — `src/internal/api/userpref/handler.go:46-49,88-91`: Both user-preference endpoints reject parse errors and zero IDs, but not negative IDs. `vehicle_id=-7` passes validation and reaches `Timeline` or `LiveState`, producing data-store requests for an invalid entity key rather than a 400. The same positive-ID contract is used by nearby preference/config handlers and canonical API parameter checks; negative IDs are not meaningful vehicle keys and should be rejected before any reader call.
- **chunk-0104.md:6** — `src/internal/api/vehicle/handler.go:138-142,158-162,201-205,269-273,371-375`, `src/internal/api/vehicle/silence.go:93-97`: Core vehicle handlers and `Silence` accept negative IDs because they check only `URLParamInt64` parse success. `Get`, `Delete`, `Positions`, `CurrentState`, `Wake`, and silence can pass negative IDs to repositories or signal readers.
