- **chunk-0141.md:6** — `src/internal/database/settings/reset.go:306-344,358-361`, `src/internal/database/settings/repo.go:390-466`, `src/internal/database/settings/reset_test.go:20-120`: The General and Appearance reset partitions omit typed settings that are owned by those panels. `General` does not remove `api_suspended`, `completed_tours`, font settings, or AI settings even though those are persisted typed keys; `Appearance` removes theme/mode/colors but does not remove the font family, mono font, custom fonts, font scale, leading, or tracking keys. Resetting the corresponding section therefore returns only part of the UI to defaults, while subsequent `SettingsRepo.Get()` continues to overlay the old stored rows. The second copy is the complete `SettingsRepo.Upsert` key layout and the typed defaults in `settingsDefaults()`: `reset.go` has a separate hard-coded key partition that has drifted from it. Its tests validate the canonical whitelist and defensive slice/map behavior, but no test asserts that every General/Appearance key is covered or that reset output matches defaults after the corresponding read.
- **chunk-0142.md:3** — `src/internal/database/system/software_update_repo.go:22-34`, `src/internal/database/system/software_update_repo.go:51-62`, `src/internal/api/softwareupdate/handler.go:35-40`, `src/internal/api/apiparams/params.go:89-92`: One-sided firmware-history date filters are silently ignored. Both `GetByVehicle` and `GetAll` set `hasRange` only when both `start` and `end` are non-zero, pass nil bounds to the optional predicates, and never apply a range when only one side is supplied. However, the shared date-range parser supplies independent optional bounds for `?start=` and `?end=` and the handler documents/routes them as supported filters. A request with only `start` or only `end` therefore returns the full, unbounded history instead of the requested partial window. The same faulty `hasRange` pattern is duplicated in both vehicle-scoped and fleet-wide repository methods; each bypasses the `$3`/`$2` optional predicate whenever a single bound is absent.
- **chunk-0142.md:4** — `src/migrations/000188_caggs_and_mvs.up.sql:195-210`, `src/internal/database/signal/history_writer.go:255-265`: The hourly signal aggregate is incapable of producing statistics for integer-valued signals. The CAGG computes `avg_value`, `min_value`, and `max_value` only from `float_value`, while the migration's signal-log contract explicitly stores `Int32`, `Int64`, and `Enum` samples in `int_value`; `SignalHistoryWriter.Stats` then consumes those aggregate columns for arbitrary requested signals. Integer/enum fields therefore get sample counts with `0` min/max/avg rather than their actual numeric values. `history_writer.go` treats the aggregate as a generic per-signal stats source, but the CAGG comment and SQL deliberately roll up only `float_value`; this loses the second numeric typed column without `COALESCE(float_value, int_value::float8)`.
- **chunk-0143.md:5** — `src/internal/models/tesla/tesla.go:88-118`, `src/internal/database/tesla/charging_session_repo.go:25-158`, `src/internal/api/teslachargesess/handler.go:199-232`, `src/internal/database/tesla/charging_session_repo_test.go:35-36`: Fleet charging sessions remain on legacy `kWh`/`kW` units instead of the repository's SI canonical contract: the model exposes `EnergyAddedKWh`, `PeakPowerKW`, and `MaxChargeRateKW`; SQL reads/writes `energy_added_kwh`, `peak_power_kw`, and `max_charge_rate_kw`; and the Fleet API parser passes vendor fields through unchanged. The summary therefore multiplies legacy `SUM(energy_added_kwh)` by 1000 and combines those values with Wh fields, making the stored and returned units ambiguous. Adjacent charging-history code migrated `usage_wh` to watt-hours in `000191_tesla_charging_history_si.up.sql` and already uses `TeslaChargingHistoryEntry.UsageWh`; the two account-charging paths now diverge (billing history canonical Wh, fleet sessions legacy kWh/kW). Tests preserve the legacy names and values rather than asserting a canonical SI conversion.
- **chunk-0143.md:6** — `src/internal/database/tesla/user_profile_repo.go:44-68`, `src/internal/database/tesla/user_profile_repo_test.go:74-130`: `TeslaUserProfileRepo.Upsert` dereferences `p` without validating it. A nil profile after a successful `Begin` reaches `tx.Exec(DELETE...)`, then panics on `p.Email`; the surrounding tests cover begin/delete/insert/commit errors but contain no nil-input case. The sibling order replacement and driver replacement repositories explicitly reject nil batch elements before dereference, and this package's constructor/tests treat malformed inputs defensively. Profile Upsert should reject nil with a wrapped error (or be guaranteed nonnil by its caller); otherwise a malformed caller can panic after mutation is attempted.
- **chunk-0143.md:7** — `src/internal/database/telemetry/fleet_error_repo.go:161-191`, `src/internal/database/telemetry/fleet_error_repo_test.go:373-432`, `src/internal/database/tesla/charging_history_repo.go:152-194`, `src/internal/database/tesla/charging_session_repo.go:117-161`: `TeslaFleetTelemetryErrorRepo.UpsertErrors` returns an inserted count of zero whenever one batch entry fails, even if earlier entries already succeeded inside the transaction. The transaction rolls back on the error, but the implementation discards partial progress via `return 0`; it also differs from this chunk's charging batch repos, which return their partial `upserted` count plus a contextual error. The test explicitly pins count zero on an `Exec` failure, so it codifies the inconsistent transaction-success accounting rather than the package's alternate batching semantics; callers cannot distinguish whether any row was committed and receive an error with a count that is always zero.
- **chunk-0144.md:5** — `src/internal/database/trip/repo.go:392-435`, `src/internal/database/trip/detail_repo.go:129-137`, `src/internal/database/trip/monthly_trip_test.go:312-331`: The monthly-trip generator treats any existing trip at `(vehicle_id, month_start)` as eligible for `UpsertMonthTrip` ownership. `UpsertMonthTrip` then renames the row, changes `ended_at`, and sets `auto_generated=true` even if it is a user-created trip. A user trip at the current month boundary is clobbered into an autogenerated monthly summary, while the past-month finalize test only protects user rows in the separate stale-finalization path. The lookup has no `created_by_user IS NULL` or `auto_generated` guard. `Get...`/detail code and monthly generation share the same `trips` table, but only the generator mutates it; the current-month upsert path is not constrained like `finalizePastMonths`, so user names and custom end dates are lost. The existing user-trip preservation test stops at past months and does not cover this same-boundary current-month path.
- **chunk-0144.md:6** — `src/internal/database/trip/repo.go:44-69`, `src/internal/database/trip/detail_repo.go:127-137`: The trip charge/count and cost overlap queries use an active charging session's `ended_at`, falling back to its `started_at`. For an active session that began before `t.started_at`, the fallback makes `COALESCE(cs.ended_at, cs.started_at) < t.started_at`, so a session that still overlaps the trip is excluded even though it is active until now. Both the list aggregate and detail aggregate therefore undercount charge count and cost for early-in-window sessions. The same lower-bound expression appears in both trip SQL projections and can diverge from actual overlap semantics. Compare the correct interval `[started_at, NOW()]` for an active session against `[trip.started_at, trip ended/NOW()]`; the current fallback treats its active end as its start.
- **chunk-0144.md:7** — `src/internal/database/tesla/vehicle_driver_repo.go:48-73,105-147`, `src/internal/database/tesla/vehicle_driver_repo_test.go:73-143,144-217`: Replace operations trust invitation/driver fields supplied by the caller instead of anchoring writes to the method's `vehicleID`. `ReplaceDriversForVehicle` deletes drivers for vehicle 42 but inserts each row using `d.VehicleID` and `d.VIN`; `ReplaceInvitationsForVehicle` similarly inserts `inv.VehicleID`/`inv.VIN`, and `InsertInvitation` uses the supplied invitation identity but has no vehicle consistency check. A stale or malformed object can delete one vehicle's rows and recreate them under another vehicle, bypassing a per-vehicle replacement contract. Driver and invitation replacement are parallel transactional paths; both tests build objects whose IDs match 42 and therefore never pin cross-vehicle arguments. There is no repository guard binding output rows to the vehicleID argument used by DELETE.
- **chunk-0144.md:8** — `src/internal/database/tesla/vehicle_driver_repo.go:105-147`, `src/internal/database/tesla/vehicle_driver_repo_test.go:212-299`: Invitation replacement and upsert persist `created_at` as the local operation time and discard the API/model `inv.CreatedAt` value. This changes externally reported invitation creation time: repeated replacement/upsert calls rewrite the timestamp to now, so displays and later sorting no longer reflect when Tesla created the invitation. Both `ReplaceInvitationsForVehicle` and `InsertInvitation` contain the same `now`-for-`created_at` behavior and the tests assert “timestamps set to now,” which pins the wrong source timestamp rather than preserving model evidence. `FetchedAt` should be refreshed, while `CreatedAt` should come from the Tesla record.
- **chunk-0145.md:5** — `src/internal/database/worker/status_queue_repo.go:254-266`, `src/internal/api/queuestatus/handler.go:204-211`, `src/web/src/features/admin/components/QueueStatusPanel.tsx:81-87`, `src/web/src/features/admin/components/continuation-admin-2/QueueWorkerCard.tsx:17-19`: The automation counter query assigns the age of the oldest `status='running'` row to `OldestPendingAgeSecond`, and that value is serialized as `oldest_pending_age_seconds`. Because automations explicitly have no queued/pending state, a long-running automation is reported to the API/UI as an old pending job even when `pending` is zero. Operators can therefore receive a false queue-delay warning for active work. The repository comment and SQL acknowledge that automation has no pending state; nevertheless the handler and both queue-panel components consume the same field as pending age and format it as a duration. Notification/export compute it correctly from their actual pending rows, so the automation contract needs a distinct running-age field or no pending-age value.
- **chunk-0146.md:5** — `src/internal/dataquality/scorer.go:258-289`, `src/internal/dataquality/firmware_canonicalization_test.go:185-237`: `mergeFieldScores` reconstructs the duplicate ratio by weighting each side's ratio with `SampleCount`, although those ratios are calculated from `duplicateCount/comparisonCount` where the comparison denominator is `sampleCount-1` per vehicle/field stream. This loses the exact comparison counts at scoring boundaries and can materially bias merged `DuplicateRatio`/composite severity when fragmented firmware rows are merged; using sample counts is explicitly only documented as the sole denominator available, not as an exact duplicate merge. The test at `TestMergeFieldScores` pins sample-weighted behavior as mean-like expectations and supplies ratios rather than duplicate/comparison counts, so it cannot detect or prevent incorrect weighting from the true stream denominators.
- **chunk-0147.md:3** — `src/internal/domain/charging/guards.go:13-15`, `src/internal/app/chargingsvc/service.go:34-49`, `src/internal/domain/charging/fsm.go:20-36`: `CanComplete` requires positive added energy and a non-decreasing battery level, but `setupFSM` never registers it as a guard. The charging FSM consequently accepts `charging -> completing` and `completing -> completed` without validating completion data. The domain unit test proves the guard exists, but the service never invokes it, so a completed session with zero energy or an impossible battery decrease is persistable. The SubFSM and parent FSM expose an explicit completion guard and test it independently, while only the production service wiring omits registration; the parent transition is a direct copy of the guarded domain behavior that can otherwise be bypassed.
- **chunk-0147.md:4** — `src/internal/adapter/postgres/queries/charging.go:13-24,48-69`, `src/internal/adapter/postgres/charging_repository.go:25-31,68-72,82-86`, `src/internal/database/charging/repo.go:18-39`, `src/migrations/000184_charging_si.up.sql:1-120`: The hexagonal charging adapter fabricates FSM/session fields that do not exist in the canonical SI charging schema: reads hard-code `fsm_state='completed'`, `sub_fsm_state=''`, and `charger_connected=false`, while `cost_cents` is synthesized from `cost_decimal`; writes upsert only those values and ignore the domain `FSMState`, `SubFSMState`, and `ChargerConnected`. Consequently `GetByID` followed by `HandleEvent(EventStartCharge)` looks at the fake disconnected state and cannot enter charging, and starts never persist their real FSM state. The database repository shown beside it uses the canonical session table directly, but the hexagonal service/adapter path is incompatible with that same contract. The canonical migration's SI table and the domain service maintain separate concepts: the migration stores live session telemetry columns, while the adapter invents 14-column domain projections and test fixtures pin the synthetic values; this risks every event/transition flow silently acting against false state.
- **chunk-0147.md:5** — `src/internal/app/chargingsvc/service.go:66,99,107,113`, `src/internal/app/exportsvc/service.go:47,76,84,90`, `src/internal/domain/vehicle/validation.go:20`: Charging/export service timestamps are created with `time.Now()` rather than UTC-normalized time, and the second copy in export repeats the same contract violation during create, completion, and transition-history records. `time.Time` carries the local location even though database/API timestamps are expected in UTC; serialized values and same-instant comparisons can therefore retain source-location differences across hosts/configurations. The export and charging application services both construct persisted timestamps independently instead of a shared clock/UTC boundary; unlike the vehicle-domain validator, they do not normalize `time.Now()` before storing or emitting it.
- **chunk-0148.md:3** — `src/internal/domain/fsm/sub_fsm.go:31-52`, `src/internal/domain/fsm/sub_fsm.go:60-71`, `src/internal/domain/fsm/engine_test.go:492-506`: `RegisterSubFSM` couples deactivation to `ResetOnExit`: when the parent exits an owning state with `ResetOnExit=false`, the OnExit hook leaves the SubFSM `Active=true` and still at its current state. The API comment promises the SubFSM is deactivated on parent exit regardless of reset, while `FireSub` only checks the `Active` flag and therefore allows work to continue inside a SubFSM after its parent has left the state; an inactive child may also remain reusable until a later parent entry. The auto-enter hook sets `Active=true`; there is no corresponding unconditional deactivation. The existing parent-exit tests cover only `ResetOnExit=true` and therefore would not catch this branch. It risks stale child transitions and terminal bubbling from a state no longer owned by the parent.
- **chunk-0149.md:5** — `src/internal/domain/vehicle/validation.go:11-18,65-76`, `src/internal/domain/vehicle/fsm_test.go:145-165`, `src/internal/handler/dto/vehicle.go:26-31`, `src/internal/handler/dto/vehicle_test.go:28-39`: The domain claims to validate a VIN checksum, but `isValidVINChecksum` only checks length (at its caller) and forbidden characters. It does not validate the VIN check digit at position 9, so any 17-character string containing allowed uppercase/lowercase alphanumerics is accepted as a valid VIN. Transport validation also enforces only length, leaving malformed VINs to reach enrollment unless some unrelated caller rejects them. The method name/comment/documented domain invariant promise full checksum validation, while the tests cover only `I/O/Q`, length, and forbidden-character examples rather than a mismatched check digit. This creates persisted invalid vehicle identities and cache/adapter lookups that cannot be reconciled by VIN.
- **chunk-0149.md:6** — `src/internal/domain/vehicle/types.go:27-32`, `src/internal/app/vehiclesvc/service.go:97-98`, `src/internal/adapter/tesla/mapper.go:54-60`, `src/internal/port/external/tesla.go:13-17`: The domain lifecycle projection retains legacy unit-bearing names `OdometerMiles`/`RangeMiles`, while the active SI contract requires canonical meter values and display-boundary conversion. The mapper/vehicle service copy these values into those fields from Tesla API state without an explicit SI conversion or source unit, so same-named legacy fields can be interpreted as miles and expose values through mixed contracts. The same legacy suffixes appear in the Tesla state port, Tesla mapper, and vehicle service. They bypass the canonical `DistanceM`/range SI boundary and make negative/policy tests and future consumers ambiguous about which unit the cached working data contains.
- **chunk-0149.md:7** — `src/internal/domain/charging/validation.go:17-23`, `src/internal/domain/charging/types.go:15-20`, `src/internal/domain/trip/validation.go:8-21`, `src/internal/domain/trip/types.go:11-23`: Numeric charging/trip aggregate validation is incomplete: charging sessions reject invalid battery levels and charger types but allow negative added energy, maximum power, and cost, while trips reject negative distance and energy but allow negative maximum speed and efficiency. These are impossible persisted aggregates and can propagate into analytics, charge completion, and display calculations once domain objects bypass other handlers. The neighboring validation code applies explicit non-negative/bounded checks to some fields, but omits the remaining numeric invariants in the same aggregates. For example, `StartBatteryLevel > 100` is rejected but `EnergyAddedWh < 0` is accepted; a negative trip speed passes even though distance/energy are considered safety-critical.
- **chunk-0150.md:3** — `src/internal/enums/parse_climate.go:9-11`, `src/internal/enums/parse_test.go:122-137`, `src/internal/tesla/codec/coercion.go:257-270`, `src/internal/tesla/codec/coercion_test.go:498-524`, `src/internal/enums/constants.go:42-47`: `ParseHvacPower` reports false for the canonical HVAC state `HvacOverheatProtect`/`OverheatProtect`, although the codec defines all four active states — `On`, `Precondition`, and `OverheatProtect` — as true and only `Off`/`Unknown` as false/unknown. Callers reading live or legacy values (`vehicle_service`, `redis_state_provider`, and watch handlers) therefore disagree with the telemetry codec: an HVAC system in overheat protection is physically powered/running but is reported as off. The same enum mapping exists in `coerceHvacPower` and the codec test table; those explicitly require `OverheatProtect` to map true. `ParseHvacPower` independently implements an incomplete substring mapping and drifts on a generated-proto variant.
- **chunk-0150.md:4** — `src/internal/enums/parse.go:24-41`, `src/internal/enums/parse_test.go:90-120`, `src/internal/service/vehicle_service.go:345-356`, `src/internal/service/vehicle_service.go:528-533`, `src/internal/automation/redis_state_provider.go:74-77`: `ParseEnumBool` treats every non-empty string not containing `Off` as true, so unknown enum strings such as `SentryModeStateUnknown`/`Unknown` are converted to an enabled/active boolean. This is dangerous for consumers of enum-backed booleans (notably SentryMode): the guard repository explicitly reserves an unknown token and distinguishes it from `Off`, while the generic helper loses that distinction and can report sentry on when state is unknown. String matching is also prefixed and case-insensitive only by accident; it has no enum-specific false/unknown contract. The test suite currently pins unknown as absent for no cases and includes only explicit normalized `Idle`/Armed/Off examples. A separate sentinel-aware implementation in the guard/daylog code treats `Unknown` differently, so live-state fallback can disagree with stored security state when telemetry input is prefixed or unknown.
- **chunk-0151.md:5** — `src/internal/events/events.go:58-79`, `src/internal/events/events.go:87-102`, `src/internal/events/events_test.go:28-54`, `src/internal/outbox/mqtt_publisher.go:84-115`, `src/internal/outbox/doc.go:12-39`: The in-process MQTT event bus silently reports no delivery result: `Publish` checks timeout only, not `token.Error()`, while `Subscribe` neither waits for the subscribe acknowledgement nor checks its error. Callers therefore receive neither a failed publish nor a failed subscription, can miss events after broker partitions/reconnects, and there is no retry signal at the bus boundary. `outbox.MQTTBusPublisher.PublishOutbox` handles both the timeout and `token.Error()` and returns typed retryable failures, but repository search found no `Store.Append` producer or composition of `NewDispatcher`/`NewMQTTBusPublisher`; the direct bus remains the production publication path and conflicts with the outbox's at-least-once contract.
- **chunk-0151.md:6** — `src/internal/events/events.go:35-45`, `src/internal/outbox/store.go:14-23`, `src/internal/outbox/mqtt_publisher.go:35-37`, `src/internal/outbox/dispatcher.go:36-45`: The event envelope is split across incompatible domain names and optional fields: `events.Event` marshals `{type, vehicle_id, vin, data, timestamp}`, but `outbox.Event` replaces `data` with `Payload` and omits the canonical top-level event timestamp shape. The payload passing boundary is therefore payload-shaped, not an `events.Event`, while documentation says the outbox can wrap one without re-marshalling; consumers cannot depend on a single stable event envelope across direct publish and outbox replay. The outbox row carries raw payload bytes and explicitly documents replay of `(event_type, vehicle_id, ts)`, but neither direct bus publish nor the outbox store exposes or enforces a timestamp/vehicle envelope consistently. Out-of-order or at-least-once replay can mix payload contracts and break subscribers that decode the direct `events.Event` shape.
- **chunk-0152.md:3** — `src/internal/export/processor.go:263-320`, `src/internal/export/columns.go:15-40`, `src/internal/export/export_test.go:21-42`, `src/internal/export/types.go:28-32`: Trip exports advertise `TypeTrips`, dispatch into `processTrips`, and expose trip CSV/JSON cell helpers, but `columnCatalog` has no `"trips"` entry. `processTrips` therefore gets a nil trip column list. Default CSV export writes a header with no columns and then writes no fields for every trip; a caller-supplied `columns` allowlist is not validated and the filtered JSON/CSV paths produce empty trip cells instead of trip columns. `TestSupportsColumnSelection` also omits trips, so the broken path has no coverage. The drive/charging branches use catalog-backed columns from the same processor, but trips are the only supported export in the switch that has neither a catalog entry nor a supported-column branch. JSON without a column selection does happen to retain the historical `tripRow`, but all column-aware CSV/JSON requests and its `AvailableColumns` contract fail.
- **chunk-0152.md:4** — `src/internal/export/analytics.go:50-52,91-96,99-148,202-214`, `src/internal/api/analytics/queries.go:133-223,261-336`, `src/internal/export/processor.go:31-55`: The export analytics implementation claims to mirror the fleet API but diverges on request bounds and completeness: it calls the drive/charging repositories with `time.Time{}` bounds instead of `req.StartDate`/`req.EndDate`, then only filters drives against a computed 30-day/default cutoff and sessions against that same cutoff, silently ignoring `req.EndDate`; it discards repository errors instead of returning a partial-result record; and `fleetSessions += len(allSessions)` counts every fetched session while its loop filters individual sessions by cutoff. It also leaves `allDriveEfficiencies`, `chargeEfficiencies`, `tempVsEfficiency`, and charging-start-battery work empty or absent even though the canonical API analytics endpoint populates per-drive Wh/km and temp-vs-efficiency. The two implementations use the same repositories/models and output keys but differ in date semantics: the API passes independently optional cutoff/end bounds and adds partial-result reasons, while the export can include late-ending or old records inconsistently and report aggregate session counts that do not match the filtered rows. Missing efficiency populations produce zero statistics where the live report contains data, even for identical repositories and ranges.
- **chunk-0152.md:5** — `src/internal/export/account.go:20-105,126-166`, `src/internal/export/account_test.go:34-119`: `processAccount` records `manifest.TotalSizeBytes`, but does not set it until after `manifest.json` has been marshaled and written to the ZIP. The first and only `json.MarshalIndent(manifest,...)` sees the zero-value `TotalSizeBytes`, so every account archive's manifest claims `0` bytes even though the archive then has a real size; the code has no second marshal to correct it. The implementation also skips a table fetch or CSV-encode error with only a warning, while the manifest comment says it records the gap, but the manifest contains no skipped/error table list at all—only successfully emitted tables. Account archive consumers/tests cannot verify actual archive bytes or distinguish a missing table from a successful table with zero rows. The existing tests cover CSV rows/date/column behavior and core-table names but not manifest completeness or the `total_size_bytes` invariant.
- **chunk-0152.md:6** — `src/internal/export/processor.go:555-598`, `src/internal/export/account.go:71-92`, `src/internal/database/export/repo.go:24-46`, `src/migrations/000180_drop_legacy_telemetry.up.sql:45-55`: Backup and account export allowlists still include several names that migration `000180` drops, including `vampire_drain_events`, while also retaining `daily_mileage`, `vehicle_states`, and `visited_locations`. `processBackup` queries each stale name and silently drops failures; account export does the same and records only successful tables. Neither manifest/backup metadata gives an honest omission/error list, so a job can be marked ready and consumers can interpret an incomplete archive as a full snapshot. The same stale table names are duplicated across the backup map and account allowlist, and the migration explicitly removes those relations. The account/backup result can therefore differ from the live schema and silently omit data without changing record-count/status semantics.
- **chunk-0153.md:5** — `src/internal/flags/store.go:250-254,260-278`, `src/internal/api/apiflagsh/handler.go:85-91,105-145`: The HTTP handlers and store do not normalize flag keys consistently. `Set` and `Delete` trim keys before touching Redis, but the handler response and audit row retain the original chi URL parameter, and `Get` looks up the raw handler key even though `Store.Get` trims it internally. Therefore a key containing surrounding whitespace can be stored/deleted under its trimmed name while the API reports/audits a different key, creating an audit trail that cannot identify the flag actually changed and making the `Deleted` response misleading. Store code independently trims in `Get`, `Set`, and `Delete`; handler code repeats only the HTTP-facing key checks and never applies the same contract, so transport and persistence disagree.
- **chunk-0153.md:6** — `src/internal/flags/store.go:298-322`, `src/internal/flags/store_test.go:42-72`: `GetFloat` and `GetInt` only fall back for parse errors; that is mostly correct for integers, but `GetFloat` accepts non-finite strings such as `NaN`, `+Inf`, and `-Inf` because `strconv.ParseFloat` returns them successfully. This contradicts the package contract that malformed values return the caller's default and can inject a non-finite feature value into calculations or comparisons. Numeric parsing is tested only with finite `"3.14"` and `"forty-two"`; no test excludes `NaN`/infinity, so the implementation currently passes a malformed value through silently.
- **chunk-0153.md:7** — `src/internal/flags/doc.go:8-9`, `src/internal/flags/store.go:72-74,363-386`: The package documentation names the Redis Pub/Sub channel as `teslasync:flags:changes`, but the implementation publishes and subscribes on `teslasync:flags:invalidate`. An external subscriber that follows the documented channel will never receive invalidations or writes remain stale locally until the TTL expires. The implementation is internally consistent with `RedisInvalidateChannel`, while `doc.go` repeats an obsolete/different public channel name; there is no second implementation using `:changes` in this chunk.
- **chunk-0155.md:3** — `src/internal/fsm/automation/machine.go:51-53`, `src/internal/fsm/automation/machine.go:259-276`, `src/internal/fsm/automation/machine_test.go`: The observer contract says callbacks are delivered after unlocking, but `transition()` invokes `observer(...)` synchronously while `ExecutionFSM.mu` is still held (every `transition` caller holds the same mutex). An observer that calls any FSM method, such as `State()`, `Transitions()`, or `ContextSnapshot()`, deadlocks. The concurrency tests exercise FSM reads with no observer, so they do not cover this contract violation or callback reentrancy. This is distinct from ordinary observer delivery: `SetObserver` only stores a callback, and there is no async or unlocked delivery path.
- **chunk-0155.md:4** — `src/internal/fsm/automation/machine.go:101-115`, `src/internal/fsm/automation/machine_test.go:306-323`: `IsRunComplete()` returns true for `Idle`, `Succeeded`, `GaveUp`, `Skipped`, and `Disabled`, but omits `Partial`, even though `MarkPartial()` documents partial completion and resets the execution failure counter. A caller using this API after `MarkPartial()` will incorrectly treat a completed run with some successful actions as still active; only `Succeeded` is asserted by the existing table and lifecycle tests. `ResetFromSuccess()` accepts both `Succeeded` and `Partial`, confirming that `Partial` is a successful terminal execution state just like `Succeeded`.
- **chunk-0155.md:5** — `src/internal/fsm/charge/machine.go:127-143`, `src/internal/fsm/charge/machine.go:170-183`, `src/internal/fsm/drive/machine.go:145-169`: Charge completion accepts `BatteryLevel` for start/end snapshots and `Soc` only as a start fallback; it never updates the end snapshot or continuous end battery from `Soc`. Drive completion does update `EndBattery` from `Soc` in `extractEndSnapshot`, so the two session implementations disagree for telemetry batches that report `Soc` without `BatteryLevel`. In charge completing state, `EndBattery` therefore remains zero and the required-end check keeps waiting until timeout even though a valid end battery signal exists. No charge-only copy of the drive `Soc` fallback exists. The second implementation is `extractEndSnapshot` at `src/internal/fsm/drive/machine.go`; its `Soc` fallback is the behavior the charge machine should preserve.
- **chunk-0155.md:6** — `src/internal/fsm/drive/state.go:51-68`, `src/internal/fsm/drive/machine.go:153-181`, `src/internal/fsm/drive/validation.go:12-46`, `src/internal/tesla/units/units.go:24-58`, `src/internal/tesla/units/units_test.go:22-27`: The drive sub-FSM is not SI-safe even though telemetry normalization converts `Odometer` to meters: `Distance()` returns raw end-minus-start meters and is documented as miles, while validation compares it against `>500 miles` and efficiency against `100-600 Wh/mi`. Normal reads of a several-kilometer trip can therefore trigger false distance/efficiency warnings or use the wrong units. The canonical unit conversion tests explicitly require `Odometer` values to be produced as meters, so this is a contract mismatch rather than vendor-specific legacy data. The charge sub-FSM uses SI `EnergyAdded` in Wh and W values consistently; the drive implementation mixes canonical SI distance/energy with legacy mile/mi validation constants and labels.
- **chunk-0155.md:7** — `src/internal/fsm/drive/machine.go:172-181`, `src/internal/fsm/drive/state.go:28-43`, `src/internal/fsm/drive/validation.go:20-29`: Drive accumulation treats every `PackCurrent*PackVoltage` sample as exactly one watt-hour divided by 3600 (`watts/3600.0`) and accepts an unused `now` argument; it neither subtracts the previous sample timestamp nor scales by actual elapsed seconds. Dense telemetry batches therefore accumulate synthetic energy (one additional 1/3600-hour sample per sample) that is not Wh, and sparse batches undercount because their event interval is ignored. `ValidationIssues` can consequently report negative or implausible net energy/efficiency based on fabricated values. `accumulate` is also called during `Recovered` state, but still has no elapsed-time state. This differs from all current validation semantics, which assume cumulative `TotalEnergy`/`RegenEnergy` values in Wh.
- **chunk-0155.md:8** — `src/internal/fsm/command/machine.go:185-224`, `src/internal/fsm/command/machine_test.go:97-155`: `StatusMessage()` returns an empty string for `WakeTimeout`, `Retrying`, and `TimedOut`; it also has no status for `Failed` versus retry scheduling despite distinguishing terminal states elsewhere. The wake and command lifecycle tests verify state transitions and some final messages but never assert status text for these in-progress states, so SSE/UI consumers receive no status during wake timeout, retry delay, or command timeout instead of useful progress. The neighboring states each have concrete messages; the omissions create gaps in the same switch rather than separate domain contracts.
- **chunk-0156.md:3** — `src/internal/fsm/machine.go:275-324`, `src/internal/fsm/machine.go:287-324`, `src/internal/fsm/machine.go:237-243`: Public FSM APIs `CheckPending` and `CancelPending` access and mutate the `pending` debounce field without acquiring `VehicleFSM.mu`, while all writes through `ProcessSignalsAt`/`handleDebounced` and reads through `DebugInfo` assume synchronized access. Concurrent timeout/check/reconciliation callers can race the debounce pointer with a signal batch, producing a data race and potentially committing a stale pending transition or losing one; the retry comment in `CheckPending` also promises commit behavior but provides no synchronization. `ProcessSignalsAt`, `commit`, and `DebugInfo` lock `m.mu` for the same fields; `CheckPending` and `CancelPending` are the only public paths in this chunk that bypass that protection.
- **chunk-0156.md:4** — `src/internal/fsm/telemetry/machine.go:181-196`: Public `Snapshot()` returns connection-FSM metrics without locking and reads mutable fields (`vin`, `data_source`, `batch_count`, `signal_count`, and `lastBatchAt`) while `RecordBatch`, timeout checks, and transitions update them under `mu`. This creates a data race for any concurrent health/API snapshot call. The private `snapshotLocked()` implementation already enforces that the caller holds the mutex, but public `Snapshot()` calls `time.Since` and reads all fields directly instead of locking or delegating under the lock.
- **chunk-0156.md:5** — `src/internal/fsm/notification/delivery.go:164-176`, `src/internal/fsm/notification/delivery.go:101-132`, `src/internal/fsm/notification/notification_test.go:59-68`, `src/internal/fsm/notification/notification_test.go:70-80`: `MarkChannelResult` ignores its `channelType` when aggregating the notification state: if a channel result is recorded for a channel that was never configured, every existing configured channel remains `pending`, `allDone` stays false, and no terminal transition occurs. Conversely, duplicate/unknown channel results neither validate nor add channels, leaving legitimate deliveries stuck in `Sending` or exposing no terminal state. Delivery transition tests cover only configured channel names, so this silent failure mode is not detected. The channel status loop scans and updates configured types by exact string equality, but the subsequent `allDone/anySuccess/anyFailed` aggregation does not distinguish a matched channel from an unknown one; all callers therefore have partial/no result if an external integration supplies a misspelled or newly added channel type.
- **chunk-0157.md:3** — `src/internal/api/geocode/handler.go:67-71`, `src/internal/geocoding/search.go:94-102`, `src/internal/geocoding/google.go:48-64`, `src/internal/geocoding/azure.go:50-68`, `src/internal/geocoding/nominatim.go:97-114`, `src/internal/api/geocode/handler_test.go:409-422`: Reverse coordinates are parsed and forwarded without geographic-range validation: `NaN`, `Inf`, and latitude/longitude values outside `[-90,90]`/`[-180,180]` pass `strconv.ParseFloat` and are sent to Google, Azure, and Nominatim. The boundary test only asserts that valid corners pass; it does not reject invalid/floating-point special values. Forward search has the same gap because `parseLatLng` only performs `fmt.Sscanf` and does not verify ranges or finite values before returning results. Provider implementations independently build outbound URLs from the parsed values (`google.go`, `azure.go`, `nominatim.go`), and the search parser independently accepts malformed upstream strings. Invalid coordinates can produce provider 4xx/HTML responses, JSON encoding failures, or cache/tracking keys at impossible locations instead of returning a local `400`/skipping malformed search results.
- **chunk-0157.md:4** — `src/internal/geocoding/search.go:62-72`, `src/internal/geocoding/nominatim.go:99-105`: Both Nominatim HTTP clients implement the same one-second limiter by sleeping before `httpClient.Do`, while holding a mutex but ignoring the request context. A cancelled/deadline-expired request still waits for the full sleep and every other waiting call blocks on the same mutex; `time.Sleep` cannot wake when the caller context ends. Reverse and forward clients have separate copies and clocks, and neither test exercises cancellation during throttling. Burst traffic can hold unrelated Nominatim requests for multiple seconds and bypass the effective request deadline even though the handler has installed a timeout.
- **chunk-0157.md:5** — `src/internal/geocoding/geocoder.go:50-80`, `src/internal/geocoding/nominatim_test.go:7-54`, `src/internal/geocoding/geocoder_test.go:7-65`: `GeoResult.ShortName` caps a `DisplayName` with a byte-length slice at 60 bytes, not by UTF-8/rune boundary. Provider address strings can contain multibyte Unicode, so a valid 60-byte+ string may be cut through the middle of a rune and return invalid UTF-8. The existing display-name tests use only ASCII and would pin the current behavior. This is a provider-facing label path used directly by drive/telemetry handlers and the geocode API, so overseas addresses can produce malformed response strings or replacement-character display corruption.
- **chunk-0157.md:6** — `src/internal/geocoding/azure.go:31-48,81-91`, `src/internal/geocoding/nominatim.go:136-175`, `src/internal/geocoding/google.go:104-145`: Azure reverse conversion does not populate the canonical `GeoResult.Name` field even though Azure's address response may include POI/premise data and the package contract explicitly requires POIs from every provider for distinguishing endpoints. Only Google and Nominatim extract POI/amenity/shop/premise names in separate adapters. `azureAddress` defines only freeform/street/locality fields, so a configured Azure provider with the first result being a POI falls back to `Road, Suburb`/`DisplayName` while the same coordinate through Google or Nominatim can return its named place. Drive start/destination labels and geofence attachment therefore change provider-dependent data quality.
- **chunk-0157.md:7** — `src/internal/geocoding/search.go:94-102`, `src/internal/geocoding/search.go:104-108`: Forward search drops every result at exact coordinates `(0,0)` and only because the parser/consumer uses the sentinel `lat == 0 && lng == 0`. Separately, `parseLatLng` accepts partial numeric strings (for example `12junk`) via `fmt.Sscanf` without requiring full-string parsing and may manufacture a coordinate. Nominatim can legitimately return Null Island or a malformed provider field can produce a partial number; the result loop then reports `Skip` for the former and may return unusable coordinates for the latter. There is no provider-result range/finite-value validation before forwarding the API payload.
- **chunk-0157.md:8** — `src/internal/geocoding/chain.go:28-36`, `src/internal/adapter/geocoding/chain.go:23-42`, `src/internal/api/geocode/handler.go:92-99`: Both geocoder chain implementations accept `(nil, nil)` from an upstream provider as an ordinary success; the package chain returns it immediately, while the hexagonal chain also stops short-circuiting and returns the nil address. Only the HTTP reverse handler has a defensive nil-result check; telemetry/trip service paths do not necessarily guard it and may call methods such as `ShortName` on the nil result. The provider interfaces permit no explicit invariant that success must return a non-nil value, and each chain has independent implementation/test coverage. The package chain tests cover an empty provider result but not a nil result with no error, while the adapter chain tests similarly omit this contract violation.
- **chunk-0158.md:5** — `src/internal/handler/dto/vehicle.go:58-69`, `src/internal/handler/dto/vehicle_test.go:65-87`, `src/internal/domain/vehicle/types.go:27-32`, `src/internal/app/vehiclesvc/service.go:97-98`, `src/internal/port/external/tesla.go:13-17`, `src/internal/adapter/tesla/mapper.go:54-60`, `src/web/src/features/dashboard/widgets/OdometerCounterWidget.tsx:20-92`: The vehicle DTO still exports legacy `RangeMiles`/`OdometerMiles` fields with `rangeMiles`/`odometerMiles` JSON keys. This preserves a unit-bearing transport contract even though the active pipeline stores and reads SI (`DistanceM`, meters) and display code expects SI values; the mapper also copies Fleet API state directly into the legacy domain fields, so clients can receive unconverted mile values while treating them as the same metric that newer SI widgets convert as meters. The DTO duplicates the legacy names already present in the domain, service, Tesla port, and mapper. The DTO tests pin those exact mile keys, but the frontend has canonical SI consumers (for example `range_m` and the odometer meter display path), so old and new contracts can disagree without runtime type or unit validation.
- **chunk-0158.md:6** — `src/internal/handler/middleware/cors.go:16-43`, `src/internal/api/router.go:625-637`: `CORS` sets `Access-Control-Max-Age` with `http.StatusText(cfg.MaxAge)`, which is not a duration formatter: `http.StatusText(86400)` is empty, and in other configurations the header becomes an HTTP reason string such as `""` instead of decimal cache seconds. The default preflight value therefore fails browser CORS max-age handling and may be emitted as an invalid/empty header. A separate active router middleware correctly supplies a numeric `MaxAge: 300`, so the unused handler package implementation is an easy duplicate to drift. Any caller that adopts this local CORS helper gets behavior different from the canonical router.
- **chunk-0158.md:7** — `src/internal/handler/middleware/auth.go:28-58`: `Auth(validateFn == nil)` is documented to check only for an `Authorization` header, but it accepts the malformed bearer token `"Bearer "` because `strings.TrimPrefix` yields the empty string and the nil-validator branch never validates that `token` is non-empty. It therefore passes a header with no credential for any route using this middleware. The JWT and ForwardAuth implementations elsewhere have explicit identity/token checks, while this standalone helper does not, creating an auth inconsistency that can become an authorization bypass if this middleware is mounted instead of the canonical composition-root auth.
- **chunk-0158.md:8** — `src/internal/handler/middleware/idempotency.go:18-73`: Idempotency replay is keyed only by request path plus user-supplied key, without HTTP method, authenticated subject, body, or tenant scope, and it caches every downstream status and body after the first mutation. The same key can replay another user's or operation's response at the same URL; it also replays errors as successes, and the Redis write uses `context.Background()` rather than request context. There are no verified callers in `src`, but the implementation is exported middleware and advertises state-mutating protection globally. A future mount would give keys cross-user replay risk, while no cache namespace includes method/body/identity and `X-Idempotency-Replay` exposes replay without a canonical replay contract.
- **chunk-0159.md:3** — `src/internal/handler/v1/trip_handler.go:30-35`, `src/web/src/api/hooks/useTrips.ts:25-34`, `src/internal/handler/v1/charging_handler.go:30-38`, `src/web/src/api/hooks/useCharging.ts:60-66`: The v1 trip list handler rejects the canonical snake_case parameter used by its frontend hook: it only reads `vehicleId`, while `useTrips` sends `vehicle_id`. A valid `/trips?vehicle_id=...` request therefore returns `400 vehicleId query parameter required`; the charging handler exposes the defect even though it intentionally falls back to `vehicle_id`. `useTrips` and `useChargingSessions` both send snake_case, but the Trip and Charging handlers disagree: trip accepts only camelCase, charging accepts camelCase first and snake_case second. This breaks a canonical frontend/backend route contract and can silently mix legacy and canonical parameter names.
- **chunk-0159.md:4** — `src/internal/handler/v1/admin_observability_handler.go:75-93`, `src/internal/database/observability/vehicle_cost_repo.go:44-102`, `src/internal/handler/v1/admin_observability_handler.go:149-155`: The vehicle-cost window is not validated consistently: an invalid `since` is silently converted to the service's 30-day default when `until` is absent, and a valid old `since` with no `until` bypasses the advertised 90-day maximum. When `until` exists, both bounds are checked; the two code paths therefore either broaden an invalid filter unexpectedly or permit an unbounded SQL range older than the intended cost/ingest window. The route's test contract rejects increasing windows over 90 days, but only tests ranges that include `until`; repository SQL accepts arbitrary `since..time.Now()` and has no independent 90-day guard. A bookmark or typo such as `since=garbage` or a six-year-old date changes query scope and cost without returning a validation error.
- **chunk-0159.md:5** — `src/internal/handler/v1/admin_audit_handler.go:49-64`, `src/internal/database/audit/log_query_repo.go:88-110`, `src/internal/handler/v1/admin_observability_handler.go:79-91`: Optional audit and vehicle-cost dates are not parsed to the same strict contract. `AdminAuditHandler` silently ignores malformed `since`, `until`, and `entity_id` values, causing the read to proceed with a broader or weaker filter rather than a `400`; observability similarly ignores malformed `since` when no `until` is supplied. Invalid user/filter input is thus indistinguishable from an omitted filter at the transport boundary, and can return audit/history records outside the operator's requested window. The repository code does apply correct SQL bounds once nonzero/valid values reach it, but the handlers have no strict parse step before service calls. Neighboring bounded analysis/date helpers return validation errors and the observability route already rejects malformed/unordered bounds when `until` is present, demonstrating the missing invariant.
- **chunk-0160.md:3** — `src/internal/handler/v1/trip_handler.go:28-39`, `src/internal/handler/v1/charging_handler.go:28-42`, `src/web/src/api/hooks/useTrips.ts:24-40`, `src/web/src/api/hooks/useCharging.ts:64`, `src/web/src/api/hooks/useTrips.test.tsx:149-157`, `src/web/src/api/hooks/useCharging.test.tsx:247`: `GET /trips` and `GET /charging-sessions` are not honoring the canonical query contracts used by their hooks. The trip handler accepts only `vehicleId`, while both frontend APIs send `vehicle_id`; thus a normal trip request that follows the app hook is rejected as 400. The charging handler accepts both spellings, but neither list handler reads or forwards `start`/`end` bounds: both call vehicle-only repository services and return the entire history even though the frontend range controls send them. This bypasses server-side workspace-range filtering, can return extra rows and can invalidate chart/list trust semantics. The sharing rules and test contracts explicitly require snake_case `vehicle_id` (`useTrips`/`useCharging` tests pin it), and the repository ports already expose `ListByDateRange`; the handlers/services instead implement one vehicle-only path. In contrast, `src/internal/handler/v1/charging_handler.go` already demonstrates backward-compatible vehicle parameter handling but still omits range forwarding.
- **chunk-0160.md:4** — `src/internal/handler/v1/export_handler.go:29-78`, `src/web/src/api/hooks/useAdmin.ts:348-355`, `src/web/src/api/hooks/useAdmin.test.tsx:821-833`, `src/internal/domain/export/types.go:8-24`, `src/internal/app/exportsvc/service.go:42-50`: The new export endpoint's request/domain contract is incomplete and not aligned with the frontend caller. `useCreateExport` POSTs `{type, format, vehicleId}`, but `createExportRequest` has no `type` field and `ExportJob` has no export-type field; it silently discards the caller's export kind instead of enqueueing the requested drive/charging/analytics/account export. The handler also accepts zero-valued `DateFrom`/`DateTo` when omitted and never validates `format`, `type`, vehicle scope, or date ordering, so malformed jobs can be persisted with `time.Time{}` bounds and no information on what to generate. Frontend export tests explicitly pin the camelCase body and request type/format, while the backend DTO and aggregate only retain format, vehicle, and dates. This is a transport/domain contract split, not ordinary naming variety: the export type drives worker selection and cannot be reconstructed after being dropped.
- **chunk-0160.md:5** — `src/internal/handler/v1/user_handler.go:11-57`, `src/web/src/api/hooks/useUser.ts:29-39`, `src/web/src/api/hooks/useUser.test.tsx:198-213`, `src/internal/port/repository/user.go:9-14`, `src/internal/adapter/postgres/user_repository.go:33-40`: `PUT /users/me` documents and decodes `displayName`, but the handler explicitly does not persist it; it returns only `userId` and `email`, omitting the submitted display name and existing authenticated-user user fields. The frontend mutation treats this response as authoritative and primes the TanStack `users/me` cache, so an accepted profile update appears successful while the backend never changes and every subsequent GET may return stale/old identity. A `UserRepository.Save` and complete user domain persistence already exist in the repository layer, but this handler has no user repository dependency and instead contains an explicit unwired stub. The frontend test requires the submitted name to reach a valid success response/cache update, exposing the behavioral mismatch rather than just a duplicate implementation.
- **chunk-0163.md:5** — `src/internal/integrations/homeassistant/catalog.go:16-137`, `src/internal/mqtt/mqtt.go:136-163`, `src/internal/tesla/types.go:19-55`: The Home Assistant catalog declares fixed metric units (`km`, `km/h`, `°C`) for vehicle state published by `PublishVehicleData`, but the MQTT payload exposes raw values from `tesla.VehicleDataResponse` without a conversion or unit marker. Tesla API values can follow the vehicle/user unit preference (for example miles/imperial temperatures), so HA will label those values incorrectly. The mismatch affects battery range, speed, odometer, and both temperature entities, producing materially wrong dashboards and automations. The same raw state fields are published in `PublishVehicleData`; the HA catalog independently assigns metric units. It has no unit conversion contract or unit-preference lookup, so the two implementations can drift silently.
- **chunk-0163.md:6** — `src/internal/integrations/homeassistant/discovery.go:116-123,160-169,189-192`, `src/internal/integrations/homeassistant/discovery_test.go:92-96`: `PublishVehicle` rejects an empty VIN, but `UnpublishVehicle` performs no equivalent validation and publishes under an unvalidated/lowercased VIN. `PublishVehicle` also builds `stateBase` from the original VIN without trimming whitespace, while `PrefixFor` trims and lowercase-normalizes it, so whitespace-bearing VINs create discovery paths and state topics that do not match. Publish and unpublish are the two sides of the entity lifecycle but implement different VIN contracts; tests cover only empty VIN on publish. An invalid or whitespace VIN can leave stale discovery entities that point at the wrong state topic, and unpublish can fail to retract the topics created by publish.
- **chunk-0163.md:7** — `src/internal/integrations/nhtsa/client.go:420-429`, `src/internal/domain/vehicle/validation.go:11-18,65-76`, `src/internal/integrations/nhtsa/client_test.go`: The NHTSA VIN validator promises only allowed characters/length and does not validate the mandatory ISO VIN check digit at position 9. Because the decode and cache paths accept that same syntactic form before making an upstream request, an invalid 17-character VIN is sent/cached as a vehicle identity. This repeats the incomplete domain VIN validator in two integration boundaries; the existing tests exercise length/allowed characters but not mismatched check digits, so cache, decode, recall, and downstream safety applicability can be keyed by a malformed VIN.
- **chunk-0164.md:3** — `src/internal/jobs/digests/weekly.go:88-111`, `src/internal/jobs/digests/yir.go:90-113`, `src/internal/jobs/embeddings/ttl.go:64-75`, `src/internal/jobs/indexers/charge_curve.go:125-159`, `src/internal/jobs/indexers/docs.go:122-145`, `src/internal/jobs/indexers/drive.go:124-154`, `src/internal/jobs/indexers/idle_drain.go:125-155`, `src/internal/jobs/indexers/log_trace.go:123-153`, `src/internal/jobs/indexers/route.go:89-117`, `src/internal/jobs/indexers/update_notes.go:120-148`, `src/internal/jobs/triage/alert_inbox.go:124-155`, `src/internal/jobs/triage/feedback.go:88-118`, `src/internal/database/settings/repo.go:570-587`: Every job accepts any non-empty, non-`off` `ai_mode` as enabled. The settings repository has no enum validation: it returns the stored value unchanged, and these jobs gate only on exact `off`. A typo, corrupted setting, future/unknown mode, or empty-value drift (the repository normally normalizes empty to `off`, but a fake or alternate reader need not) can therefore turn a job into its positive path and perform fan-out/deletes. This is fail-open even though the surrounding tests and comments claim only `off`/errors are fail-closed. The same weak predicate is repeated across all background-job gates in this chunk, while their tests use `"cloud"`/`"local"` as on modes but do not test unknown values; settings is the authoritative stored-mode source and currently guarantees only string/default-off behavior, not a closed mode set.
- **chunk-0164.md:5** — `src/internal/jobs/embeddings/ttl.go:52-95`, `src/internal/jobs/embeddings/ttl_test.go:80-138`: The TTL path is not atomic across the two embeddings tables. If deleting from `embeddings_768` succeeds and deleting from `embeddings_1536` fails, `RunTTL` returns `res` containing the 768 deletion plus an error; the first deletion remains committed while callers cannot retry only the failed table without one-sided semantics. Conversely, the live test validates only the 768 table and no transaction/rollback or retry contract covers cross-table consistency. The implementation performs two independent `db.Pool.Exec` calls in one loop; the test exercises success of only one table and does not cover a partial SQL failure between the two physical stores.
- **chunk-0166.md:6** — `src/internal/metrics/business.go:141-184`, `src/internal/signal/live_store.go:136-193`, `src/internal/signal/store.go:100`: `TelemetryLagSeconds` is never refreshed in production: repository-wide search finds no call to `StartTelemetryLagRefresher`, and no startup wiring calls `refreshTelemetryLag` or `RecordSignalReceived` schedules. `RecordSignalReceived` is called by live-store updates, but without the missing goroutine the gauge remains on its initial value (and can never describe elapsed time). Meanwhile `VehicleLastSeen` is a separate gauge reset to zero on `Store.Update`, so it does not provide the same rolling-lag computation. Operators can see a permanently stale or uninitialized `teslasync_telemetry_lag_seconds` series despite live telemetry arriving; the unit-tested helper passes in isolation while the production metric has no time-driving update path.
- **chunk-0167.md:5** — `src/internal/ml/anomaly/baseline.go:220-228`, `src/internal/ml/anomaly/baseline.go:271-328`, `src/internal/ml/anomaly/safe_ranges.go:38-59`: The learned anomaly trainer does not copy the defensive filtering used by the range/charging trainers: it accepts NaN, +/-Inf, and out-of-domain observation values without filtering. `Train` passes the source slice straight to `learned`, whose sort/mean/stddev/percentile calculations can then emit NaN or non-finite envelopes. Separately, the four TPMS safe-range bounds are expressed in bar here while the observed signal-log values and API detector are in Pa, so clamping/learned bounds use incompatible magnitudes. The range and charging trainer copies filter invalid values (`src/internal/ml/range/trainer.go:274-280`, `src/internal/ml/chargingcurves/clustering.go:424-440` and related helpers), so anomaly behavior diverges under corrupt/nullable telemetry. The repository already has a parity test that converts ML TPMS bar bounds to Pa to compare maps, but the trainer itself still exposes bar fallback/learned bounds rather than canonical SI Pa.
- **chunk-0167.md:7** — `src/internal/ml/chargingcurves/clustering.go:192-207`, `src/internal/ml/chargingcurves/clustering.go:371-399`, `src/internal/ml/chargingcurves/clustering.go:407-439`, `src/internal/ml/chargingcurves/clustering.go:55-60`: `LearnedCluster` adds a `duration_min_mean` JSON field and charges the trainer with converting `DurationMinutes()` (minutes), while the model migration and active SI canonical rules require duration values to remain SI-seconds with unit conversion only at the display boundary. The trainer's `duration_mean` contract therefore introduces a legacy unit name into the API DTO instead of exposing canonical seconds. A frontend/API consumer could interpret or convert this field differently from the other canonical SI fields; it also makes the per-cluster envelope inconsistent with the SI canonical charging model. The same session DTO exposes an SI-canonical `EndedAt`/`StartedAt` pair, so the trainer should derive an unambiguous duration in seconds or leave this metric out rather than inventing a `_min` transport field.
- **chunk-0167.md:8** — `src/internal/ml/chargingcurves/clustering.go:371-399`, `src/internal/ml/chargingcurves/clustering.go:407-439`: The charging trainer treats `SessionCount` as the count of all sessions in a cluster, but computes peak, average power, energy, duration, delta-SOC, and ramp statistics only from whichever pointers pass the `pluckFloat`/`rampShapes` filters. It also sets learned source when the cluster has at least three sessions, even if several required measurements are nil or invalid; for example a cluster can report `SourceLearned`/`SessionCount=3` while its mean is based on zero or only one valid pointer. The resulting JSON claims a learned cluster with sparse underlying numeric coverage, and no exported per-statistic sample count distinguishes which mean/stddev has enough data. `SampleCount` is not sufficient to prove statistical validity; nil/negative/out-of-domain values and one-sided partial measurements can silently reduce confidence without changing the source label.
- **chunk-0168.md:5** — `src/internal/models/automation_step_trigger.go:31-36`, `src/internal/database/automation/step_child_repo_persistence.go:161-172`, `src/internal/database/automation/step_child_repo_query.go:116-128`, `src/internal/api/automation/handler.go:1013-1025`, `src/internal/automation/trigger/geofence.go:208-216`: `AutomationStepTriggerGeofence.DwellMinutes` is exposed and accepted in the API, but persistence never writes it and hydration reads no column for it, so every dwell value reloads as zero. The API conversion also builds a wire DTO from this model field, while the repository's insert and read SQL omit it entirely. The same geofence trigger object is consumed at `DwellMinutes` by runtime dwell handling and at the API boundary, but the DB copy loses the value. Event dwell automations can therefore be decoded as enter/other behavior or evaluated without a valid dwell threshold, depending on the separate runtime checks.
- **chunk-0168.md:6** — `src/internal/models/backup/backup.go:128-147`, `src/internal/models/backup/backup.go:83-99`, `src/internal/api/backup/restore_handler.go:132-145`, `src/internal/models/backup/backup_test.go:111-145`: `BackupConfig.Validate` does not enforce the documented maximum retention of 100, while both `Normalize` and the restore handler clamp `MaxRetention` to 100 and define the model maximum as 100. Inputs with large retention values pass model validation and can remain above the supported bound unless a separate coercion step happens to run. `Normalize` and `clampConfigBounds` agree on the upper bound, but `Validate` disagrees; current tests cover values below/negative but not >100. This allows inconsistent create/update behavior between callers and drift in the canonical bounds contract.
- **chunk-0168.md:7** — `src/internal/models/automation.go:76-94`, `src/internal/automation/engine.go:233-238`, `src/internal/automation/condition/conflict.go:83`, `src/internal/database/automation/repo_mutation.go:134-141`: `AutomationFull.AutoDisabled()` is documented as run-history-based derivation, but its implementation always returns false. Engine execution, conflict detection, and safety/metadata paths therefore cannot distinguish an automatically disabled automation, while the repository now no-ops its persisted disable setter. A persisted-column replacement that is not implemented leaves `Enabled=true` and `AutoDisabled=false` for every automation, so safety disable and loop/failure workflows can still permit later execution and conflict checks treat unsafe automations as eligible.
- **chunk-0170.md:5** — `src/internal/models/drive/drive.go:30-128`, `src/internal/database/drive/repo.go:47-52,154-161`, `src/internal/database/drive/repair_quarantine.go:130-135,224-230`, `src/internal/api/telemetry/telemetry_sessions_drive_tracking.go:1228-1240`: The canonical post-migration drive schema has peak traction power (`peak_power_w`), but the `drivemodel.Drive` transport/persistence struct has no `PeakPowerW` field and the canonical drive SELECT/complete/repository paths do not expose it: `drive.go` only carries `AvgPowerW`, and `repo.go` selects/writes no `peak_power_w`. A separate repair snapshot knows the column exists, while normal reads and completion never populate or project it, so peak-power data is lost from the normal API contract despite the SI drive schema, migration comments, and telemetry completion code tracking a maximum. `src/internal/database/drive/repair_quarantine.go` independently snapshots/restores `peak_power_w`, proving that column is canonical on disk, whereas `Drive` and `driveColumns` omit it. Consumers therefore cannot read one drive aggregate that the database and recovery path preserve, and any API response or restore/display contract can silently disagree about available drive fields.
- **chunk-0171.md:5** — `src/internal/models/settings/endpoint_controls.go:88-146`, `src/internal/models/settings/settings.go:147-162`, `src/internal/tesla/endpoint_controls.go:24-43`: `LegacyPollingConfig.AllowsFleetOperation(..., automatic=true)` bypasses the actual automatic-polling policy: it checks only `EnabledVehicleDataEndpoints()` and therefore ignores `AutoPollingEnabled`, `FleetEndpoints`, and `AutoEndpoints`. As a result, a caller using this method may report automatic polling allowed when the canonical `PollsEndpoint`/Tesla client path rejects it (master switch off, route disabled, or automatic preference false). The method also does not reject duplicate sub-endpoints the way the Tesla path's `fleetEndpointKeys` rejects repeated names. The canonical automatic policy is implemented by `PollsEndpoint` and `EnabledAutoVehicleDataEndpoints` in `src/internal/models/settings/settings.go`, and enforced against `/api/1/vehicles/{vin}/vehicle_data` by `src/internal/tesla/endpoint_controls.go` via `fleetEndpointKeys`. The duplicate implementation is currently out of sync even though it appears to be the same operation gate.
- **chunk-0171.md:6** — `src/internal/notification/quiet_hours.go:194-208`, `src/internal/database/quiethours/repo.go:281-293`, `src/internal/ai/tools/schedule/quiet_hours.go:627-642`: The notification runtime `minutesOfDay` parser accepts any string with at least five characters, not exactly an `HH:MM` value: `"12:00junk"` passes because the parser examines only characters 0-4; `"99:99junk"` is also accepted and yields 5979 minutes. The database validator and AI validator both require `len(s) == 5`, so an unsanitized or legacy model value can be treated as active/schedule-end behavior by the runtime even though the canonical API/tool rejection contract says it is malformed. `IsWindowActiveAt` and `NextWindowEndAt` both use `minutesOfDay`; the database copy uses `validHHMM` at `src/internal/database/quiethours/repo.go` and the AI copy at `src/internal/ai/tools/schedule/quiet_hours.go`. Those copies pin the exact five-character contract, making this runtime-only parser an edge-case divergence that can prevent a window from failing closed.
- **chunk-0172.md:5** — `src/internal/models/telemetry/position.go:9-22`, `src/internal/models/telemetry/position_test.go:4-188`, `src/internal/database/position/repo.go:108-121,178-191,223-230`: The chunk still exposes a legacy source-unit position contract (`SpeedMph`) while the database and repository read side are SI-canonical (`speed_mps`, `altitude_m`). The model comment/test suite and repository boundary explicitly preserve this dual shape. A future caller can therefore mistake the MPH-shaped model for canonical SI, and the model/repository adaptation is one of the two boundaries where unit drift can silently change map or telemetry values. A second copy of the same transitional contract appears in `src/internal/worker/worker_jobs.go:174-182`, where fleet data constructs the legacy-shaped `Position`; the write repository performs the MPH→m/s conversion. The code package is already mixed: the DB is SI, but the model is not, so Phase-48 migration/canonical naming can diverge at the read and write edges.
- **chunk-0172.md:6** — `src/internal/models/tesla/tesla.go:87-118`, `src/internal/database/tesla/charging_session_repo.go:25-108,117-158`, `src/internal/api/teslachargesess/handler.go:199-230`: This chunk retains legacy unit-bearing fields (`EnergyAddedKWh`, `PeakPowerKW`, `MaxChargeRateKW`, `AvgCostPerKWh`) as canonical persisted/API model fields and repositories read/write legacy-named database columns. The model contract mixes vendor billing values with canonical SI aggregation: summary SQL converts `SUM(energy_added_kwh) * 1000.0` into watt-hours while leaving the individual session values in kWh/kW. This is the same blocked migration pattern as legacy unit suffixes, and UI/AI consumers can lose which unit a float represents. The model has a second, nearby summary shape in `TeslaChargingHistorySummary` at the same file lines 79-84 and repository consumers use both summary types in `src/internal/database/tesla/charging_history_repo.go:131-143`; after Phase-48, these names and conversions need to land together rather than retaining kWh/kW and Wh/cost shapes independently.
- **chunk-0174.md:6** — `src/internal/mqtt/dlq_inspector.go:293-318`: `Replay` does not check the supplied context until after it calls `Publish`. A replay request whose context is already canceled therefore still performs the broker publish and only then returns `context.Canceled`; no broker-side precondition can undo the side effect. `dlq_inspector_test.go:278-296` explicitly tests cancellation but only checks the returned error, not whether `Publish` was called, so the current side-effect bug is not covered. The HTTP caller therefore may observe cancellation while an unintended replay has already occurred.
- **chunk-0175.md:5** — `src/internal/notification/computed/compare.go:72-118`, `src/internal/notification/computed/evaluator.go:96-119`, `src/internal/notification/computed/evaluator.go:151-174`, `src/internal/notification/computed/evaluator_test.go:62-103`: Calendar percent-change windows are asymmetric: `WindowBounds("day"/"week"/"month")` ends at the current instant, but `PreviousWindowBounds` returns a complete preceding calendar day/week/month. `%_change_*` therefore compares an elapsed fraction of the current day/week/month with the entire previous period (and the existing tests cover only rolling or previous-window bounds, not this partial-vs-complete comparison). This can fire or suppress alerts based on time of day rather than equal-length history. Rolling windows remain equal-length because they derive the duration in the rolling branch. The bug is exercised by both `Evaluate` and `Preview` through the same `PreviousWindowBounds`/`WindowBounds` pair, so any alert-rule kind and preview path share the false comparison; no test pins the current period to equal elapsed duration or the intended full-period semantics. A second copy of the calendar-bound design is not needed—the risk follows from the two independently implemented current/previous boundary rules.
- **chunk-0176.md:6** — `src/internal/notifier/webhook.go:205-209`, `src/internal/notifier/webhook.go:212-225`, `src/internal/notifier/webhook_test.go:101-125`, `src/internal/notifier/webhook_test.go:145-153`: `Send` sets the canonical HMAC signature header before merging `Options.Headers`, so a caller can replace `X-TeslaSync-Signature` with an arbitrary value. This violates the option contract: `Result.Signature` reports the digest computed from `Secret` while the actual HTTP request carries a different or unsigned header when the caller supplies that header name. It can also mask a signed request as unsigned. Guard the reserved signing header (and preferably remove caller attempts to overwrite signing-sensitive headers) or merge custom headers first and set the canonical signature last; add a test proving a `Headers` entry cannot alter the signed result/header. The existing custom-header test only checks ordinary headers and a content-type override, and the no-secret/with-secret send tests do not cover reserved-header collisions. A mismatched receiver validation and an unverified caller-supplied signature are the concrete edge-case risks.
- **chunk-0177.md:5** — `src/internal/ocpp/dispatcher.go:94-104`, `src/internal/database/ocpp/store.go:60-74`: Duplicate or retried `StopTransaction` frames have inconsistent semantics between the two `SessionStore` implementations. `MemorySessionStore.StopSession` finds any transaction in the map and unconditionally overwrites `EndedAt`, `EndMeterWh`, and `StopReason`, even if that session is already closed; the Postgres store closes only rows still having `ended_at IS NULL` and returns `unknown transaction` when the same stop is retried. It therefore returns success from memory but protocol-level `InternalError` from durable storage after the first successful stop, and can replace the original stop timestamp, meter reading, or reason if a duplicate frame arrives. The memory implementation in `src/internal/ocpp/dispatcher.go` and the PostgreSQL implementation in `src/internal/database/ocpp/store.go` are alternate implementations of the same port; because `cmd/ocpp-server` selects between them (`src/cmd/ocpp-server/main.go`), identical charger behavior can depend on the deployment backend. Tests exercise only the first stop and do not cover repeated stop frames.
- **chunk-0178.md:5** — `src/internal/ops/migrationgate.go:506-518`; `src/internal/ops/migrationgate_test.go:306-335`: `VerifyMigrationGate` dereferences `o.Spec.Target.DeletionPolicy` whenever an ExternalSecret hook does not use `creationPolicy: Orphan`. `o.Spec.Target` is a pointer and may be nil; malformed or incomplete test/render YAML then panics instead of returning a finding. The nearby unset-creation-policy case only removes `creationPolicy`, leaving a non-nil `spec.target`, so it does not cover the missing-target nil case.
- **chunk-0179.md:5** — `src/internal/ops/rollback.go:326-389`: `Evaluate` treats a present sample with a zero `ObservedAt` as an observed, usable measurement: the stale-age branch explicitly checks `!sample.ObservedAt.IsZero()` and therefore skips the unknown/staleness guard, then may mark a required signal as `proceed` when its value is healthy or only warn/hold as applicable. This contradicts the evaluator contract that missing/stale required telemetry must never proceed; a broken timestamp source would be counted as evidence. The precedence comment at lines 321-325 and the missing/stale-required rules at lines 360-369 define zero timestamps as missing/unusable, but the implementation silently accepts them. Add an explicit zero-time case that applies `MissingRequiredVerdict` for required signals (and clearly reports unknown for optional signals).
- **chunk-0179.md:6** — `src/internal/ops/supplychain.go:183-204`: The root-level workflow `env` image scanner has a pre-filter of `strings.Contains(v.Value, "/")`, so bare image references such as `alpine:3.20` or `nginx:1.27` never reach `looksLikeImageRef` and are not governed by the digest-pinning rule. Job-level image collection does call `looksLikeImageRef` directly, so this creates a location-dependent blind spot: a mutable root `env` image with only a tag/registry syntax can change what a workflow executes. `collectImageRefs` already governs job/container/service/step image inputs correctly by calling `looksLikeImageRef`; the root `env` branch alone narrows the same contract and should remove the slash-only pre-filter, using the shared image classifier instead.
- **chunk-0180.md:5** — `src/internal/outbox/dispatcher.go:224-238,329-368`: `pollAndPublish` discards every error returned by `markPublished`, `markRetry`, and `markFailed`, and still increments `published`, `retried`, or `failed` and returns no error. A successful broker publish can leave the row permanently `in_flight` if the database status update fails, while RunOnce reports it published; a failed broker publish can leave the row claimed if the retry/failed update fails, while RunOnce reports it retried/failed. The only database failure path the dispatcher returns is claim failure. The `claim` path handles `rows.Err()` and returns errors, but mark-transition failures are silently converted into positive counters. Subsequent stale-leases may re-dispatch rows that this tick incorrectly reported terminal or retried.
- **chunk-0180.md:6** — `src/internal/outbox/dispatcher.go:220-238,272-295,329-368`, `src/migrations/000213_events_outbox.up.sql:35-38,52-60`: Claim rows are leased with `lease_holder`, but `markPublished`, `markRetry`, and `markFailed` update only by row `id` and do not verify the lease holder/status. If A's lease expires, B claims and publishes the same row, then A completes and marks B's newly claimed row published/retried/failed using stale `row.Attempts` or old retry error. Similarly, the old publisher can overwrite a fresh in-flight row with its terminal state. The migration explicitly defines the lease contract (`another dispatcher will steal the row` after expiry), while the marking SQL has no ownership predicate; claim and mark therefore do not form a safe compare-and-transition lease protocol across pods.
- **chunk-0180.md:7** — `src/internal/outbox/dispatcher.go:248-269`: `tickSweep` never checks `rows.Err()` after the stale-lease scan and silently ignores `rows.Scan` failures. A mid-stream query/iteration error can stop the sweep while `reaped` remains partial or zero without a returned error or log, leaving stale rows in `in_flight`; the caller cannot know that all expired leases were recovered. `claim` in the same file explicitly returns `rows.Err()` after iteration, showing the established row-stream contract is applied inconsistently. The sweep loop's direct CTE plus partial counting is likewise incomplete without terminal row-error handling.
- **chunk-0180.md:8** — `src/internal/outbox/mqtt_publisher.go:93-117`: `PublishOutbox` advertises that it respects the caller context, but it only reads the context deadline to shorten `WaitTimeout`; it does not check `ctx.Err()` before or after publishing and a cancellation without a deadline may still wait the full MQTT timeout (default 5s). It also waits after a deadline is already exceeded unless a remaining positive deadline fits in `wait`; those cases are not enough to guarantee the documented context behavior. The dispatcher stops processing other claimed rows when its context is canceled only after each publish helper returns, so a canceled dispatcher tick can spend an extra timeout interval inside this adapter instead of returning promptly.
- **chunk-0183.md:5** — `src/internal/platform/httputil/logging.go:38,61-70,122-138`; `src/internal/api/api_call_log_middleware.go:259-279,319-365`; `src/internal/platform/httputil/sink.go:66-74`: Outbound diagnostic capture does not apply the repository's canonical sensitive-data redaction policy: it redacts only a small substring-based URL-key set and stores raw opted-in request/response bodies. Secrets or private identifiers under JSON keys or URLs such as `email`, `vin`, `latitude`, `authorization`, or nested fields can therefore be persisted in `api_call_logs`, despite the package comments promising outbound/body redaction parity. `api_call_log_middleware.go` is the second copy and uses `redactKeyPattern` for URL/header/body redaction, including recursive JSON handling; `sink.go`'s contract/documentation points to the same expected behavior but `logging.go` bypasses it. The difference can leak credentials or PII whenever outbound body capture is enabled.
- **chunk-0183.md:6** — `src/internal/platform/httputil/retry.go:99-132`; `src/internal/platform/httputil/retry.go:37-56`; `src/internal/platform/httputil/client.go:43-49`; `src/internal/adapter/tesla/client.go:33-41`: `RetryableTransport` retries the same `http.Request` without restoring `req.Body` via `req.GetBody`, so POST/PUT retry attempts commonly send an empty or partially consumed body. It also uses unconditional `time.Sleep(sleepDur)` and is not canceled by `req.Context()` during backoff; `base.RoundTrip` may return a canceled request, but the transport can still sleep before ending the retry chain.
