The same package's function-style `Retry` implementation checks `ctx.Err()` and uses a context-aware wait at lines 37-56; transport retry copies that behavior but omits both request-body reset and context cancellation during the sleep. The two retry paths therefore have incompatible cancellation and request semantics, and callers enabling `RetryableTransport` can submit duplicate HTTP operations with missing payloads.
- **chunk-0188.md:5** — `src/internal/resilience/doc.go:3-7`, `src/internal/resilience/resilience.go:99-106`, `src/internal/resilience/resilience_test.go:54-69,72-87`, `src/internal/resilience/health_test.go:23-30`: The package documentation states that a component becomes degraded after 2 consecutive failures and unhealthy after 5, but the implementation uses degraded at 3 and unhealthy at 10. The tests in `resilience_test.go` and `health_test.go` assert the implementation thresholds (3 degraded, 10 unhealthy), so callers and future code may rely on the stale 2/5 contract and misreport health degradation. `HealthMonitor.RecordFailure` and the package docs are parallel copies of the health-threshold contract; keeping one copy at stale thresholds risks off-by-one health-state or alert decisions. The implementation/config tests establish 3/10 as the executable behavior, but `doc.go` remains authoritative-looking package documentation.
- **chunk-0191.md:5** — `src/internal/science/stats.go:155-171`, `src/internal/science/weather.go:13-24`, `src/internal/app/sciencesvc/weather.go:115-116`: `Pearson` accepts `NaN` or infinite inputs and can return a `NaN`/infinite correlation with `ok=true`; unlike `LinReg`, it has no finite-value guard. `WeatherCorrelations` passes archive-derived residual arrays directly to `Pearson`, so the app can record a non-finite correlation as successful instead of unknown, and the notebook receives it as `Parameters`. `LinReg` at `src/internal/science/stats.go:32-55` validates both argument vectors and rejects `NaN`/`Inf`, showing the same statistical package has inconsistent finite-value handling. The caller chain is `WeatherCorrelations` → `Pearson` → `rep.DensityR/WindR` in `src/internal/app/sciencesvc/weather.go`.
- **chunk-0192.md:5** — `internal/service/current_state.go:122-170`, `internal/service/current_state.go:403` (`ResolveCurrentStateWith` / assembler call); `internal/service/current_state_bulk.go:170-177`, `internal/service/current_state_bulk.go:346-357` (`PrefetchCurrentStates` / `durableSnapshot`): The request-level `now` chosen by current-state resolution and used by bulk `StatesAt` is not propagated to the per-vehicle durable fallback; `durableSnapshot` instead calls `SignalStateReader.State(..., time.Now().UTC())`. A caller can supply a different/frozen instant, and the bulk prefetch path can use that instant while the fallback per-vehicle path silently snapshots wall-clock time. The same vehicle can get different point-in-time signal-log fallback values for prefetched versus per-vehicle resolution, violating the stated identical-verdict contract and potentially changing state near a timestamp boundary.
- **chunk-0193.md:5** — `src/internal/signal/live_state_reader.go:112-125`, `src/internal/signal/live_state_reader_test.go:79-103,133-151`: `LiveSignal` treats a non-nil `*Value` as an observed live signal even when `Value.Raw` is nil. `LiveState` explicitly skips nil values before falling back to `StateReader`; `LiveSignal` does not, so a live-layer entry such a stub/store can produce with a nil `Raw` (for example a malformed/decoded envelope) suppresses the signal_log fallback and returns `(nil, nil)`, despite the live value not being observable. The fallback path in the same reader checks absence only via `v != nil`; the sibling `LiveState` path checks both the `*Value` and `Raw`, so the two read methods disagree on the same fallback contract.
- **chunk-0193.md:6** — `src/internal/signal/noop_live_store.go:18-34`, `src/internal/signal/live_store_bulk.go:111-122`, `src/internal/signal/live_store.go:72-75,105-181`: The no-op implementations of the live-store interfaces do not apply the same context, vehicle ID, or name validation as `HybridLiveSignalStore`. In particular, `Update`, `GetSignal`, `GetAll`, `Update...`, and `GetAllBulk` accept nil contexts and invalid IDs (and `GetAllBulk` silently omits invalid IDs), while the hybrid implementation rejects nil contexts, non-positive IDs, nil batches, and blank names via package sentinels. This makes substituting the documented no-op store change observable error behavior and can mask invalid caller state or miswiring. The hybrid boundary is the second copy of the contract: `validateLiveSignalContext`, `validateLiveSignalVehicleID`, `validateLiveSignalStoreMode`, and the live-bulk validation loops establish the expected validation semantics that the no-op store bypasses.
- **chunk-0193.md:7** — `src/internal/signal/coerce.go:38-105`, `src/internal/signal/coerce_test.go:89-99`, `src/internal/signal/redis_cache.go:808-830`: `Float64` advertises and, for strings/`json.Number`, enforces rejection of non-finite numbers, but primitive `float64(+Inf/NaN)` inputs bypass all validation because they are handled before numeric validation. The implementation therefore returns `(NaN/Inf, true)` for raw primitive floats, while decoded legacy strings return the non-numeric sentinel path and `redis_cache.decodeLegacySignalValue` also has an independent conversion path. Callers cannot distinguish a valid numeric zero from a raw non-finite signal, and the helper contract is inconsistent across input representations. The second copies are `Float64Value`/`Store.GetFloat` consuming the helper without extra checks, and Redis legacy decoding (`strconv.ParseFloat` plus string type handling) can produce primitive non-finite values that this helper then accepts.
- **chunk-0193.md:8** — `src/internal/signal/agreement/agreement.go:291-294`, `src/internal/signal/agreement/agreement.go:273-281`: `absDuration` negates a negative duration without guarding `time.Duration`'s minimum value. A `Sub`-derived minimum-duration value would make `absDuration` remain negative instead of absolute; the nearest-match candidate delta ranking could then select the wrong candidate and count a disagreement or agreement incorrectly when timestamps are far enough apart to saturate time arithmetic. `matchNearest` is the second copy of the comparison logic and applies `absDuration` to both the initial delta and every bounded candidate delta; an overflowed negative absolute value breaks the ordering invariant for those candidate comparisons.
- **chunk-0194.md:3** — `src/internal/signal/signaltest/state_reader.go:267-284` (`cloneTimelineRows`); `src/internal/signal/state_reader.go:48-60` (`TimelineRow`); `src/internal/signal/pivot.go:123-207` (`forwardFold` / `timelineFolder.flush`): `cloneTimelineRows` claims to deep-copy rows but copies only `Timestamp` and a top-level `Fields` map. It omits `ReceivedAt` and `ObservedAt`, and nested map/slice `SignalValue` objects remain aliased through the shallow `Fields` copy. A fake configured with `SetTimeline` therefore cannot faithfully preserve the real reader's provenance contract, and caller mutations can also mutate stored compound values. `TimelineRow` carries `ReceivedAt` and `ObservedAt`, and `forwardFold` populates them per row; the fake loses both fields despite claiming a defensive copy.
- **chunk-0195.md:3** — `src/internal/signal/store.go:95`, `src/internal/signal/store.go:381`: `UpdateValues` and `HydrateValues` call `cloneSignalValue`, but no definition of that symbol appears in either manifest source file, so these paths do not compile. Both live-update and hydration paths share the same missing helper; both storage paths risk the same build failure.
- **chunk-0196.md:5** — `src/internal/slo/catalog.go:49-56,263`; `src/internal/slo/tracker.go:80-83,195-205`: `FastBurnSeverity` is parsed and validated as an SLO-level override (`ticket` for the continuity SLO), but it is never applied to runtime tier evaluation. `Tiers()` always marks FastBurn `page`, and `snapshotOne` derives `highest_severity` and the returned tier severity from that static value instead of the catalog field, so a ticket-only continuity SLO is reported and surfaced as a page alert. The catalog parser is the first copy of the severity contract, and the tracker has a separate hard-coded severity copy (`page`/`ticket`); behavior differs because the catalog override is dropped.
- **chunk-0198.md:5** — `src/internal/tesla/auth.go:143-151`: `tokenRequest` always replaces the stored refresh token with `TokenResponse.RefreshToken`. OAuth refresh responses commonly omit `refresh_token`, in which case the decoded field is empty and this call overwrites a valid existing refresh token, causing every later refresh attempt to fail with `no refresh token available`; the same decoded-empty value can also be persisted by callers. The refresh branch in `RefreshTokens` starts with the old token but `tokenRequest` has no merge-preservation rule for an omitted response field.
- **chunk-0198.md:6** — `src/internal/tesla/client.go:327-353`, `src/internal/tesla/client_auth.go:256-284`: The circuit-breaker executes the request closure repeatedly, but both `doRequest` and `doRequestWithTokenOptions` consume the supplied `io.Reader` once and then keep passing the same now-exhausted reader to `http.NewRequestWithContext` on later breaker attempts. A transient failure/retry can therefore resend a body as EOF or an empty request, unlike an HTTP retry that must replay the original payload. Both methods implement the same read-once closure pattern; a successful first attempt is unchanged, but retransmitted attempts diverge and lose the request body.
- **chunk-0198.md:7** — `src/internal/tesla/budget.go:280-290`: `canReserve` performs signed `int64` additions for the total and background caps without overflow checks. Large corrupted/persisted or repeated snapshot values can overflow to a negative total/background cost; the subsequent `> policy limit` comparisons can then allow a charge that is actually beyond the cap. This mirrors the reserve checks in the memory implementation while the durable repository performs SQL additions and enforces the limits separately, but both implementations share the same unbounded input assumption.
- **chunk-0199.md:3** — `src/internal/tesla/normalize/normalize.go:151-157,183-200`; `src/internal/tesla/normalize/normalize.go:287-305`; `src/internal/tesla/units/units.go:50-92`: The fixed-wire TPMS pressure contract is implemented only in `units.ToSI`, but normalize does not classify TPMS pressure fields as fixed-wire. `toSI` therefore performs a unit-history lookup before conversion and drops all four `TpmsPressure*` values when no pressure history exists. The units package explicitly documents bar as wire-fixed and ignores the selected pressure unit, but normalize defeats that behavior by requiring unit context first. `units.IsFixedBarPressureField`/`fixedBarPressureFields` provides the second copy of the intended fixed-wire contract, while normalize's fixed-wire bypass list omits it (`IsFixedMileDistanceField`, `IsFixedKiloToBaseField`, and `IsFixedMphSpeedField`).
- **chunk-0199.md:4** — `src/internal/tesla/normalize/normalize.go:287-305`; `src/internal/tesla/normalize/normalize.go:151-157,182-198`; `src/internal/tesla/units/units.go:52-92`: `coerceFloat` accepts `float32`/`float64` values without checking for NaN or infinity, despite its comment claiming the numeric contract is valid. NaN and ±Inf can arrive through protobuf float/double values and are then passed into unit conversions; fixed-wire and unit-history paths both leak them into the post-normalization atomic. The resulting non-SI/non-finite values can be persisted or processed by downstream state instead of being rejected as invalid. The units and normalize conversion branches both consume this helper, and fixed conversions can transform finite inputs into overflow/Inf (for example very large kilometers or PSI); no finite-value guard exists in either `coerceFloat` or `units.ToSI`.
- **chunk-0200.md:3** — `src/internal/tesla/normalize/normalize.go:146-183` (`toSI` fixed-wire dispatch); `src/internal/tesla/units/conversions.go:91-113` (`fixedBarPressureFields` / `isFixedBarPressureField`); `src/internal/tesla/normalize/normalize_test.go:216-337`: Fixed-bar TPMS pressure fields bypass unit conversion in `units.ToSI`, so they should also bypass `histRepo.At` and never depend on unit history. `normalize.toSI` only bypasses fixed mile, fixed kWh/kW, and fixed mph fields; for `TpmsPressureFl/Fr/Rl/Rr` it first calls `At` for `KindPressure`, allowing no unit history or a repository error to drop a field whose wire value is documented and tested elsewhere as always bar. Even when `At` succeeds, the wrong setting cannot affect the final value, but conversion still unnecessarily performs a unit lookup and can fail. `units.ToSI` already has the fixed-bar override (`raw * 100000.0`) and pins the four TPMS fields as bar. The normalize fixed-wire branch is missing that same override, unlike its explicit fixed-mile, fixed-kilo, and fixed-mph branches.
- **chunk-0201.md:3** — `src/internal/tesla/router/router.go:217-279` (`Router.Route`); `src/internal/tesla/router/router.go:295-385` (`Router.RouteBatch`): `RouteBatch` does not preserve `Route`'s no-primary-writer behavior: after grouping a field by destination it appends the signal_log dual-write item before verifying that the primary destination has a registered writer, then performs the dual write unconditionally. If a future/refactor-created missing writer is encountered, a batch can write the same atom to signal_log even though the single-item `Route` path returns a `no writer` error before attempting any secondary write. This also violates the documented “same routing and dual-write semantics” contract and can create cold-log-only data for a primary write that was rejected. The two paths in the same file decide dual writes at different stages: `Route` performs the primary write/returns on setup only then dual-writes; `RouteBatch` groups/queues dual items first and has a separate missing-writer branch that only affects primary results.
- **chunk-0201.md:4** — `src/internal/tesla/router/writers/snapshot_base.go:350-367` (`bindSnapshotValue`, `bindSnapshotBatchValue`); `src/internal/tesla/router/writers/tire_pressure_writer.go:178-210` (`WriteBatch`): Batch binding deliberately accepts `time.Time`, while single-item binding rejects it: `bindSnapshotBatchValue` adds a standalone `time.Time` arm even though `bindSnapshotValue` is the authoritative single-write scalar contract and rejects every non-bool/string/numeric type. That makes the same snapshot writer persist an arbitrary `time.Time` in batch mode where a single write would return a type error. It currently masks real routing/type drift for non-timestamp columns and is also relied on by the tire-pressure batch wrapper after coercing epoch fields, so a future tire-pressure change can silently route through a different contract. `Route` calls `bindSnapshotValue`; `RouteBatch` calls `bindSnapshotBatchValue`; these must have matching scalar contracts unless a wrapper explicitly owns the TIMESTAMPTZ column.
- **chunk-0202.md:3** — `src/internal/tesla/router/writers/tire_pressure_writer.go:289-318` (`coerceEpochToTime`): The unix-epoch overflow guard compares against `float64(math.MaxInt64)`, which equals `2^63` at float64 precision. An input of exactly that value passes the guard and then `int64(whole)` can convert out of range (producing a wrapped implementation-defined value, commonly `MinInt64`) instead of returning an invalid-epoch error. An extreme last-seen timestamp can consequently be persisted as a non-attested epoch rather than rejected. The one conversion copy in this chunk has only NaN/Inf/non-negative checks before the float-to-int64 conversion; no second copy is needed because the direct arithmetic boundary itself causes the bug.
- **chunk-0202.md:4** — `src/internal/tesla/router/writers/snapshot_base.go:169,245`; `src/internal/tesla/router/writers/tire_pressure_writer.go:272`; `src/internal/tesla/router/writers/snapshot_base.go:245`: Single-write snapshot paths bind `atom.EmittedAt` directly, while `snapshotWriter.WriteBatch` keys rows by `atom.EmittedAt.UTC().Round(0)` and tire-pressure batch conversion also normalizes through that same batch path. Positions, security-event, and signal-log single writes establish the same UTC/monotonic-free contract. The snapshot and tire-pressure single paths can therefore disagree with batch writes for the same instant when a producer supplies a non-UTC location, monotonic component, or finer-than-database precision, creating duplicate conflict-key representations or a different stored timestamp across transport modes. The sibling writers at `positions_writer.go:285`, `security_event_writer.go:253`, and `signal_log_writer.go:246` are the second-copy behavior and show the intended canonical UTC+Round(0) normalization.
- **chunk-0204.md:3** — `src/internal/tesla_pipeline/software_update_observer.go:132-174` (`extractFirmwareVersionFromAtomics`, `PickFirmwareVersionFromSignals`); `src/internal/service/vehicle_service.go:359-369`, `src/internal/service/vehicle_service.go:463-469`; `src/internal/app/new.go:952-958`: Firmware precedence is inconsistent across copies. The pipeline observer and startup backfill prefer `SoftwareUpdateVersion` and fall back to `Version`, while the vehicle state builder prefers `Version` and only uses `SoftwareUpdateVersion` as a fallback. If one payload/snapshot contains different values, runtime observer/backfill and normal vehicle state APIs can record/present different software versions. The chunk's two pipeline helpers agree with each other, but `vehicle_service.go` contains the opposite precedence and `new.go` intentionally shares the observer/backfill precedence; this is a cross-file defect, not merely formatting duplication.
- **chunk-0205.md:3** — `src/internal/tracing/tracing.go:77-87` (`normalizeEndpoint`): `normalizeEndpoint` treats a `https://` endpoint as insecure whenever `OpenTelemetry.Insecure` is true, because it returns `true` for both HTTPS and configured insecure mode. This bypasses TLS for an explicitly HTTPS OTLP endpoint and only differs between two exporter branches, so TLS transport behavior cannot be chosen independently of endpoint scheme. The exporter setup in `Init` immediately applies the returned flag through both `WithInsecure()` and gRPC insecure credentials; the scheme-producing branch and the exporter-consuming branch disagree when the endpoint is HTTPS and `Insecure` is true.
- **chunk-0207.md:3** — `src/internal/v2h/decider.go:347-368`; `src/internal/v2h/decider_test.go:65-139`: `validate` only checks that `CurrentSoC`, `MinSoC`, and `MaxSoC` are individually in `[0,1]` and that `MinSoC <= MaxSoC`; it does not enforce `MinSoC <= CurrentSoC <= MaxSoC`. A plan starting below the floor will remain below it (`evalDischarge` is disabled but hold/charge logic does not raise a low `CurrentSoC` to `MinSoC`), and a plan starting above the ceiling can remain above it. This violates the advertised hard guardrails and makes the two ceiling/floor tests pass only because their fixture starts inside the allowed range. The implementation contracts in `decider.go:2-31,45-67,348-368` and the package test fixtures in `decider_test.go:65-139` promise that `CurrentSoC` is bounded by the configured floor/ceiling, but validation omits the cross-field invariant.
- **chunk-0207.md:4** — `src/internal/v2h/decider.go:227-283`; `src/internal/v2h/decider_test.go:37-139`: Discharge revenue mixes the displacement and export cases incorrectly: when the house has net import, `revenuePerWh` is set to `RoundTripEfficiency * RateBuyPerWh`, then applied to all `EnergyWh`, including the portion reported separately as `GridExportedWh`. The implementation has no split between grid-offset energy and true exports, so it credits excess battery energy at the import tariff rather than the sell tariff and can rank/choose the same discharge plan using the wrong economics. `evalDischarge`'s own `netHouseImport`/`gridExport` split is evidence of two outputs, but `revenuePerWh` collapses them back into one price (`decider.go:227-283`); the existing tests verify only the SoC floor/ceiling and cheap-charge path, never mixed import/export revenue (`decider_test.go:37-139`).
- **chunk-0207.md:5** — `src/internal/v2h/decider.go:347-368`; `src/internal/v2h/decider.go:326-341`; `src/internal/v2h/decider.go:180-245`: `validate` does not reject NaN or infinite values. Go comparisons allow NaN to bypass every range check (for SoC, power, efficiency, and rates are unvalidated), while `DurationHours = +Inf/-Inf/NaN` can produce non-finite or zero/negative slice energy and Infinity can poison `Plan` totals/SoC. Infinite or NaN tariff values similarly corrupt median sorting and decision arithmetic because the median helper uses ordinary comparisons without filtering non-finite samples. The bounded-input contract in `decider.go` is weakened by absent finite-value checks; median and all downstream plan accounting are non-finite propagation points with only slice count and scalar positivity checks as protections.
- **chunk-0208.md:4** — `src/internal/webpush/service.go:230-239` (`endpointHash`); `src/internal/api/push/handler.go:287-310` (`pushAuditDetail`, `endpointFingerprint`): `endpointHash` does not hash the endpoint and assumes the first 24 bytes are only a provider host. That assumption does not hold for arbitrary subscription URLs: for an endpoint with a short scheme/host and a token beginning before byte 24, the returned “hashed” prefix may expose the opaque per-subscription token. The push audit path already demonstrates the safe pattern by discarding the URL and logging only a 16-hex-character SHA-256 fingerprint. Keeping truncation in webpush logging while using SHA-256 fingerprinting for push subscribe/unsubscribe audits gives inconsistent privacy behavior; a malformed or unusual provider endpoint could leak credentials in warn/info logs despite the API audit sanitization.
- **chunk-0209.md:3** — `src/internal/worker/worker_lifecycle.go:322-338` (`recordVehicleFailure`); `src/internal/worker/worker_lifecycle.go:358-378` (`recordVehicleAsleep`): Both exponential-backoff routines calculate the multiplier with an unbounded shift and then convert it into a `time.Duration` before applying the public cap. Once a vehicle reaches enough consecutive failures/asleep responses, the shift can overflow `int64`; the resulting negative or wrapped duration escapes the cap and can immediately expire or remain invalid instead of restoring the intended 5/10-minute backoff. The failure and asleep counters both use the same pattern (`30 << counter` or `1 << counter`, then conversion/multiplication); each copy therefore has the same high-count overflow edge even though the intended ceiling differs.
- **chunk-0209.md:4** — `src/internal/worker/unit_drift_validator.go:297-302` (`Run`); `src/internal/worker/unit_drift_validator.go:259-275` (`Start`); `src/internal/worker/unit_drift_validator.go:527-550` (`incrementDrift` / `incrementCanary`): `DryRun` is stored in a global validator field by every `Run` call, while metrics emission is read independently by both counter methods. Concurrent passes with different options can therefore switch the dry-run state mid-pass: a non-dry pass can be suppressed by another dry-run pass, or a dry-run pass can emit metrics from state left by an ordinary pass. `incrementDrift` and `incrementCanary` duplicate the global `dry` lookup and are both exposed to the same mutable pass state; the constructor/comment says concurrent `Run` calls are safe, but metric emission is not independent per invocation.
- **chunk-0212.md:3** — `src/internal/audit/recorder.go:188-214`; `src/internal/api/audit.go:21-47`; `src/internal/database/audit/repo.go:158-206,237-266`: The Phase-45 “single-write-path” audit recorder is not actually used by existing audit writers. `insertAuditLog` and the reveal/impersonation repository methods still insert directly into legacy columns and omit `category`, `before_value`, `after_value`, `trace_id`, `prev_row_hash`, `row_hash`, and `success`. Those mutations bypass redaction, hash chaining, and trace correlation, while the migration's/recorder's comments claim they are backward-compatible shims with the new chain intact. Operators can therefore have nominally verified chains that exclude real mutation rows. `src/internal/audit/recorder.go` is the intended second implementation and requires a chain head plus canonical row hash; the direct writers are second copies of audit persistence with narrower behavior, and verification excludes `row_hash IS NULL` rows.
- **chunk-0212.md:4** — `src/migrations/000212_phase45_operator_confidence.up.sql:133-146`; `src/internal/database/gdpr/artifact_repo.go:34-51,70-87`; `src/migrations/000212_phase45_operator_confidence.down.sql`: `export_jobs.storage_kind` is described as restricted to `local_fs`, `s3`, or `NULL`, but migration 000212 adds it as an unrestricted `text` column and the down migration removes it. The GDPR artifact table has a real `CHECK (storage_kind IN ('local_fs', 's3'))`, so later writers of `export_jobs.storage_path`/`storage_kind` can persist arbitrary values that the download layer will reject inconsistently or treat as an unsupported backend. `gdpr_export_artifact.storage_kind` at migration lines 144-148 is the second copy/behavioral reference for the allowed storage kinds and currently rejects invalid values before use; `export_jobs.storage_kind` lacks the equivalent database guard.
- **chunk-0212.md:5** — `src/internal/audit/recorder.go:268-321`; `src/internal/audit/recorder.go:140-162`: `VerifyChain` recomputes each row from its own stored `prev_row_hash` and verifies the stored `row_hash`, but it never checks that `prev_row_hash` equals the `row_hash` of the immediately preceding audit row. It also does not validate continuity across multiple Recorder processes because hydration only selects a chain head. A row may be deleted, reordered, or linked to a stale/different branch while every remaining row's local hash still recomputes successfully; the API reports the chain intact (`badID == 0`) with no continuity invariant. Hydrate loads one arbitrary latest `row_hash` as state, while `Record` does not reserve a database transaction or compare the chain head at insert time, so the second copy/risk is concurrent or legacy branching rather than a linked chronological chain.
- **chunk-0213.md:5** — `src/tools/aistream-contract/main.go:137-156` (`per-event field coverage`); `src/internal/ai/stream/writer.go:584-633` (typed payload structs); `src/web/src/hooks/useAiStream.ts:48-67` (`AiStreamEvent` union): Field validation is not actually per-event: every Go tag and every TypeScript field reference is searched globally across the writer and hook, and `tsHasFieldRef` accepts any property/key substring. A field can therefore appear in the wrong payload while the expected field still passes, and a missing field can be masked by a same-named field on another event or in a comment. The writer has separate payload structs for each event and the TypeScript union has separate variants, so the contract checker has a second authoritative event-scoped shape but performs only an undifferentiated repository-wide substring check.
- **chunk-0213.md:6** — `src/tools/aistream-contract/main.go:76-81` (`expectedFields`); `src/internal/ai/stream/writer.go:625-634` (`limitErrorPayload`); `src/web/src/hooks/useAiStream.ts:58-67` (structured `error` variant): The contract defines `error` as requiring only `message`, but the backend and frontend intentionally share four additional structured limit fields (`reason`, `retry_after_s`, `banner_level`, `baseline_available`) with distinct wire names/types. Because the field map omits them, adding, removing, or misspelling one of these limit fields cannot be detected by this contract tool. The actual backend payload and frontend union contain a second error-event shape for limit decisions; the contract is stale and provides coverage for only the legacy plain-error subset.
- **chunk-0214.md:5** — `src/tools/aivet/main.go:299-326` (`handleCall`), `src/tools/aivet/main.go:397-405` (`joinPath`); `src/tools/aivet/main_test.go:1-177`: The route scanner canonicalizes every literal `/ai/...` path to `/api/v1/ai/...`, even when it is mounted at the repository root or otherwise outside the `/api/v1` sub-router. Therefore a bare or wrapped root-level `/ai/foo` route can be mistaken for the canonical guarded API route and can satisfy registry ownership/mount checks; the scanner's comment explicitly assumes this divergent mount cannot occur, but the implementation does not verify the enclosing mount chain. This is an audit blind spot that can allow an unguarded AI endpoint outside `/api/v1` to pass `aivet`. The scanner has no second, independent mount-point check: `joinPath` only concatenates AST-discovered prefixes, then `handleCall` rewrites `/ai/` paths unconditionally rather than requiring the canonical `/api/v1` prefix to be present in `pathStack`.
- **chunk-0214.md:6** — `src/tools/aivet/main.go:610-628` (`importsUseAiStream`); `src/tools/aivet/main_test.go:70-106` (`TestImportsUseAiStream`): The SPA wiring checker requires the entire canonical import to appear on a single source line: it checks only trimmed lines beginning with `import`, then searches for both tokens on that same line. Valid multiline ESM imports (for example an import block containing `useAiStream` followed by `from '@/hooks/useAiStream'`) are reported as missing even though the component imports the required hook. The unit cases document only one-line imports and therefore leave this false-negative behavior untested. `runW1Checks` is the sole consumer of this predicate and converts this detector error into a missing wiring violation; a valid TSX component can fail the repository audit solely because of formatting.
- **chunk-0215.md:5** — `src/tools/archmetrics/main.go:377-418` (`collect`); `src/tools/archmetrics/main.go:617-637` (`computeForbiddenEdges`); `src/internal/arch/arch_test.go:23-50` (`TestForbiddenEdges`): Package import metrics and forbidden-edge detection merge every `.go` file, including `_test.go`, because `collect` calls `parseImports` for the package before separately counting production and test files. A test-only import can therefore create or preserve a forbidden-edge finding in the baseline/CI comparison even when production code has no such edge; this makes `archmetrics -compare` both less accurate than the live package graph and falsely sensitive to test files. The live `TestForbiddenEdges` should represent production package imports, but `archmetrics` can report a graph synthesized from test imports as well. The architecture tooling has two independent import graph implementations: `parseImports` over all Go files versus `go/packages` over packages; their dependency sets differ for `_test.go`, so a duplicate test dependency is treated as a real architectural regression in one gate but not the other.
- **chunk-0216.md:5** — `src/tools/eval-schema-check/main.go:16-47`; `src/internal/ai/eval/golden.go:13-24,49-93`; `src/internal/ai/eval/types.go:100-108`; `src/cmd/ai-eval/main_test.go:147-163`: The standalone checker and runner both use `yaml.Unmarshal` without `KnownFields(true)`, and the schema validator validates only explicitly enumerated fields. A misspelled or newly introduced YAML key is silently ignored by both loaders, so a goldens file can appear valid and fail to exercise the intended assertion or context override. The CLI test already demonstrates load-error coverage only for values that are in the schema and fails empty IDs/lists, not unknown-key rejection.
- **chunk-0216.md:6** — `src/tools/eval-schema-check/main.go:23-47`; `src/internal/ai/eval/golden.go:29-46`; `src/internal/ai/eval/types.go:35-39`; `src/internal/ai/eval/runner.go:75-103`: `context_overrides` is parsed into `GoldenInput.ContextOverrides`, but `RunGolden` never copies that map anywhere: it constructs `StrategyInput` with only `LastMessage` and `History`, whose type has no override/context field. Consequently every YAML `context_overrides` block is inert in the actual harness, and feature-specific tool scopes or replay metadata can differ from the golden's declared scenario without being reported. The schema loader accepts these values as valid, while the runner silently ignores them for both GenericStrategy and registered Strategy paths.
- **chunk-0219.md:5** — `src/migrations/000255_automation_action_wait.down.sql:3-31`; `src/ops/migrations/manifest.yaml:462-475`; `src/migrations/000255_automation_action_wait.up.sql:11-37`: The rollback does not actually make the migration reversible when the new descriptive command names were used: it restores the old `automation_actions_command_name_check` constraint and then never removes `cabin_overheat_protection_on` or `hvac_on` rows. PostgreSQL validates the restored CHECK against every existing automation action, so a row containing either newly authorized name causes the down migration itself to fail; this contradicts the manifest's recommendation that rollback is handled by removing the new graph forms, and the down SQL leaves neither SQL cleanup nor a way to protect the constraint replacement. The up migration is the second behavioral copy: it explicitly adds both descriptive names to the new constraint and permits existing command rows to retain them, while the down path restores an old closed vocabulary without first deleting/exporting those rows.
- **chunk-0223.md:5** — `src/web/src/__tests__/lazyRoutes.parse.ts:4-14` (`importsIn`); `src/web/src/__tests__/lazyRoutes.parse.test.ts:77-85` (manifest and inline negative controls): The static-import extractor accepts `import()` calls with extra arguments because it reads only `arguments[0]` and does not validate the call's argument count. Inline lazy loaders and manifest `load` thunks are therefore parsed as static even for unsupported forms such as `import('./feature', { with: 'options' })`, weakening the drift/coverage guards even though shared-loader parsing has a separate argument-count check. The shared-loader path already rejects extra dynamic-import arguments at `src/web/src/__tests__/lazyRoutes.parse.ts:20-40`, but the other two consumers reuse `importsIn` without that check, so identical invalid syntax can be accepted in one route form and rejected in another.
- **chunk-0223.md:6** — `src/web/src/__tests__/lazyRoutes.parse.ts:74-79` (`checkShadowing`); `src/web/src/__tests__/lazyRoutes.parse.test.ts:42-60` (shadowing negatives): Shadow detection has no lexical scope awareness: it throws for any variable, parameter, function, or class declaration with the namespace name anywhere in the AST, not only for declarations that can affect a `lazy(routeLoaders.X)` expression in their scope. A valid App file with an unrelated nested parameter or local named `routeLoaders` is consequently rejected even if the route namespace binding is not shadowed at that lazy call. The test suite asserts only same-source-name ambiguity and does not provide an unrelated-scope positive control, so this global AST-name rejection can remain latent while the associated false-negative/false-positive behavior is untested.
- **chunk-0224.md:5** — `src/cmd/slogen/main.go:183-264` (`parseCatalog`/catalog validation), `src/internal/slo/catalog.go:141-260` (`parseCatalog`), `src/cmd/slogen/main.go:113-177` (`validateCatalog`/`validateSLO`), `src/internal/slo/catalog.go:57-77` (`validateCatalog`): The SLO catalogue loader has two independent strict-YAML parsers and validators that are not behaviorally identical. The codegen path requires non-empty owners and descriptions and rejects malformed fast-burn severities, tags, and duplicate names, while the runtime path's validators do not require description/owner and can therefore accept a catalogue that `slogen` rejects (or silently expose an owner/description empty at runtime); either parser's grammar changes can make generated alerts and the admin runtime disagree. `parseCatalog` is duplicated wholesale between the runtime and `cmd/slogen`; `rewriteWindow` is also duplicated in `src/cmd/slogen/generate.go:135-143` and `src/internal/slo/tracker.go:304-316`, while the runtime comments explicitly depend on the codegen implementation remaining byte-for-byte equivalent.
- **chunk-0231.md:5** — `src/web/src/__tests__/startupProviders.contract.test.ts:90-98`, `src/web/src/main.tsx:143-147`: `mountedComponents` scans the JSX region with a component-name regex but never strips JavaScript comments. The comment in `main.tsx` that mentions `<GuardedLink>` and `<GuardedNavLink>` is therefore treated as rendered JSX: `mountedComponents` adds both names, neither is registered in `MAIN_GLOBAL_MOUNTS`, and the valid startup tree is falsely reported as containing unapproved mounts. The same parser can also mistake JSX examples in comments elsewhere for mounts or miss real mounts if comment noise changes. This detector has the same outer mount intent as the app-tree allowlist, but differs in edge semantics by counting comment text as runtime JSX; the allowlist then fails a valid source layout rather than isolating mount ownership.
- **chunk-0231.md:6** — `src/web/src/__tests__/startupProviders.contract.test.ts:135-141`: The static-feature-import assertion recognizes only a single-line ES import whose whole statement ends in `from "..."` or `'...'`; it cannot detect multiline imports, side-effect imports, or alternate relative paths that load `src/features/**`. Consequently a static feature dependency can bypass this source audit despite still reaching the entry chunk, undermining the CLEAN-06 guarantee the test claims to enforce. The companion `web/scripts/check-bundle-size.mjs` startup-feature check detects feature modules in the built closure, so source-level audit and built-output audit have different coverage and can disagree about whether the invariant is protected; the source regex can pass while the actual build still fails or vice versa depending on import syntax.
- **chunk-0232.md:5** — `src/web/src/hooks/useUrlState.ts:73-94`: The parsed URL value is memoized only by `raw`, while parsing also depends on `opts.defaultValue` and can depend on `opts.parse`. When a convenience hook changes its default (for example responsive `density` changes default from `compact` to `comfortable`) but the parameter remains absent, the memo cache returns the old default because `lastRaw.current === raw` remains true; the URL can also be absent while the rendered state still has a stale value. The defaults are owned here by `useUrlState` and copied into the convenience wrappers (`useUrlString`, `useUrlBoolean`, `useUrlNumber`, `useUrlEnum`, and `useUrlArray`); differing page defaults such as `density` in `useDrivesListPageFilters.ts` expose this cache key mismatch after viewport/controller changes.
- **chunk-0232.md:6** — `src/web/src/__tests__/url-state-adoption.test.ts:33,38-52`: `URL_HOOK_RE` omits the canonical `useUrlBatch` export even though batching is one of the URL-state APIs and is used for atomic multi-key filter updates. A filter-bearing page that adopted batching without also calling a single-key convenience hook would evade this static adoption guard. The same canonical module exports `useUrlBatch` at `src/web/src/hooks/useUrlState.ts:265-287`; the guard recognizes the five value-hook families but silently excludes the actual atomic API, so a future page could use an implementation-specific local URL writer and still not be detected.
- **chunk-0235.md:5** — `src/migrations/000235_charge_autopilot_profiles.up.sql:1-14`; `src/internal/database/charging/autopilot_repo.go:16-23,43-79`; `src/internal/api/chargeautopilot/compute.go:21,29-36,59-61,157`; `src/web/src/types/charging.ts:274`; `src/web/src/features/charging/components/smart-charge-modernization/AutopilotCard.tsx:87`; `src/web/src/features/charging/components/AutopilotPanel.tsx:104`: The new feature introduces canonical-storage/transport energy fields named `battery_capacity_kwh` and `BatteryCapacityKWh` instead of SI Wh. The migration stores capacity as a floating-point legacy kWh column, while every backend calculation, preview, and frontend save payload copies that unit. This conflicts with the repository's established SI contract and makes future vehicle-capacity or rate calculations depend on callers retaining an implicit kWh unit; migration 235 also bypasses the active Phase-48 naming/storage rule. The duplicate copies are the SQL column, database model/repository, API profile/preview types, and two frontend implementations; a unit suffix or conversion drift in one is not protected by a shared canonical energy type.
- **chunk-0235.md:8** — `src/web/src/features/charging/components/AutopilotPanel.tsx:47-245`; `src/web/src/features/charging/components/smart-charge-modernization/AutopilotCard.tsx:26-223`; `src/web/src/features/charging/pages/SmartChargePage.tsx:444`; `src/web/src/features/charging/components/AutopilotPanel.test.tsx:146-245`: The repository contains two full Charging Autopilot implementations. The live Smart Charge page uses `AutopilotCard`, while `AutopilotPanel` remains a separate implementation with its own form state, saved-profile hydration, mutation handling, savings rendering, preview, and timeline UI. They already differ in trust/error behavior and enablement: the card requires a hydrated profile and blocks Run unless the persisted profile is enabled, while the panel enables Run from draft state and has weaker loading/error handling. Future fixes or removals of one copy leave the feature split into stale behavior, as the panel is not mounted by the live page but is retained by active tests. The two files repeat every profile field, save/preview/run action, rate-plan options, SOC controls, preview explanation, savings display, and timeline. At least `AutopilotCard` is the live owner from `SmartChargePage`, while `AutopilotPanel` is only a second orphan implementation plus test subject.
- **chunk-0240.md:6** — `src/web/src/api/sseClient.ts:197-221` (`parseEnvelope` / `coerceValue`); `src/web/src/hooks/useSSE.ts:54-82` (`parseSignalChangeEvent` / `coerceValue`); `src/web/src/api/__tests__/sseClient.test.ts:159-194`; `src/web/src/hooks/useSSE.test.ts:118-196`: The two SSE payload parsers are intentionally named as separate implementations but disagree on malformed contracts: `parseEnvelope` rejects unknown kinds and non-finite vehicle IDs, while `parseSignalChangeEvent` maps unknown kinds to `"unknown"` and accepts a numeric NaN/Infinity `vehicle_id`; it also does not validate `ts` as a string, unlike the typed parser. The two test suites therefore certify incompatible behavior for the same backend event, so one consumer can receive or forward an envelope that another rejects/corrupts. Both parsers also coerce mismatched booleans with `Boolean(value)`, making string `"false"` or numeric values outside the exact boolean path become `true`. A single `signal_change` wire payload is parsed independently at these locations, with no shared authoritative guard or error contract; the disagreement is a validation/contract defect, not merely duplicated test cases.
- **chunk-0241.md:5** — `src/web/src/api/analytics.ts:28-28` (`getEnergyStats`), `src/web/src/api/analytics.ts:37-37` (`getFleetAnalytics`); `src/web/src/api/analytics.test.ts:75-82,98-105`: These two helpers interpolate an RFC3339 `start` value directly into a query string, unlike route/detail, trips, drives, and charging helpers that serialize bounds through `URLSearchParams` (with encoding). A valid offset-bearing timestamp such as `2024-01-01T00:00:00+05:30` reaches the server with `+` interpreted as a space, so `time.Parse`/date-range parsing can reject an otherwise valid bound. The current analytics tests cover only date-only strings and therefore do not exercise this branch. The same module already constructs encoded query strings in `getRouteEfficiencyDetail` (line 56) and `getTrips` (line 106), while `src/web/src/api/drives.ts:7` and `src/web/src/api/charging.ts:27` encode paginated date ranges with `URLSearchParams`; the two raw interpolations are divergent copies with timezone-offset and reserved-character failure risk.
- **chunk-0258.md:5** — `src/web/src/api/fleetStateSSE.ts:125-128` (`mutateVehicleState`, `BatteryLevel`); `src/web/src/hooks/useVehicleLive.ts:362-364` (`parseSignals`, `BatteryLevel`); `src/internal/service/vehicle_service.go:226-231` (`BatteryLevel` live mapping): The SSE patch and live-signal decoder use different fractional-percent conversions for the same `BatteryLevel` signal: the SSE patch uses `Math.trunc(numberValue)`, while `useVehicleLive` uses `Math.round(n('BatteryLevel'))`; the Go canonical state assembler uses integer conversion (`int(f)`), which truncates. For example, `80.6%` becomes `80` in both the SSE patch and Go mapping but `81` in the live-signal path, so equivalent telemetry snapshots can disagree depending on transport. Make transport mappings preserve one documented integerization rule, aligned with the canonical Go state assembler, or reconcile the behavior intentionally across all three copies. The SSE patch is explicitly intended to mirror canonical vehicle-state assembly, but currently only its truncation copy matches Go; a second live-state implementation rounds and can expose 1-point drift. The risk is wrong battery percentage, trust metadata, and UI/derived-state decisions immediately after live events until an authoritative reconcile replaces the patch.
- **chunk-0308.md:1** — src/web/src/components/charts/useMeasuredAxisWidth.ts:32-49: `fallbackCharacterRatio` is not included in the measurement cache key or effect dependencies, so changing it after a successful canvas measurement leaves the stale measured width returned instead of recalculating the caller's fallback policy; this also applies when a previously measured chart rerenders with canvas measurement unavailable, because `measurement?.key === key` is reused without checking whether canvas fallback should supersede it. Companion coverage in src/web/src/components/charts/useMeasuredAxisWidth.test.ts:67-87 currently checks fallback changes only before a measurement exists and does not cover this stale-cache transition.
- **chunk-0321.md:2** — src/web/src/components/feedback/RateLimitBanner.tsx:114-116: the banner declares role="alert" (assertive by platform semantics) while also setting aria-live="polite". The conflicting ARIA modes can cause browsers/screen readers to discard or inconsistently announce the rate-limit message; use the shared status/alert convention consistently instead of pairing an assertive role with polite live-region politeness.
- **chunk-0323.md:7** — `src/web/src/components/feedback/__tests__/NavigationGuardProvider.test.tsx:188-214` (`skips popstate handling once after the user confirms discard`): The final post-discard regression guard is `expect(true).toBe(true)`: it explicitly acknowledges that timing may or may not reopen the dialog, but performs no assertion on URL restoration, the pending promise, or whether a second popstate is consumed. Comment-only intent cannot catch a broken skip flag or an infinite prompt loop. The test suite can pass even if the skip-next-popstate behavior is removed entirely, because the only preceding assertion only checks that the dialog closes, not that a subsequent `popstate` is suppressed. Replace this with deterministic navigation/popstate control and assert the dialog and destination/URL remain stable.
- **chunk-0324.md:5** — `src/web/src/components/feedback/VitalsConsentPolicyGate.tsx:44-57`; `src/web/src/features/settings/pages/PrivacyPage.tsx:65-70`; `src/web/src/lib/webVitalsConsent.ts:220-242`: The App-root gate does not validate the live `/system/version` flag before publishing it. It collapses every non-`undefined` payload with `Boolean(data.require_cookie_consent)`; consequently runtime values such as `null`, `0`, or `"false"` become `not-required`, and `undefined` also becomes the live `false` policy, permitting or holding against the wrong server contract. PrivacyPage already treats a boolean with a runtime `typeof` check as the only resolved policy and derives `unknown` for missing/null/string/number flags, so the gate can diverge from the privacy UI and fail open on malformed transport data. Require a `typeof data?.require_cookie_consent === 'boolean'` guard, map only that boolean to the policy, and publish `unknown` for loading, missing, transport-error, or malformed payload state; separately derive `resolved` from query state rather than `data !== undefined`, so stale/error refetches cannot masquerade as a resolved live policy. PrivacyPage’s `policyKnown` check at `src/web/src/features/settings/pages/PrivacyPage.tsx:67` is the second, stricter interpretation of the same field; the shared decision function at `src/web/src/lib/webVitalsConsent.ts:220-242` expects only an unknown/required/not-required live state, so this malformed-value conversion bypasses the same fail-closed contract.
- **chunk-0326.md:1** — src/web/src/components/forms/RangePicker.tsx:98-112,199-204: Calendar-day arithmetic is implemented again as local `Date` construction plus millisecond division (`dateFromIso`/`diffDaysInclusive`), even though `src/web/src/lib/dateRange.ts` owns civil-date and timezone rules. More importantly, `matchPresetId(value.start, value.end)` at the active-preset calculation omits the component's `timezone` argument, and `maxDateObj` defaults to browser-local `new Date()` instead of today's date in `timezone`; a timezone supplied by a vehicle/page can therefore get the wrong active preset and can enable the wrong upper-day boundary. Companion coverage in `src/web/src/components/forms/__tests__/RangePicker.test.tsx:88-104` tests timezone-independent preset matching/manager behavior but does not exercise timezone or DST-civil-day parity.
- **chunk-0326.md:3** — src/web/src/components/forms/UnitInput.tsx:100-116: Invalid input is committed as `null` in `commit()`, but `dirtyRef.current` is left true when it returns on the validation-error branch. Pressing Enter on invalid text followed by blur calls `commit()` twice and invokes `onChange(null)` twice; it can also surface the error repeatedly before any edit. The implementation should clear or preserve the draft explicitly instead of leaving it dirty after an invalid commit attempt, so Enter and subsequent blur produce one terminal outcome.
- **chunk-0326.md:5** — src/web/src/components/forms/TagInput.tsx:230-247: `commitAll()` discards an invalid or duplicate pending tag by replacing `pending` with the synthetic-separator commit's empty remainder. The component contract says validation blocks the commit until the user edits, but for `validateTag` failure this clears the pending text immediately while retaining only the error; the user loses the text they need to correct. The paste/blur/Enter paths share this behavior because they call `commitAll`; `commitText()` should preserve the full unresolved raw text for invalid candidates while clearing it only when an added/rejected duplicate outcome has completed as designed.
- **chunk-0326.md:7** — src/web/src/components/forms/TreeSelect.tsx:367-414: All search, loading, empty, selection, disabled-reason, and no-results labels are authored as hardcoded English strings in this shared primitive, and several analogous variants are emitted in the same render path (`Filter tree`, `Clear search`, `Select/Clear all`, `selected of`, `No items available`, `No matches for`, `Loading…`, and row labels). These strings bypass the established `react-i18next` catalog used by sibling form primitives, so the controlled generic component cannot preserve locale parity and exposes untranslated copy in non-English shells. Move these user-facing labels/count templates into the existing i18n context/catalog rather than keeping component-local English fallbacks.
- **chunk-0328.md:2** — src/web/src/components/layout/Layout.tsx:792-806 and src/web/src/hooks/useNotificationListener.ts:168-179: both realtime alert paths synthesize an alert timestamp with `new Date().toISOString()` when the SSE payload omits `created_at`, and then pass it to `getAlertDrillthroughHref()`; this fabricates the event time and may center a destination chart/alert context at arrival time rather than leaving timestamp-dependent context absent. The same fallback appears in both copies, so an alert that has only `vehicle_id` or `rule_signal` is converted into a false `t=<now>` query while unknown event time should remain unknown. Modify both copies (ideally through one alert-to-drillthrough helper) to omit `created_at`/`t` when the payload provides none.
- **chunk-0328.md:3** — src/web/src/components/layout/NotificationBellPanel.tsx:26-32: `SEVERITY_TONE.label` hardcodes English `"Info"`, `"Warning"`, and `"Critical"` and is reused as a notification item's accessible dot label. The neighboring severity filter already translates the three labels via `NotificationSeverityFilter` (`src/web/src/components/layout/NotificationSeverityFilter.tsx:30-43`), so this copy remains English under non-English locales and is inconsistent with the filter/catalog i18n pattern. Add translation keys/fallbacks through `useTranslation()` rather than embedding the labels in the tone registry.
- **chunk-0329.md:1** — src/web/src/components/layout/WorkspaceContextControl.tsx:82,290: the active calendar year is read with `new Date()` plus `civilDateInTimeZone`, but the displayed custom date bounds are passed through `formatDate`/`formatDateShort` as synthetic local midnight strings (`${range.start}T00:00:00`) rather than the canonical `formatDayKey` calendar-key formatter; that mixes calendar keys with instant parsing, so rendering in a browser timezone or locale can shift/format the business dates differently from the range's `range.timezone`. Companion code in src/web/src/features/dashboard/widgets/MotorHistoryWidget.tsx:101 and src/web/src/features/dashboard/widgets/TirePressureHistoryWidget.tsx:161 repeats the same synthetic-midnight pattern for stored `YYYY-MM-DD` keys. Use `civilDateInTimeZone`/`formatDayKey` as appropriate so key semantics remain timezone-neutral and the three surfaces cannot disagree.
- **chunk-0329.md:2** — src/web/src/components/layout/PageHeaderSticky.tsx:62-79: after the observer has already made the bar visible, changing `targetId` to an element that does not exist leaves `visible` true because the new effect returns before resetting it, and the absent target also has no fallback visibility update. The bar therefore remains mounted while the requested sticky source is gone; the replacement-observer test at src/web/src/components/layout/PageHeaderSticky.pilot.test.tsx:163-173 and src/web/src/components/layout/__tests__/PageHeaderSticky.test.tsx:157-164 cover target replacement but not this missing-target transition.
- **chunk-0329.md:3** — src/web/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx:31-61: `DIAGNOSTIC_NAV_ENTRY`, `LIVE_LOGS_NAV_ENTRY`, and `RBAC_NAV_ENTRY` point to `/diagnostic`, `/live-logs`, and `/admin/rbac`, but those routes are absent from the generated `src/web/src/lib/routeRegistry.ts` route source and no matching path registrations appear in `src/web/src/App.tsx`; the registry-backed sidebar therefore exposes entries that the router cannot resolve, while `src/web/src/components/layout/Sidebar.test.tsx:44-57` asserts only that the three paths are unique and absolute and does not verify them against the route registry/App. Register canonical routes or remove/redirect these stale entries so navigation, generated registry, and tests agree.
- **chunk-0334.md:2** — `src/web/src/components/layout/status-bar/PresentationModeSegment.tsx:102-117`: the direct status-bar trigger is the only presentation control in this chunk that has fixed `h-5/min-h-0` sizing and omits the `min-h-11 min-w-11 shrink-0` mobile-target classes used by the neighboring status-bar segments. It therefore exposes only a 20px-high control at mobile widths, despite the status-bar's fixed 44px touch-row contract.
- **chunk-0335.md:1** — src/web/src/components/layout/status-bar/StatusOverflowRow.tsx:30-44: `StatusOverflowRowContent` always renders the status-dot `<span>` even when `dotClassName` is omitted; the component's own contract says the dot is for status-bearing items and omitted for items with no status, but callers such as src/web/src/components/layout/status-bar/MoreSegment.tsx:170-184 pass no dot class and therefore get an empty dot occupying a column, with an extra gap before the label/icon. Render the dot conditionally on `dotClassName`.
- **chunk-0335.md:2** — src/web/src/components/layout/status-bar/RecentPagesSegment.tsx:58-66,89-90: the page-local `formatRelative` reimplements the relative-time/date policy instead of the cataloged shared date formatters and the component snapshots `Date.now()` without scheduling an update; an open recents popover therefore keeps old minute/hour/day labels indefinitely after it stops receiving new navigation events. This copy also risks locale/translation drift relative to src/web/src/components/ui/CommandPalette.tsx:291-301, which has a separate implementation with minute/hour/day suffix semantics. Prefer the shared localized formatter plus a refresh seam, or parameterize an extracted recent-age formatter used by both surfaces.
- **chunk-0335.md:3** — src/web/src/components/layout/status-bar/useAboutBuild.ts:8-15: local `formatUptime` duplicates uptime duration formatting used elsewhere (for example src/web/src/features/system/components/status/helpers.tsx:48-56) but silently drops the minute remainder for day/hour uptime (`200000` renders `"2d 7h"` rather than the true elapsed `"2d 7h 33m"`), while invalid/zero handling also differs across copies. Use one canonical uptime formatter so tooltip and modal provenance cannot drift from system-status displays.
- **chunk-0336.md:1** — src/web/src/components/maps/MarkerCluster.tsx:196-205: coordinate validation checks only `Number.isNaN`, so `Infinity`, `-Infinity`, and finite coordinates outside latitude/longitude bounds are added to Leaflet and can corrupt clustering or the map viewport. This differs from the finite/bounds validation in src/web/src/lib/geo.ts:51-56 (`isValidLatLng`) and the finite guards in src/web/src/components/maps/AnimatedMarker.tsx:83 and src/web/src/components/maps/RoutePlayback.tsx:247; use the shared GPS validity contract rather than accepting non-physical fixes.
- **chunk-0336.md:2** — src/web/src/components/maps/RoutePlayback.tsx:91-99: `buildOffsets` derives offsets from `points[0].timestamp` and does not validate that first timestamp. If the first or another timestamp is invalid, arithmetic can produce `NaN` offsets, while an invalid later timestamp is silently mapped to `0`; `totalMs`/binary seeks then use a broken timeline and nearest-frame results become unreliable. The established replay timeline in src/web/src/hooks/useTripReplay.ts:54-68 explicitly finds the first valid timestamp and preserves unknown timestamps; parameterize/adopt that behavior instead of inventing zero offsets for malformed telemetry.
- **chunk-0337.md:2** — src/web/src/components/mobile/PullToRefresh.tsx:153-158: the refresh promise is awaited inside `try/finally`, but no rejection is caught; because the event handler calls `void release()`, a rejected `onRefresh()` escapes as an unhandled promise rejection instead of being settled silently after resetting the indicator as the component contract states. Add a catch (while preserving `finally`) so rejected refreshes do not become unhandled rejections.
- **chunk-0341.md:4** — src/web/src/components/status/StickyChipBar.tsx:70-73 and src/web/src/components/status/StickyCompactHero.tsx:88-90: both force `behavior: 'smooth'` for scroll-to-section/top, while the cataloged motion primitive in `src/web/src/components/layout/PageHeaderSticky.tsx:91-98` reads `useMotionPreference()` and uses `behavior = reduce ? 'auto' : 'smooth'`; users with reduced-motion enabled therefore still receive smooth scrolling from these two status controls.
- **chunk-0343.md:5** — `src/web/src/lib/typography-agent/orchestrator.ts:85-99` (`loadPersisted`); companion coverage `src/web/src/lib/typography-agent/orchestrator.test.ts:60-64`: Persisted-spec validation checks only that the JSON fields exist and have broad primitive types; it does not validate enum values (`ratio`, `density`) or numeric ranges/bounds. A JSON object such as `{"ratio":"unknown","density":"unknown","baseSizeMinPx":15,...}` passes the cast and can later make `RATIO_VALUES[ratio]`/`DENSITY_MULTIPLIERS[density]` undefined and compile `clamp(...)`/leading values containing `NaN`. Reversed, zero-width, or nonpositive viewport/base values are likewise accepted, risking division by zero and invalid CSS tokens. The corrupt-spec test only covers malformed JSON and a missing-field object, so these malformed-but-well-shaped records are not covered. The loader feeds both the provider's initial singleton spec and the settings-seed path; bad persisted state persists until local storage is cleared. Validate against `RATIO_VALUES`, density keys, positive viewport/base values, and `viewportMinPx < viewportMaxPx` with `baseSizeMinPx <= baseSizeMaxPx` before returning the parsed spec.
- **chunk-0346.md:2** — src/web/src/components/ui/MaskedValue.tsx:109-133: `revealed` is component state that is not reset when `value` changes, so a revealed value remains revealed after the parent supplies a different raw secret or identifier. This violates the privacy primitive's "Initial render is always masked" contract and can expose a newly loaded secret without another explicit reveal interaction. Add an effect keyed by the underlying raw value (or move reveal state to a value-tagged state) to return to masked and clear/re-arm the auto-hide timer whenever `raw` changes; the current timer callback in src/web/src/components/ui/MaskedValue.tsx:116-122 may otherwise also hide a reveal that has not just been initiated for the new value. Companion coverage in src/web/src/components/ui/__tests__/MaskedValue.test.tsx:48-145 covers initial/reveal/hide behavior but has no rerender-to-a-new-value case, so this privacy state transition is unprotected.
- **chunk-0347.md:1** — src/web/src/components/ui/SignalConfigModal.tsx:14-91,287-289: The signal interval labels/descriptions and preset names/descriptions are hard-coded English outside the i18n layer even though the surrounding modal uses `useTranslation()` and a localized catalog for every action. Presets such as “Real-time Driving”, “Balanced”, “Low Power”, and their runtime category descriptions remain English in every locale, so switching locale/localizing the modal does not change these controls. Add cataloged translation keys (or existing canonical localized equivalents) for both the option labels/descriptions and preset identity/description; otherwise users receive a mixed-language dialog and cannot rely on the shared locale contract.
- **chunk-0348.md:5** — `src/web/src/components/ui/ThemePicker.tsx:67-68`, `225-269`; `src/web/src/components/ui/ThemeProvider.tsx:420-425`, `482-489`, `528-536`: ThemePicker snapshots custom primary/accent from localStorage only once, while ThemeProvider can replace those values from backend settings or a cross-tab `theme.customColors` broadcast. The stale picker state can then be reapplied by clicking the Custom swatch or editing its builder, reverting a provider/backend update. Derive the picker state from the live custom theme in `ThemeContext`, or synchronize it when `ctx.themes.custom.primary/accent` changes; keep localStorage only as the provider fallback. ThemeProvider is the authoritative source exposed by `themes.custom`; ThemePicker has a second independently initialized copy of the same settings.
- **chunk-0349.md:1** — src/web/src/components/ui/Tooltip.tsx:171-197: `updatePosition()` only ever sets `maxWidth` for multiline/boundary tooltip states and never clears it when `multiline` or `boundaryRef` is removed, or when an available-width clamp becomes unusable. A rerender from a bounded multiline tooltip to a plain or non-boundary tooltip therefore leaves the old width cap in the inline style, constraining text that is supposed to revert to the normal single-line/surface behavior; the companion placement tests cover measurement changes but not prop/state transitions that remove the width constraint.
- **chunk-0351.md:1** — src/web/src/components/ui/tableValueFilters.ts:8-10: `tableValueKey()` serializes `NaN` and infinities through `JSON.stringify`, which maps them to `"null"`; numeric zero/false/null are separately tested, but a non-finite numeric reading therefore receives the same key as an unknown/null reading (`tableValueKey(NaN) === tableValueKey(null)`) even though the value types are distinct. The same implementation also feeds `buildTableFilterValues()` and filtering/matching in `DataTable.tsx`, so a NaN row can be merged into or filtered alongside unknown values instead of being represented as its own canonical non-finite value; companion coverage in `src/web/src/components/ui/tableValueFilters.test.ts:5-45` treats only null/zero/false strings and does not cover non-finite inputs.
- **chunk-0352.md:3** — src/web/src/components/vehicles/VehicleHeroCard.tsx:133-134,270: `powerDisplay` converts SI watts according to `unitPrefs.power`, but the chip labels every result with the hardcoded `kW` suffix. In watts mode (shared preference may be `W`) the chip displays watt-derived values such as `42000 W` as `42000 kW`, a unit/scale display defect; use `unitPrefs.power` as the rendered suffix and cover the watt branch in the suite, especially src/web/src/components/vehicles/VehicleHeroCard.test.tsx:69-106.
- **chunk-0352.md:4** — src/web/src/components/vehicles/VehicleTwin.tsx:1412-1420: this component constructs `ResizeObserver` unconditionally, while src/web/src/components/vehicles/VirtualizedVehicleGrid.tsx:47-59 guards for its absence and provides a window-resize fallback. In non-browser/older-browser/test environments where `ResizeObserver` is unavailable, mounting `VehicleTwin` throws before render instead of degrading to the fixed `SIZE_MAP` size; add the same feature guard/fallback and mirror it in src/web/src/components/vehicles/__tests__/VehicleTwin.test.tsx:660-686.
- **chunk-0352.md:6** — src/web/src/components/vehicles/VehicleTwin.tsx:1434-1436,1490-1499: `photoState` is retained when `paint.id` or `model` changes the `photoUrl`; if a first compositor URL fails, `photoState` remains `failed`, so that branch permanently stops rendering any `<img>` and prevents a retry for a subsequently valid paint/model URL. Conversely, switching from a ready photo to a new URL never explicitly resets loading state, so old composited rendering can remain visible until the browser loads the replacement; reset `photoState` to `loading` when `photoUrl` changes, and cover repaint model/paint transitions in src/web/src/components/vehicles/__tests__/VehicleTwin.test.tsx:710-724.
- **chunk-0353.md:5** — `src/web/src/features/action-center/components/RecommendationDetails.tsx:23-31` (`formatCost`): Currency projections are transported as ISO-4217 minor units, but this formatter unconditionally divides by `100`. That is only correct for currencies with exponent 2; it converts JPY, KRW, and other zero-decimal currencies incorrectly, and three-decimal currencies such as KWD/BHD by a factor of ten. Negative, zero, and valid amount behavior also depend on the currency's `Intl.resolvedOptions().maximumFractionDigits`. Use the shared currency minor-unit formatter contract (which resolves the currency exponent), including the existing invalid-code fallback behavior; do not infer every monetary unit is a cent. `src/web/src/features/fleet-ops/helpers.ts:133-149` (`formatMinorUnits`) and `src/web/src/features/ownership/formatters.ts:14-29` / `src/web/src/features/advanced-intelligence/formatters.ts:25-40` already account for the currency exponent. Leaving this duplicate's hardcoded `/100` means the same `cost_minor` wire value can display differently across Action Center and other money surfaces, and exotic-currency projections cannot be represented correctly.
- **chunk-0354.md:1** — src/migrations/000251_geofence_charging_classification.up.sql:14-16: The final `UPDATE geofences SET enabled = true WHERE needs_review = false AND enabled = false` re-enables every manually disabled, reviewed geofence, not only places recognized as charging by the preceding migration. A previously disabled home/work/custom place or other user choice is silently re-enabled after deployment, changing routing, alerts, and place matching; companion manifest wording at `src/ops/migrations/manifest.yaml:471-482` labels this blanket change intentional, but it is still an operator-visible data-loss-of-choice bug. Restrict re-enablement to qualifying charging/discovery rows, or remove the blanket update if the classifier migration should not alter existing enablement.
- **chunk-0355.md:6** — `src/web/src/features/admin/components/RedisDiagnosticEmptyState.tsx:190-193`: The stale/TTL branch treats an invalid `l1_last_seen_at` as a valid recent-looking timestamp: `lastSeenL1` is a truthy `Date`, but its `getTime()` is `NaN`, so the `> 7 days` comparison is false and the component skips the no-telemetry branch and falls through to the neutral empty-cache branch. Invalid timestamps are already retained by the API decoder when the response supplies an unparseable string (`src/web/src/api/devtools.ts`), so malformed metadata can produce an incorrect diagnostic classification instead of identifying it as absent/stale/unknown; the guard should verify finite time before applying freshness thresholds.
- **chunk-0356.md:1** — src/web/src/features/admin/components/api-keys/helpers.ts:12-20: `isRecentlyUsed` measures recent activity only with `now - ts <= windowMs`; it does not reject timestamps in the future, so an API timestamp ahead of the supplied clock (clock skew, stale clock, or malformed future data) satisfies the condition even for an arbitrarily distant future date. That inflates `summary.recentlyUsed`; this copy's test at src/web/src/features/admin/components/api-keys/helpers.test.ts:87-93 explicitly pins the wrong behavior by asserting a future usage timestamp is recent, and src/web/src/types/admin.test.ts:121-123 covers only past timestamps.
- **chunk-0356.md:2** — src/web/src/features/admin/components/RedisDiagnosticEmptyState.tsx:190-193: `lastSeenL1` is constructed with `new Date(meta.l1_last_seen_at)` but neither validity nor a finite timestamp is checked. An invalid-but-non-null backend timestamp yields a truthy `Date` whose `getTime()` is `NaN`; the `> SEVEN_DAYS_MS` comparison is false, so the component bypasses the stale/no-telemetry branch and incorrectly renders the neutral "No signals cached" fallthrough. Guard the parsed instant with `Number.isFinite(lastSeenL1?.getTime())` (or equivalent shared parsing semantics) so malformed timestamps take the no-recent-data diagnosis rather than masquerading as recent absence; companion branch coverage in src/web/src/features/admin/components/__tests__/RedisDiagnosticEmptyState.test.tsx:343-363 covers only valid old/recent timestamps.
- **chunk-0358.md:7** — `src/web/src/features/admin/components/QueueJobDrawer.tsx:34-62`, `src/web/src/features/admin/components/continuation-admin-2/WorkerJobsDrawer.tsx:18-24`: Both duration resolvers reject `duration_ms: 0` as though it were missing, while a measured zero duration is valid evidence and can be distinguished from a missing/unknown value. In a row with `duration_ms = 0` and no usable timestamps, the current copies suppress the “Took 0…” segment even though the backend supplied an authoritative zero; the fallback also cannot recover a zero if timestamps are unavailable. Preserve zero as a zero duration and use timestamp subtraction only when the explicit field is not numeric.
- **chunk-0358.md:8** — `src/web/src/features/admin/components/data-quality/FieldQualityTable.tsx:115-124`, `src/web/src/features/admin/components/continuation-admin-2/FieldQualityTable.tsx:59-61`: The legacy field-quality table converts an unknown `unversioned_sample_count` into `0` via `r.unversioned_sample_count ?? 0`, while the continuation implementation and catalog null-safety contract retain unknown provenance as an em dash rather than treating it as measured zero. Missing provenance becomes a confident “0 unattested” in the legacy copy, which falsely implies that all observations are attested; the active continuation copy already distinguishes null from zero, so the legacy table should not be used as an alternate interpretation.
- **chunk-0359.md:7** — `src/web/src/features/admin/components/security-access/helpers.ts:107-119`; `src/web/src/features/admin/components/continuation-admin-3/SecuritySummary.tsx:21`; `src/web/src/features/admin/components/security-access/SummaryStatsRow.tsx:37`: `timeSince` accepts an unparseable timestamp and derives `NaN` arithmetic, then can produce an incorrect result instead of the shared time formatter's canonical `—` placeholder. It also reimplements relative instant formatting rather than using `formatRelative`/`formatRelativeTime`, which makes malformed and future timestamps differ between security surfaces and the rest of the app. The helper is used by both the continuation summary and the legacy summary row. An invalid backend `createdAt` value can render as `NaNm ago`, `NaNh ago`, or `NaNd ago` instead of an unknown age; canonical instant formatting already rejects invalid dates.
- **chunk-0362.md:1** — src/web/src/features/admin/components/devtools/tools/HashCalculator.tsx:34-61: `compute()` captures `inputVal`, awaits `crypto.subtle.digest()`, and then unconditionally writes `hashResult` or `errorMsg`; editing the textarea only clears the displayed result, but an older in-flight digest can still resolve or reject after a newer edit and publish a digest for the old text. This reverses the stale-result guard the component otherwise enforces and can show a misleading SHA-256 result with the current input. Guard result application by a generation/request identity captured at click time, or by checking that the current input is still the value that started the digest.
- **chunk-0362.md:2** — src/web/src/features/admin/components/devtools/helpers.ts:47-139 and src/web/src/features/admin/components/devtools/tools/CronParser.tsx:21-35: valid cron expressions with step/range/list syntax are parsed by `parseCronField` and will produce the correct next-run set in `getNextCronRuns`, but `describeCron()` collapses many of them to generic summaries: `*/5 * * * *` enters the “Every minute” branch, and similarly range/list/day-of-week/day-of-month expressions do not describe the actual schedule. Worse, `getNextCronRuns()` applies day-of-month and day-of-week as `&&`, whereas standard cron semantics use each field according to the cron contract (wildcard/non-wildcard combination affects OR-vs-AND). The description and next-run list can therefore disagree with the entered expression; preserve the parsed field semantics in description logic and implement cron DOM/DOW matching consistently rather than treating both fields as independent required matches.
- **chunk-0362.md:3** — src/web/src/features/admin/components/devtools/tools/Base64Tool.tsx:28-31 and src/web/src/features/admin/components/devtools/tools/JwtDecoder.tsx:27-32: both decode binary bytes with the non-fatal default `new TextDecoder().decode(...)`; invalid UTF-8 sequences are silently replaced with U+FFFD instead of being treated as invalid decoded content. Base64 decoding consequently reports a success result for arbitrary valid Base64 containing corrupt text, while JWT decoding can return replacement characters instead of setting the established `error: 'invalid'` state. Decode with fatal UTF-8 validation (or first validate with `{ fatal: true }`) so neither tool converts malformed payload bytes into plausible text.
- **chunk-0362.md:4** — src/web/src/features/admin/components/devtools/tools/ByteSizeConverter.tsx:24-39: validation guards only the parsed input itself (`Number.isFinite(num)`) before computing `num * Math.pow(1024, unitIdx)`. A large but finite value such as `1e308 TB` produces `Infinity` bytes before the result grid is formatted; `fmtNumber(Infinity, ...)` can therefore render a misleading non-finite value instead of an invalid state. Guard the computed byte magnitude and every converted result with `Number.isFinite(bytes)`/`Number.isFinite(convertedValue)` so overflow degrades to the same invalid empty state as negative or non-finite input.
- **chunk-0363.md:6** — `src/web/src/features/admin/components/devtools/tools/TimestampTool.tsx:27-35` (`parseUnix`): The Unix parser uses a digit-count heuristic (`>10` means milliseconds, `<=10` means seconds), so a valid seconds timestamp with 11 or 12 digits is interpreted as milliseconds. For example, `10000000000` is an 11-digit value representing the year 2286 in seconds, but this code constructs a date from `10000000000` milliseconds (1970-04-26); similarly shaped future and negative seconds values are misclassified. The split should be based on a magnitude/observed timestamp contract (for example the established seconds-vs-milliseconds range), rather than `trimmed.replace('-', '').length > 10`. This tool is the only Unix parser found in the reviewed chunk, but it has a real conversion defect at the 11–12 digit ambiguity boundary; no second copy is needed because the defect is in the parser's edge-case branch, but malformed values should also be validated after construction to avoid silently accepting overflow/invalid dates.
- **chunk-0365.md:6** — `src/web/src/features/admin/components/gas-price/GasPriceControlPanel.tsx:34-36,99-113`; `src/web/src/features/admin/components/continuation-admin-2/GasConfiguration.tsx:13-14,26-48`: The newer `GasPriceControlPanel` issues toggle/config mutation writes without `useOperationalMode()` eligibility or pending disables, while the established continuation gas panel checks `canWrite`, `writeBlockReason`, `toggle.isPending`, and `config.isPending` before exposing writes. The differing contracts mean an operator in a blocked/paused operational mode can still mutate gas-price settings through `GasPriceControlPanel`, even though the same settings page must require the shared operational-write guard. The established `GasConfiguration` copy demonstrates the expected server-operation policy; leaving this panel permissive can produce write attempts against a scope/mode that should be read-only and inconsistent control behavior/disabled state between duplicate admin surfaces.
- **chunk-0366.md:5** — `web/src/features/admin/components/gdpr-export/GDPRLifecyclePanel.tsx:47-49,64-66,75-76`; `web/src/features/admin/components/gdpr-export/GDPRDownloadPanel.tsx:21-33`; `web/src/features/admin/pages/GDPRExportPage.tsx:54-67`: The lifecycle panel treats any `Date.parse()`-accepted `expires_at` as an instant, while the download panel and page use a strict calendar/ISO validator. An unzoned value, impossible calendar date, or normalized/ambiguous string can therefore make Lifecycle render an “Expires”/“Expired” event while the download surfaces mark the manifest expiry unknown; malformed expiry evidence is inconsistent across the same selected artifact.
