The strict validator is duplicated in the download panel and page, but Lifecycle uses the loose path. Keep one canonical instant parser for both validity and millisecond conversion so lifecycle, download eligibility, and page summaries cannot disagree.
- **chunk-0367.md:6** — `src/web/src/features/admin/components/ingest-xray/XRayHeader.tsx:79-90`; `src/web/src/types/admin-diagnostics.ts:267-274`; `src/web/src/features/admin/components/ingest-xray/XRayHeader.test.tsx:94-126`: The “Distinct fields” card treats an omitted `unique_fields` as zero (`data.unique_fields ?? 0`). The DTO contract explicitly says the server omits this field and absence means unknown, not zero, so a valid partial response incorrectly reports “0 distinct fields” instead of an em dash. Keep the metric unready/unknown when `unique_fields` is absent while loading/other totals remain independent; update the sparse-payload test expectation accordingly. The API contract and test fixture omit `unique_fields`, but the implementation and its assertions currently collapse that omission to a fabricated zero reading; similar null/unknown semantics are already preserved for age and malformed counts elsewhere in the chunk.
- **chunk-0368.md:5** — `src/internal/automation/action/command.go:96-105` (`DecodeCommandSpec`); `src/internal/automation/action/command.go:172-191` (`DecodeTypedCommandSpec`); `src/internal/automation/action/command.go:199-207` (`automationTeslaCommand`); `src/migrations/000255_automation_action_wait.up.sql:18-43`: Legacy/raw command configs validate `cfg.Command` directly against `tesla.IsKnownCommand()`, while the typed CTI path validates after mapping descriptive names through `automationTeslaCommand()`. As a result, `cabin_overheat_protection_on` and `hvac_on`, both added to the database command vocabulary by migration 255, are accepted by the typed executor but rejected by `DecodeCommandSpec`/the legacy `Execute` path. A raw wait/command graph cannot round-trip or execute these canonical descriptive command names even though the migration and typed executor explicitly support them. `DecodeCommandSpec` should use the same adapter mapping (`automationTeslaCommand`) and validation rule as `DecodeTypedCommandSpec`; otherwise live typed actions and legacy-compatible/snapshot execution paths remain behaviorally inconsistent for the two migration-added names.
- **chunk-0370.md:5** — `src/web/src/features/admin/components/security-access/helpers.ts:128-139` (`timeSince`): Invalid timestamp strings are not rejected before arithmetic; `Date.now() - new Date(iso).getTime()` becomes `NaN`, so `floor(NaN)` produces a fallback containing `NaN` (for example the localized `NaN`-day branch) instead of the universal missing-value placeholder. The helper is used by the security summary surfaces and should return a stable absence value for garbage timestamps. The canonical instant formatter `src/web/src/lib/dateFormat.ts:361-372` (`formatRelativeTime`) explicitly parses and returns `—` for invalid values, while this copy has no invalid-date guard; malformed retained history can therefore display `NaN` text in one security surface while the shared date layer degrades safely.
- **chunk-0370.md:6** — `src/web/src/features/admin/components/security-access/helpers.ts:259-269` (`deriveTimeline`): The Sentry transition check compares the raw enum strings instead of the normalized boolean states. Because `isSentryActive` treats values such as `On`, `Armed`, `SentryModeStateArmed`, and `SentryModeStateAware` as the same active state, moving between two active aliases currently creates a Sentry timeline event with a misleading transition. It should compare `currSentry !== prevSentry` (as the lock/door branches compare normalized state) and only emit when the semantic state actually changes; otherwise low-level enum alias changes can corrupt the security event timeline.
- **chunk-0371.md:5** — `src/migrations/000255_automation_action_wait.down.sql:3-31`; `src/ops/migrations/manifest.yaml:512-525`; `src/migrations/000255_automation_action_wait.up.sql:11-37`: The down migration restores the closed command-name constraint before it removes any new descriptive-command rows, so rollback can fail while those rows exist. PostgreSQL drops the expanded constraint, adds the old CHECK, and must validate it against every existing `automation_actions` row; any row using `cabin_overheat_protection_on` or `hvac_on` violates the old constraint and aborts the down migration. The manifest instead promises that an operator must export/delete those graphs before explicit rollback, but the down SQL itself performs no such cleanup and retries the old constraint regardless, so it is not a protected or repeatable rollback. The wait child table is also removed only after the constraint replacement, although its `DELETE FROM automation_steps` and cascade are valid once they are reached. `000255` up explicitly adds both descriptive command names to the expanded vocabulary, and handler/runtime tests exercise those names as real command actions. The stored data can therefore satisfy the up migration while violating the down migration.
- **chunk-0372.md:7** — `src/web/src/features/admin/components/structural-closure/live-logs/helpers.ts:24-33` (`formatTime`); `src/web/src/features/admin/components/structural-closure/live-logs/LiveLogsStream.tsx:20,25,194-195`: Live-log timestamps are formatted from epoch milliseconds with `new Date()` plus `getHours/getMinutes/getSeconds/getMilliseconds`, ignoring the application's locale/timezone preferences. Every stream row and exported log line can therefore display the host-local wall clock instead of the user's selected timezone; this bypasses the cataloged instant-date layer and can shift log ordering labels across DST or timezone changes. Route this through a locale/timezone-aware shared instant-time formatter that preserves millisecond precision, rather than treating the browser zone as the business timezone. DST and timezone preference changes can make identical received timestamps render in different wall-clock buckets depending on where the UI executes, while the rest of the app uses canonical formatting with explicit locale/tz options.
- **chunk-0373.md:6** — `src/web/src/features/admin/hooks/useSecretRotationPage.ts:46-72` (`oldest` and `soonestExpiry` reductions); `src/web/src/features/admin/components/structural-closure/secret-rotation/SecretRotationSummary.tsx:85-100` (oldest/soonest KPI rendering): The oldest-secret and soonest-expiry selectors only reject `null`/`undefined`; a runtime `NaN`, infinite value, or negative remaining-expiry value is treated as measured evidence. `oldest` can select `NaN` because the first null comparison allows it in, while `soonestExpiry` can select `NaN`/`Infinity` and then be rendered by a number formatter as fabricated finite data. The selectors and chart/sort consumers should first require finite operands, with age/nonnegative-domain checks where appropriate. Malformed or partial transport data can change the “oldest secret” and “soonest expiry” KPIs, their labels, and chart ordering from unknown to a false numeric reading instead of showing the established `—`/unknown state.
- **chunk-0374.md:5** — `src/web/src/features/admin/components/tesla-feature-flags/parseFeatureFlags.ts:64-69` (`parseFeatureEntries`): Configured and primitive feature values use `Boolean(...)` to derive `enabled`. JavaScript `Boolean("false")` is `true`, and a configured object such as `{ enabled: "false" }` is therefore reported enabled despite Tesla supplying an explicit JSON string that should not be truthy for a boolean state. Unknown values should use the existing record/boolean contract: only JSON booleans should drive bare/configured flags, while missing or non-boolean `enabled` values should be false rather than truthy coercion. This is isolated to malformed/wrongly typed payload hardening, but it can turn a disabled feature into a green/enabled KPI, distribution, composition, and table row.
- **chunk-0374.md:6** — `src/web/src/features/admin/components/tesla-orders/teslaOrderStats.ts:200-211` (`computeOrderStats`): Delivery-date facts are marked unknown for every non-cancelled order, including orders already `DELIVERED`. The function consequently can return `hasUnknownDelivery=true` for a delivered order whose normal `delivery_date` is missing, causing `DeliveryOutlookPanel` to show “Delivery date unknown” instead of “None scheduled,” even though no future delivery is expected. Delivered rows should not count as unknown outstanding deliveries (and, for stronger consistency, the future-date scan should exclude delivered rows too so contradictory future dates cannot become `nextDelivery`); cancelled and rejected rows are already excluded. Consumers including the delivery outlook, board, KPIs, and operational brief share this aggregate, so one classification edge case can propagate an inconsistent delivery fact across the page.
- **chunk-0378.md:5** — `src/web/src/features/admin/pages/LiveLogsPage.tsx:51`: The `secondaryActions` JSX prop is written as `secondaryActions=<LiveLogsToolbar controller={controller} />`, so the prop is not a valid JSX expression. This is a syntax/type error in the page and prevents the Live Logs route from compiling/rendering. It should be a braced element expression: `secondaryActions={<LiveLogsToolbar controller={controller} />}`. No second implementation is needed; the malformed prop affects the entire page build.
- **chunk-0384.md:7** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/AchievementBadge.tsx:48-50`: Nullish progress is guarded, but `NaN` is not; a partial API payload that supplies `progress: NaN` (or a non-number runtime value with numeric coercion behavior) reaches `Math.round`, producing `NaN`, then `Math.max/Math.min` preserve `NaN`. The component consequently renders `NaN%`, and `ProgressRing` receives a non-finite value instead of a valid unknown/zero fallback. The nearby comment claims the arithmetic is hardened against partial payloads, but the guard only covers `null`/`undefined`, not the non-finite result that the same comment is intended to prevent. Normalize with the shared `isFiniteNumber` helper, default invalid progress to `0`, and then clamp the finite value into `[0,1]`.
- **chunk-0385.md:5** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/analytics/OverviewVehicleComparison.tsx:121-129`: The radar's efficiency normalization treats `safe(v.efficiency)` as measured data. Missing, null, malformed, negative, or otherwise invalid efficiency becomes `0`, so the spoke `((maxEff - 0) / maxEff) * 100` reports a perfect performance value. Unknown efficiency must be withheld rather than converted into a strong radar score. The radar contradicts the leaderboard and can materially misrank a vehicle with no efficiency evidence.
- **chunk-0388.md:3** — src/web/src/features/analytics/components/drive-calendar/WeekdayPatternChart.tsx:51: `Math.round(convertDistanceFromSI(...) * 10) / 10` pre-rounds chart/export rows to one decimal. It differs from the monthly calendar chart, which keeps full converted values in `props.data` and delegates live precision and locale to `fmtNumber`, so weekday exports do not track user precision and exported precision cannot be restored after preferences change. Remove the pre-round and keep full values; use `fmtNumber` only in presentation/export formatters, preserving `null`/non-finite handling.
- **chunk-0388.md:5** — src/web/src/features/analytics/components/drive-calendar/CalendarSummaryCards.tsx:37: the streak label compares a workspace day key against `calendarDayKey(new Date())`, which uses the browser's local zone and wall-clock instant rather than the selected workspace/timezone semantics. At timezone boundaries, a period that ended yesterday/today can select the wrong streak label. Route calendar/current-day comparisons through the shared timezone/day-key utilities or pass the workspace timezone into the boundary calculation instead of constructing an inline local `Date`.
- **chunk-0389.md:6** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/lifetime-modernization/LifetimeKeyStats.tsx:27-29,39-51,57,63-79`: When `stats` is absent, `total_drives`, `total_distance_km`, and `total_energy_kwh` are coerced to `0` before every render decision. This means an initial empty or loading result can be presented as three genuine zero measurements (`0 drives`, `0 distance`, `0 energy`) instead of unknown/absent values, and a loading result with `loading=false` still enters the generic metric path. Preserve `null` for absent readings and pass it through `rawValue`; render the fallback cards only with loading/zero guards rather than fabricating source values. The page's own source contract distinguishes `stats == null` from a real zero lifetime aggregate (`lifetimeSectionState` and the page's source shell); this component collapses that distinction at the first render and can show false lifetime facts while loading or before a successful source response.
- **chunk-0396.md:6** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/true-cost/TrueCostPerDistanceChart.tsx:24-38,82-84`: The rows assign a palette-derived `fill` to each EV/gas category, but the rendered `<Bar dataKey="cost">` never uses per-row `<Cell>` elements or `fill` from the row data, so the category fill values are computed and exported into chart data but discarded by Recharts. The chart can render the two categories with the same default bar fill instead of the intended recorded-EV and modeled-gas palette colors. Add explicit `Cell` entries from the row `fill`, matching the chart's other palette contracts, or remove the unused fill field. The nearby chart-palette preservation test only inspects container data and series props, not rendered per-category fills, so the bug is not covered by the current suite.
- **chunk-0396.md:9** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/true-cost/TrueCostFixedLedger.tsx:174-211` and `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/true-cost/TrueCostFixedLedger.test.tsx:105-115`: The legacy ledger submits a fixed-cost entry without any `currency` field even though the API ledger type and the coexistence of the modernization implementation are currency-aware. The submit payload is therefore `{ vehicle_id, category, amount, incurred_on, note }`, while the test only verifies `vehicle_id` and `amount`; it does not verify that the request establishes a valid ledger currency. That creates a second write contract for the same endpoint and permits currency drift between the legacy and canonical components. Legacy code may remain discoverable from the barrel and test suite even though the active page has a newer implementation; both implementations should converge on one typed currency contract before either write path can be accepted.
- **chunk-0399.md:5** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/weekly-digest/helpers.ts:77-83`; `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/weekly-digest/useWeeklyDigest.ts:391-402`: `dayOfWeekIndex()` derives the weekday with `Date.getDay()` in the browser zone and has no timezone parameter. `getWeekRange()` already frames the digest in the selected display zone, but ISO telemetry near a zone boundary is then assigned to a chart bin by the host/browser weekday instead of the selected-zone weekday. Pass the effective `tz` into the helper and derive the weekday through the existing `zonedDateParts(dateStr, tz)` contract; the current helper silently mis-buckets data in negative/positive UTC-offset configurations. Same bug affects both the daily-distance and daily-energy series, because they call the identical unzoned helper; a second implementation is not needed, but tests should cover both series across an offset transition.
- **chunk-0399.md:6** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/weekly-digest/BatteryHealthSection.tsx:43-80`; `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/weekly-digest-modernization/BatteryPanel.tsx:46-95`: The original battery section converts missing charge evidence into displayed zero: `batteryStart ?? 0`/`batteryEnd ?? 0`, `battery gain = (end ?? 0) - (start ?? 0)`, and `chargeEnergy ?? 0` for the range estimate. Thus null SOC samples and partial charging totals become a real 0% pill, 0% gain, or estimated 0 m range. The modernization panel correctly guards these values in several branches, showing unknowns as `—`, and the hook also exposes `batteryGain`/`chargeEnergyAddedWh` as nullable; the original surface must preserve that null contract instead of fabricating measurements. The old and modernization implementations now disagree on the absence semantics, increasing the risk that a missed refresh or null API field looks like authoritative data in the legacy presentation.
- **chunk-0399.md:7** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/weekly-digest/ChargingSection.tsx:99-125`; `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/weekly-digest/DrivingSection.tsx:105-127`; `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/weekly-digest/WeekOverWeekSummary.tsx:48-87`; `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/weekly-digest/SummaryHeroCards.tsx:49-101`: The presentation bands repeatedly coerce nullable detail aggregates with `?? 0`: charging energy, charge rate, charging cost, session count, drive efficiency/duration/count, and summary metrics can be rendered as measured zero while their source is unavailable or partially missing. The modernization implementations instead retain nullable `detailMetrics` and use `available` to mark raw metrics missing; `useWeeklyDigest` already distinguishes unavailable feeds, empty successful feeds, and partial totals. The old presentation should follow the hook's source availability, not turn unknown values into zero. This affects independent loading/error/refresh paths and can produce false confidence (for example an unavailable charging feed showing 0 Wh, 0 W, or $0).
- **chunk-0399.md:8** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/weekly-digest/DrivingSection.tsx:38-43`: The top-drive efficiency calculation treats a missing `energyUsedWh` as zero (`(metrics.topDrive.energyUsedWh ?? 0) / distance`) and therefore renders `0 Wh/<unit>` for a measured-distance drive with no energy evidence. The equivalent modernization panel explicitly requires finite distance and finite measured energy and sets the value to `null`; preserve unknown rather than inventing efficiency. This differs in the same pair of implementations and can leak a false exact efficiency value into the top-drive card.
- **chunk-0399.md:9** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/weekly-digest/useFsdWeeklyDigestNotification.ts:14-22,36-57`; `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/weekly-digest/useFsdWeeklyDigestNotification.test.tsx:54-72`: The notification de-dup key uses `localDateKey(weekStart)` (browser-local calendar date) even though `weekStart` is computed in the selected display zone by `useWeeklyDigest`. On a host/browser with a different zone, the key can identify the same Auckland/user-zone week using a previous or next browser-local day. Pass the effective timezone into key construction and derive the key from zoned wall-calendar parts, or otherwise pin the key to `toISOString()`; a browser-relative date is unstable and the test only pins a host-local date. Retrying the same current-week notification under a different browser zone may create a different local-storage key and repeat delivery, while two adjacent zones may collide.
- **chunk-0399.md:10** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/weekly-digest/AlertsSection.tsx:79-103`; `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/weekly-digest-modernization/AlertsPanel.tsx:84-96`: Both alert-list surfaces render the raw severity key (`warning`, `critical`, etc.) rather than the localized severity names already constructed in `alertPieData` and the `severityLabels` map in `useWeeklyDigest`. Raw keys are visible to users and do not follow the localized German/Japanese/RTL presentation; unknown severities are also displayed without the unknown-severity fallback contract used by the pie legend. Use the severity labels/presentation contract from the hook or a shared severity registry, not raw API slugs. The chart legend and list can show different names for the same severity in localized locales.
- **chunk-0399.md:11** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/components/weekly-digest/BatteryPill.tsx:36-60`: `BatteryPill` uses inline `style` for both dynamic status color and dynamic meter width, but also uses `aria-hidden=true` on the entire text wrapper containing the visible label and percentage. This removes a useful battery reading from the accessibility tree even though a separate `role="meter"` exposes only `aria-valuenow`, not the textual label/value. In the same component, the visible percentage is hidden while the meter label/value carries a duplicate signal. It produces inconsistent screen-reader output and also conflicts with the repo's shared styling/icon/a11y primitive guidance; use shared meter/text semantics and token-based classes instead of presentation styles where possible.
- **chunk-0400.md:3** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/lib/carbonIntelligence.ts:505-522`: `analyzeCarbonSummary` does not include `green_score` in its zero-summary `empty` predicate, so a payload with all other required totals, monthly rows, and sessions at zero but a nonzero valid green score is incorrectly classified as `available` rather than `empty`; this is observable only through the returned availability discriminator and contradicts the function's empty/available branch contract.
- **chunk-0401.md:7** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/lib/mileageBudget.ts:65-72,84,121-130`: Budget configuration validation accepts impossible calendar dates such as `2026-02-30`: `new Date('2026-02-30T00:00:00')` normalizes instead of remaining invalid, and the function only checks finite `Date` rather than an exact `YYYY-MM-DD` calendar key. It also accepts non-integer `termMonths` (`1.5`) even though the contract is months; the downstream `addMonths` and monthly loop then operate on fractional month indices rather than the documented whole-month term. A persisted/edited lease configuration can therefore compute a term end from a normalized date and a fractional month count, creating month keys and elapsed allowances inconsistent with its displayed contract. Date validation should round-trip an exact civil date (as `trueCost.ts`'s UTC day validator does), and `termMonths` should require a positive integer.
- **chunk-0401.md:8** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/lib/mileageBudget.ts:84-91`: The term is converted through browser-local `new Date(yyyy-mm-ddT00:00:00)` and then uses `Math.round((termEndMs - termStartMs) / DAY_MS)` for elapsed days. This wall-clock calculation is not DST-canonical: local midnight subtraction across spring/fall transitions can yield 27, 29, or fractional-day totals, unlike the normalized elapsed-day interval used later in the model. The model attempts to normalize after this, but the two calculations can disagree about the declared month-ending date in DST zones. Use a civil-calendar month end/add-month contract and clock-free interval normalization consistently; wall-clock millisecond differences must not define lease-calendar day counts.
- **chunk-0401.md:9** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/lib/trueCost.ts:278-288,340-344`: The `monthly_energy_total` accounting identity pairs `Wh` values but applies `CURRENCY_TOLERANCE` (`0.02`) as its tolerance, whereas the other currency and per-km identities have explicit unit-appropriate tolerances. This mixes currency epsilon with an energy unit and also scales the wrong tolerance by monthly row count. Independent rounding or small discrepancy in the backend aggregate can be classified `outside_tolerance` or be tolerated on an incorrect scale; use an energy-specific tolerance constant (or explicit unrounded exact accounting tolerance for `Wh`) and keep currency and unit tolerances separate.
- **chunk-0402.md:9** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/pages/StatisticsPage.tsx:39,77`; `src/web/src/features/analytics/pages/PeriodComparePage.tsx:66,185-186`; `src/web/src/features/analytics/pages/RegimeShiftsPage.tsx:46`; `src/web/src/features/analytics/pages/FleetComparePage.tsx:333`: Efficiency conversion is inconsistent within the analytics page chunk: the first three pages use ad-hoc Wh/mi formulas derived in inconsistent ways, while `FleetComparePage` uses the canonical `formatEnergyPerDistance`; the hardcoded formulas may lose the canonical precision/null semantics and do not share the same unit-policy path. Use `convertEfficiencyFromSI` or `formatEnergyPerDistance` consistently according to whether the caller needs a numeric value or rendered text. Values such as `avg_efficiency`, period comparison operands, and regime changepoints can disagree by rounding or conversion policy even for the same SI source reading.
- **chunk-0402.md:10** — `src/web/src/features/analytics/pages/RegimeShiftsPage.tsx:91`; `src/web/src/features/analytics/pages/FleetComparePage.tsx:225-236`; `src/web/src/features/analytics/pages/TimelinePage.tsx:528`: Three analytics pages retain ad-hoc presentation logic that bypasses shared component/style conventions: Regime Shifts builds signed percentage text with raw `Math.round`, Fleet Compare uses dynamic inline `style` for an allowed progress width, and Timeline uses dynamic inline `style` for legend swatches. The catalog permits dynamic computed chart values/styles, but percentage formatting should use `useNumberFormatting`; ensure only the genuinely dynamic style locations remain and no static-color or ad-hoc number formatting is expanded in new code. Mixed local rounding and presentation can produce a non-localized minus/decimal sign or differ from shared number/color primitives, especially across locale and forced-color modes.
- **chunk-0404.md:5** — `src/web/src/features/automations/components/ComfortPanel.tsx:40-47`: The comfort configuration effect hydrates local form state only while `hydratedVehicleId !== vehicleId`. After one hydration, later server/config refreshes for the same vehicle do not refresh `enabled`, `targetTemp`, `leadMinutes`, or `icsUrl`, so stale local state can remain displayed after refreshed configuration changes and a save can submit outdated values. Reconcile local state when the stored configuration revision/data changes, rather than treating vehicle identity as the only hydration boundary. The payload is vehicle-gated, but repeated refreshes or mutation refetches for the same vehicle can repeatedly expose stale controlled form values.
- **chunk-0404.md:6** — `src/web/src/features/automations/components/automation-helper-source-closure/ActionCommandFields.tsx:11-17, 93-139`: The JSON draft (`paramsText`) and native-validation error state are initialized from props only and are not synchronized when a controlled parent changes `action.command_params` while the command remains the same. Parent reset/restoration can leave the visible draft and custom validity inconsistent with the actual payload, and the stale error can disable the advanced-parameters collapse action. Synchronize external payload changes while retaining in-progress local draft text separately. This affects the same stable row across parent-driven reset or undo without requiring a remount.
- **chunk-0405.md:3** — `src/web/src/features/automations/components/automation-helper-source-closure/ActionCommandFields.tsx:21-27`; `src/web/src/features/automations/components/automation-helper-source-closure/ActionCommandFields.tsx:99-123`: The JSON parameter draft and `showAdvanced` state are initialized only at mount and are not resynchronized when controlled `action.command_params` or `action.command_name` changes from an external source. Applying an AI draft, restoring a preset/edit, or replacing a same-kind row can leave old JSON visible while the action payload has new parameters, so the visible draft diverges from the saved payload and local edits can overwrite external state. External prop replacement is a normal controlled-component path; reconcile name/params with an effect while preserving valid local JSON-in-progress text.
- **chunk-0405.md:4** — `src/web/src/features/automations/components/automation-helper-source-closure/AutomationCardStatistics.tsx:59-63`; `src/web/src/features/automations/pages/AutomationCard.test.tsx:430`: `next_fire_time` is formatted with `formatDateTime` without validating whether the runtime value is a parseable timestamp. The card otherwise validates loosely typed counters and timestamps, but this optional field is rendered directly from the `AutomationListItem` payload; an invalid or structurally unexpected string can reach `Intl` formatting and produce the fallback `"Invalid Date"` behavior rather than the card's otherwise consistent unknown-value posture. Use `automationTimestamp(a.next_fire_time)` before formatting so malformed API values consistently render unknown/omitted instead of leaking an invalid timestamp.
- **chunk-0405.md:5** — `src/web/src/features/automations/pages/AutomationHistoryPage.tsx:91-94`; `src/web/src/features/automations/pages/AutomationHistoryPage.tsx:69-73`: Pagination recovery is implemented after data has already been queried. After direct navigation to an out-of-range page or a filter change that leaves the same URL page, `useAutomationHistoryPage` receives the stale page before the effect clamps it to `totalPages`; this can issue an unnecessary mismatched server request and briefly render the wrong page, then another request updates it. Normalize `page` against the known/returned total before querying, or include page clamping in the filter/URL update and derive the effective request page synchronously.
- **chunk-0407.md:5** — `src/web/src/features/battery/components/TOUSettingsModal.tsx:149-171`: Custom JSON accepts any envelope containing a `tou_settings` key without validating that its value is an object; malformed payloads such as `{"tou_settings":"economics"}` and `{"tou_settings":[]}` pass `getPayload()` and are sent to the live Tesla mutation instead of being rejected locally. Validate the inner value as a non-null, non-array object before forwarding it. The preset and custom-envelope paths can expose different validation contracts, with invalid shapes reaching the mutation boundary rather than the modal boundary.
- **chunk-0407.md:6** — `src/web/src/features/battery/components/VampireSplitPanel.tsx:64-69`: A successful split response with zero parked-window rows renders no empty-state message or `DataTable`; it only shows honesty text and percent metrics. Use the no-recorded-window empty contract from `vampire-drain-modernization/VampireSplitPanel.tsx` and clearly replace window-dependent evidence when the arrays are empty. The legacy copy makes authoritative empty data look like a blank or unexplained populated evidence section.
- **chunk-0408.md:3** — `src/web/src/features/battery/components/battery-care/{ArrivalSocEvidence.tsx,EndSocDistribution.tsx,ChargingEnergyMix.tsx},:93-112`: Missing shares are rendered as zero because `bucket.share ?? 0` is used as the bar value; an unavailable measurement is not the same as a measured 0%. The modernization uses unknown-bar handling in `battery-care-modernization/{SocEvidence.tsx,CareEnergy.tsx}` via `value == null`; reuse that contract so unknown shares remain unknown.
- **chunk-0408.md:4** — `src/web/src/features/battery/components/battery-care/CareScoreBreakdown.tsx:138-146`: `component.penaltyPoints ?? 0` converts an uncalibrated deduction into an apparent 0-point deduction and feeds a bar labeled calibrated. Use the modernized `CareRisk.tsx:61-69`/`EvidenceBar` unknown-value branch so unavailable deductions render `—`, not a zero.
- **chunk-0411.md:9** — `src/web/src/features/battery/components/cycle-stress/labels.ts:18-24`: `cycleStressPercent` and `cycleStressShare` do not give finite values special handling before appending `%`: `cycleStressPercent(NaN)` delegates to `cycleStressNumber(NaN)`, gets `"—"`, then returns `"—%"`; `cycleStressShare(Infinity, ...)` similarly produces a malformed `"—%"` after the inner unknown guard. Non-finite analyzer or payload values are presented as `"—%"` instead of the canonical unknown marker `"—"` and can vary visibly across cyclic components. Return `—` before `value * 100`/the percent suffix when the input is null or non-finite, and add regressions for `NaN` and `Infinity`.
- **chunk-0412.md:3** — `src/web/src/features/battery/components/energy-products-modernization/SummaryBand.tsx:14-17`: The total-capacity calculation treats an empty successful site list as complete (`sites.every(...)` is true) and reports `0` instead of unknown. It should require `sites.length > 0` before summing capacity; otherwise an authoritative empty products snapshot is mislabeled as zero capacity while `hasData` is true. Empty facts are collapsed into zero, violating the repository's unknown-honest value contract and producing an incorrect battery-capacity metric.
- **chunk-0423.md:7** — `src/web/src/features/charging/components/AutopilotPanel.tsx:332-333`; `src/web/src/features/charging/components/smart-charge-modernization/AutopilotCard.tsx:256-257`; `src/web/src/features/charging/pages/SmartChargePage.tsx:218`: These implementations derive charge-window timeline hours with browser-local `new Date(...).getHours()` and feature-local midnight normalization (`
- **chunk-0427.md:3** — `src/web/src/features/charging/components/charging-curve/helpers.ts:7-11`; `src/web/src/features/charging/components/charging-list/helpers.ts:165-171`: AC/DC inference repeats the same session classification shape but uses different fallback DC power thresholds: `helpers.isDcSession()` treats unknown charger types with peak power above 20,000 W as DC, while `computeAcDcBreakdown()` uses 22,000 W. A charger with 20–22 kW peak is therefore classified differently by the curve and list-breakdown views despite no ambiguous domain contract justifying the split. Share one AC/DC classifier (or centralize an explicit threshold constant and document why it differs) so both views agree. The two implementations are direct copies with behavior differences. The stronger copy for each threshold is not clear, but edge sessions around 20–22 kW can show conflicting AC/DC labels, counts, energy splits, and chart assumptions.
- **chunk-0430.md:4** — `src/web/src/features/charging/components/cost-analysis/ChargerTypeBreakdown.tsx:111-133`: `entry.cost ?? 0` preserves `NaN`; the share calculation therefore can produce `NaN`, and `Math.min(100, Math.max(0, NaN))` also remains `NaN`, yielding an invalid `width: NaN%` style. Malformed upstream numeric data can reach the visual bar despite the component's claim to clamp values and avoid NaN. Neutralize every numeric with `finite`-style coercion before percentage and bar-width calculation.
- **chunk-0430.md:5** — `src/web/src/features/charging/components/cost-analysis/MonthlyCostTable.tsx:86-97`: The mobile presentation manually formats currency with `${currencySymbol}${fmtNumber(row.avgCostPerKwh)}` and analogous strings. It bypasses the shared `Currency`/`formatCurrency` contract used by desktop cells and does not guarantee locale placement, negative-value formatting, or the configured average-rate precision.
- **chunk-0430.md:6** — `src/web/src/features/charging/components/cost-analysis/ForecastDetails.tsx:137-140`: The monthly-savings card concatenates `currencySymbol`, an unformatted `AnimatedNumber`, and no shared currency renderer. This duplicates currency rendering instead of using the canonical precision/currency component and can diverge for locale and negative-value formatting.
- **chunk-0430.md:7** — `src/web/src/features/charging/components/cost-analysis/useCostAnalysisData.ts:42`; `src/web/src/features/charging/components/cost-analysis/useCostAnalysisData.ts:308`; `src/web/src/features/charging/components/cost-analysis/BillVarianceCard.tsx:79`: Raw watt-hours are divided by `1000` inline in the derived rate and bill-variance paths instead of consistently using the canonical energy conversion/formatting boundary. Fixed `/1000` conversion duplicates the `convertEnergyFromSI` contract and can drift when energy preferences or precision rules change. Use the canonical helper and display formatter at the boundary.
- **chunk-0436.md:5** — `src/web/src/features/charging/pages/TeslaChargingHistoryPage.tsx:212-220`; `src/web/src/features/charging/pages/TeslaChargingSessionsPage.tsx:198-206`; `src/web/src/api/hooks/useCharging.ts:267-275`; `src/web/src/api/hooks/useCharging.ts:372-385`: Both dated pages explicitly acknowledge that their read hooks lack server bounds, load lifetime rows, and then filter them in the browser. Lifetime summaries and aggregate KPIs still remain lifetime-scoped, while tables/charts become range-scoped, creating inconsistent displayed scopes. It also prevents server-side range filtering and pagination from being correct. The second copy and risk are the history and sessions client-side filters plus their hooks, which pass no `start`/`end` query bounds; extend the hooks/endpoints to share `start_instant` and `end_instant_exclusive`, and remove the client-side truth source.
- **chunk-0441.md:4** — `src/web/src/features/dashboard/hooks/validateImport.ts:112-115,142-178`: Widget IDs are regenerated when an imported widget has a duplicate or missing string ID, but layout entries are not remapped from the original ID to the regenerated widget ID. For a duplicate `id: "dup"` followed by a second widget, the second widget receives `"dup-dup-..."`, while every layout entry with `i: "dup"` is still checked against only the regenerated IDs and is therefore dropped; import can silently lose the second widget's layout even though the widget itself is retained. A missing-ID widget likewise has no stable mapping from layout entries to the generated ID. Build a per-import ID remap from original ID to final ID before sanitizing layouts, or assign IDs before widget construction and rewrite/layout-key remapping together. The mismatch is in the import boundary: valid widget data survives while its position/size does not, causing layout restoration to differ for duplicate or malformed imports.
- **chunk-0445.md:3** — `src/web/src/features/dashboard/widgets/DriveEfficiencyChartWidget.tsx:99-103`: the rolling 30-day filter rejects drives older than 30 days but does not reject future-dated drives, so clock skew, stale cache corruption, or a malformed `start_ts` can include future readings in the efficiency chart and headline average. Add an upper bound to the same cutoff check (`start <= now`) so the filter truly covers the last 30 days.
- **chunk-0458.md:3** — `src/web/src/features/dashboard/widgets/shared/WidgetStatusGrid.tsx:26-44,85-87`; `src/web/src/features/dashboard/widgets/shared/WidgetTipCards.tsx:25-27,93-97`: Dynamic status/impact string values are looked up directly in plain objects and only use truthiness/nullish fallbacks. Runtime values such as inherited object keys (`constructor`, `toString`, or `__proto__`) resolve to an Object prototype member instead of the intended unknown/neutral entry; `statusCopy` can then pass inherited function/undefined children into i18n, and tip impact fallback lookup can likewise reach a function as a React child. Use own-property resolution or a type-safe resolver with an explicit unknown/neutral fallback for every dynamic string key. Prototype keys can bypass the caller-visible fail-soft behavior even though ordinary out-of-union strings currently work, producing missing labels or render errors for malformed/cast API data.
- **chunk-0462.md:5** — `src/web/src/features/dashcam/pages/DashcamIntelligencePage.tsx:54`: The catalog source state only maps fatal load errors, refresh errors, and `isLoading` to the source UI. An initially paused/offline query (`fetchStatus === 'paused'`, no retained data, `isLoading === false`) produces neither fatal error nor loading state, so the code renders `ClipCatalogList` with `totalCount === 0` and falsely presents `No clips imported yet`. Treat `catalogState.isRefreshBlocked`/`clipsQuery.fetchStatus === 'paused'` as an unresolved source state (loading or retained/offline guidance), not an authoritative successful empty catalog. No second implementation is needed; this directly confuses storage/import behavior with a network/catalog result on the first offline read.
- **chunk-0464.md:4** — `src/web/src/features/developer-reference/mobile-grid/useReferenceController.ts:48-58`; `src/web/src/features/developer-reference/mobile-grid/fixtureHelpers.ts:19-22`: The “matching loaded” and “selected loaded” export cases are not restricted to the loaded batch. `referenceExportKeys` ignores `loaded` for `matchingLoaded`, so a filtered export may count every matching source row even before load-more; `selectedLoaded` searches the full source rather than loaded rows and includes selected keys that have not yet been loaded. Thus the UI can report a request scope that is broader than its loaded-data contract. Pass the visible/loaded rows explicitly and intersect matching or selected scopes with that set. Export feedback, row counts, and selection semantics diverge from the visible pagination model whenever `batch < matching.length` or a selected key belongs to a later batch.
- **chunk-0467.md:5** — `src/web/src/features/diagnostics/hooks/useRootCauseWorkspace.ts:90-93`; `src/web/src/features/diagnostics/lib/serviceEvidencePack.ts:351-357`: The workspace converts a supplied instant range to `windowHours` using raw `Date.parse(...)` arithmetic. It does not verify that both bounds parse or that end is not before start, and `NaN` can enter evidence-query and ranking code; the pack builder separately turns non-finite hours into zero rather than rejecting or disabling the range. Invalid or negative ranges are not legitimate evidence-window contracts. They can alter query scope and ranking, produce inconsistent export window metadata, and change from NaN during analysis to zero only at export. Validate and normalize at the range boundary before querying or building the pack.

## Dropped false positives and exclusions

| Dropped count/group | Why dropped |
|---|---|
| 622 `not-a-duplicate` rows | Same appearance but different domain contract, precision, zone, vendor format, null semantics, generated output, test fixture, or intentional one-off behavior. |
| New-candidate headings with copy count 1 | Under the review threshold, they do not establish a real cross-file duplicate for non-trivial logic and are often a one-off local implementation. |
| Generic `contains`, `ParseInt`, `Math.round`, `new Date`, `make/copy`, percentage, and percentage-to-decimal copies | These are language-library primitives or have differing null/precision/time semantics; extraction would create a speculative abstraction. |
| Test-only dictionaries/fixtures with no shared production contract | Feature IDs, mock bodies, and assertion data are scenario inputs. A test helper should only be extracted where setup behavior is genuinely repeated and parameterizable. |
| Date/currency/number/lookalike parsers with different invalid/fallback behavior | Missing, zero, infinity, locale, rounding, timezone, and fallback edge behavior can differ; they are excluded unless source spot-check proves the contract matches. |
| Icon and UI finding clusters as “new modules” | These are adoption of cataloged primitives/registries, not new shared logic, and are retained only as migration work items. |
| Generated, protobuf, vendor, migration-baseline, and node_modules code | Excluded by scope even if text looks duplicated. |

## Near-duplicate merge decisions

| Family | Decision | Source confirmation |
|---|---|---|
| Place/geo charger identity | Merged at primitive level; fallback left caller-owned | Place-first, finite-coordinates, 0.01-degree cells match in three charging files. |
| Regularized incomplete beta | Merged | Same beta-CDF continued fraction; argument order differs only. |
| TOU tariffs and charge schedule | Merged into two layers: tariff registry and schedule engine | Planner/autopilot formulas match; wire types, ready/departure labels, alternatives, and rounding stay local. |
| Validation contracts | Merged only where trim/rune/control/limit semantics match | Location/trip/prompt implementations were spot-checked or grouped from exact matching contract text. |
| AI/settings/i18n/SSE/PWA test harnesses | Merged as test support only | Shared setup repeats complete fixtures and browser seams; feature IDs/assertions remain local. |
| ML statistics | Merged | Mean/stddev/type-7 percentile copies match; domain fallback stays package-local. |
| SLO YAML parser | Merged | Generator/runtime implement the same strict subset and have already diverged in diagnostics/validation details. |
| Chart/UI/icon/date/units groups | Kept as adoption work; not one speculative generic module | Differences include bucket source, precision, locale, domain labels, icon semantics, and a11y behavior. |

## Items needing the user decision

| Decision | Why | Default used |
|---|---|---|
| TOU backend source of truth vs frontend mirror | Prevents three rate registries drifting. | Backend Go registry owns tariffs; frontend consumes API metadata if available, otherwise parity tests pin matching values. |
| Scope of global Lucide removal | Hundreds of direct imports may span broad UI work. | Register missing concepts first, then migrate high-value feature groups; preserve semantic owner decisions. |
| Trusted-proxy policy for HTTP identity | Security-sensitive IP/header trust semantics need an owner. | Preserve existing behavior until parity tests prove the same policy, then centralize. |
| Bug backlog execution policy | The report contains hundreds of bug candidates and category noise. | Keep ranked in this plan; implementation should resolve P0/P1 items first rather than silently broad-fixing all bullets. |
